## v62 / 0.60.0, absolute pointer: a tap lands the cursor where the finger is (Sep 2026)
The "Absolute pointer" item above, shipped. The kernel now speaks the VMware absolute-pointer backdoor (`drivers/vmmouse.c`, new; hooks in `drivers/mouse.c`; four small call-site changes in `kernel/kernel.c`), and `landing/v86/embed.js` sends one `mouse-absolute` packet per tap instead of pacing a chain of PS/2 deltas across frames. Wire protocol taken from three implementations that agree, not guessed: Linux `drivers/input/mouse/vmmouse.c` (the register discipline: EAX=magic 0x564D5868, ECX=command, EBX=argument, `inl` on port 0x5658, answers come back by the hypervisor rewriting EAX/EBX/ECX/EDX), QEMU hw/input/vmmouse.c + vmport.c, and v86's src/vmware.js as vendored in `libv86.js` (decoded from its minified constants: port 22104, commands 10/39/40/41, enable 0x45414552, request-absolute 0x53424152, ID 0x3442554A, relative-packet flag 0x10000, data as EAX=status EBX=x ECX=y EDX=z). Probe at boot is the Linux one (GETVERSION must echo the magic in EBX and not answer 0xFFFFFFFF; an unclaimed port on bare hardware does neither), then enable, read the one ID word, request absolute. Positions arrive 0..65535 across the screen and map to pixels as `x*w>>16`; the sender puts the pixel centre on the wire so the two roundings land on exactly that pixel.

Premise correction, proven not assumed: the item's text said QEMU's cocoa display "does not speak this protocol". It does. QEMU's default pc machine creates a `vmmouse` alongside its vmport, and QEMU's own `query-mice` lists it as `current: true, absolute: true` once this kernel asks, so the native app gets the absolute pointer too, not only the browser demo. The `-machine vmport=off` fallback is what stands in for bare hardware.

Three real "looks right, subtly wrong" traps, each found by a real artifact, none by reasoning alone: (1) QEMU announces every backdoor packet by faking a PS/2 event through the 8042 (`ps2_mouse_fake_event`, a genuine dx=+1 packet whose only purpose is to raise IRQ12), so a driver that kept honouring PS/2 deltas once absolute mode was on would drift one pixel right per host event; `mouse_handle_byte` now keeps parsing for phase and discards contents when the backdoor is live. Observed for real, the other way round: HMP `mouse_move` only emits relative events, QEMU routed its dx=16384 to the PS/2 handler, and the kernel correctly ignored it. (2) A tap's press and release (80ms apart) both sat in the backdoor queue by the time the GUI loop next polled (v86 raises no IRQ at all for backdoor packets, the kernel polls from its own loop off the 100Hz PIT), one drain swallowed both, every sampled "is the button down now" check saw 0 -> 0, and the click never happened. Caught by a temporary per-packet serial trace under `mobiletest.mjs`, since reverted: the tour's own click landed (gui_run happened to poll between the two), the test's focus tap did not. Fix in the driver: stop draining at any button transition so the next poll sees the release as its own step, and count every left-button 0->1 in the packet stream so `mouse_click_edge` fires exactly once even when both packets came in one drain. (3) A browser follows a tap with synthesized mousedown/mouseup, v86's own `window` listeners turned those into a second click at the same instant, so every tap arrived as two press/release pairs (visible in the same trace). On the old path the extra pair was accidentally always lost to exactly the coalescing in (2); with (2) fixed it would open an app and immediately close it. `touchend` now calls `preventDefault()` for a handled tap (listener made non-passive; touchend has no scroll to block, the touchstart/touchmove passivity that fixed the scroll-trap bug is untouched).

Verified for real, not assumed: `make kernel.elf` clean (only the pre-existing `gui_draw_hello_script` warning), `./check.sh` PASS, `./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean. New `tools/checks/vmmouse-check.sh`, the first headless proof of a mouse path against native QEMU this repo has had (the PS/2 path's honest note that the monitor's mouse commands never reached the kernel headlessly still stands for PS/2): boots `-display none` with QMP on stdio, asserts `query-mice` shows the vmmouse current and absolute, injects `input-send-event` abs (16384, 24576) and asserts the kernel's serial line `vmmouse: first absolute packet x=32768 y=49152` (QEMU's own `<<1`); phase two reboots with `-machine vmport=off` and asserts `vmmouse_init: no backdoor, PS/2 relative pointer only` plus the gui_run boot marker. Both PASS. `mobiletest.mjs` (real Playwright iPhone 13 emulation, against `landing/` served locally with the fresh `kernel.elf`): `__jt.absolute` true, the kernel's own serial log (now exposed as `__jt.serial`, captured from the first boot byte) shows the first absolute packet at x=22562 y=58193, which is exactly what the tour's slot-1 target (330.5/960, 479.5/540 of 65535) should encode; dock tap -> Files view in 428ms from touch, stable 1.5s later; both screenshots inspected, not just the fingerprint: before is the settled desktop with the cursor at the centre tap, after is Files open with the cursor on the Files tile and its hover label showing. `tourtest.mjs` PASS. Deployed-site baseline run of the same script before this change: FAIL, the tap never changed the screen at all in 9s (see the drift below for why).

Test-harness fixes this pass forced, all real: `mobiletest.mjs` sampled its "before" fingerprint while the idle tour still had Files open, so "the app closed" was indistinguishable from "an app opened" and the first green run passed for the wrong reason (caught by looking at the screenshots); it now samples after the focus tap, requires the app view to still be there 1.5s later, reports tap-to-app-view time, asserts the guest logged a real absolute packet, and had stale dock constants (7 slots of 60px) from before v52/v59. `embed.js`'s idle tour had the same stale geometry (8 slots at 10%) and, since v59's reorder, was typing `help` into Mail and "what can you do?" into Reminders; each step now names its real slot with kernel.c's own tile arithmetic. Drift found and worth recording: `landing/v86/kernel.elf` had last been committed at v56.1, so the deployed demo ran a kernel three versions behind everything above it until this pass re-synced it.

Honest gaps: the native `-display cocoa` window was not opened (standing policy: headless only from a worktree agent), so QEMU's cocoa backend in absolute mode was not eyeballed; what was proven is the device-level path it feeds (`qemu_input_queue_abs` -> vmmouse -> guest), identical to the QMP injection. On QEMU the first packet the kernel sees is a 0,0 press/release if the host clicks before ever moving (observed in the HMP run), harmless, the next move corrects it. The `mouse_send_click`-style PS/2 click echo v86 still sends is drained and ignored, as designed.

Rebased onto concurrent v60/0.59.0 (wind sway) and v61/0.59.1 (dock seam/aliasing) before push, re-verified headless after the rebase; VERSION bumped 0.59.1 -> 0.60.0 (MINOR, new capability: absolute pointer) and tagged `jt-v62`.

## v63 / 0.60.1, dock hover root-caused: two real bugs, neither the one the recording suggested (Sep 2026)
The "[Fable] Root-cause the dock hover stuck-magnify bug" item above, done, with the exact instrument that entry planned (a temporary serial trace of `hover_slot`, every slot's `dock_hover_extra` and `dock_presented_extra`, and which repaint tier ran, on every iteration that moved the mouse or stepped the animation) and, for the first time, synthetic input that actually reached the GUI loop headlessly: v62's vmmouse path driven by QMP `input-send-event` absolute events (the same call `tools/checks/vmmouse-check.sh` makes), no `cliclick`, no cocoa window. The recording's sequence was replayed exactly: park on Files (slot 1) until fully lifted, then hop across four more icons 120ms apart, plus a smooth 4px-per-10ms sweep variant, with the real framebuffer `pmemsave`d mid-animation and again after settling, converted BGRA->PNG and actually looked at.

Direct answer to the open question: the kernel's own animation state is NOT stuck. In every run `dock_hover_extra[1]` stepped 9 -> 6 -> 3 -> 0 on schedule the moment the cursor left (`T689 e=9,0 -> T694 e=6,3 -> T701 e=3,6 -> T707 e=0,3,3`, ~60ms per step, fully settled inside ~200ms, never a full second), and `dock_presented_extra` tracked it one iteration behind, as designed. That rules out the "not decrementing" half of the question outright. What the pixels showed instead were two real bugs, neither visible from static reading, both now fixed:

**(1) The offscreen dock-band compose has been dead at 1920x1080 since it shipped.** The mid-animation dump showed the tray with no icons at all, just one shadow blob at the far left. The only code that can paint that picture is `gui_redraw_dock_band`'s fallback branch (`gui_draw_wallpaper_rows` then `gui_draw_dock` straight onto the visible framebuffer: wallpaper first, then tray, then ten icons one at a time), which runs only when the `dock_band_cache`/`dock_band_frame` kmallocs fail. A one-line trace confirmed it, `band alloc FAILED` on the very first hover, every boot. Root cause is in `kernel/paging.c`, not the dock code: `MAX_EXTRA_TABLES` was 4 (16MB of extra mappings), sized when the only extra region was one framebuffer, and v34 then made kheap grow through the same pool. At 1920x1080 the LFB alone (8.3MB at `0xfd000000`) spans two 4MB PDEs and takes two of the four tables, capping the heap at 4MB base + 8MB. The GUI's real working set at that resolution (`wind_base` 5.6MB, the icon caches ~1.4MB, two 1.9MB band buffers) does not fit, so v43's "compose offscreen, present only changed slots" never actually ran on the native window; every hover step has been the exact full-band on-screen repaint v40/v43 were written to remove, ~60ms each, drawn in front of the viewer with no double buffer. That is the "flashes/re-renders on hover" report, mechanically. Fix: `MAX_EXTRA_TABLES` 4 -> 16 (64KB of BSS, 64MB of mapping headroom; physical memory via pmm stays the real ceiling, QEMU's default 128MB has plenty, v86's 32MB just gets the same graceful kmalloc failure it always did). After: `band alloc OK`, the same mid-animation dump shows all ten tiles intact, and animation steps land every 5 ticks instead of 6-7.

**(2) The hit test has been half a tile off since v15.** The settled dump showed the cursor over Reminders with Notes lifted. `gui_slot_at` computed `rel = mx - slot_x(0) - DOCK_ICON / 2`, which puts every slot boundary at the CENTRE of a drawn tile: the left half of each icon (and the gap before it) hit-tested as the previous slot. The magnified icon sat one tile to the left of the cursor half the time, and "the icon I just left is still lifted while I'm over the next one" is literally what that looks like. Unchanged since the dock's first commit (v15, DOCK_ICON 56 and gap 16 then, same formula). Fix: `+ DOCK_GAP / 2` instead, so each tile owns itself plus half a gap either side; drag-to-reorder's drop target goes through the same function and now lands on the tile under the cursor too.

Honest scope: the recording's "roughly a full second" is not reproduced headless. Bug (2) explains a one-tile lag exactly and bug (1) explains the visible flashing and a repaint-bound loop; the ~60ms per step is what this session measured under TCG with no display, and whether QEMU's cocoa backend stretches that further on a real Retina window is unknown from a worktree agent that may not open one. Cursor-only tiers interleaved correctly during the smooth sweep (19 cursor-only, 15 dock-only iterations), no wrong-tier decision seen; the earlier entry's static-reading conclusions about the tier conditions were right.

Verified for real, not assumed: `make kernel.elf` clean (only the pre-existing `gui_draw_hello_script` warning), `./check.sh` PASS, `./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean, `./tools/checks/vmmouse-check.sh` both phases PASS (paging changed, so the `vmport=off` fallback was re-proven). New `tools/checks/dockhover-check.py`, the headless replay above turned into a repeatable check with PASS/FAIL on real pixels: PASS on this kernel, FAIL on the pre-fix kernel (rebuilt from the reverted diff in the same session) on its deterministic assertion (settled frame lifts slot 4, not the slot 5 under the cursor); the empty-tray assertion is timing-based and says so in the script. All trace prints removed, `git diff` is the two fixes plus the check script. `landing/v86/kernel.elf` re-synced (the hit-test fix changes what the browser demo's dock does). PATCH bump 0.60.0 -> 0.60.1, pure fixes, no new capability.

## v64 / 0.61.0, int 0x80 syscall gate, ring-3 tasks with a real lifecycle (Sep 2026)
The v28 deferred item and the "Accounts, passwords, real security" prerequisite, both `[Fable]`, shipped together since they were one change. Before: `ring3_test` was a one-shot ring-0 function that manually built an iret frame, dropped to ring 3, and the kernel halted for good on the payload's first fault; nothing could call into the kernel from ring 3 because no gate existed. After: a ring-3 task is a slot in `task.c`'s own table like any other, created by `task_create_user(entry, user_esp)`, and it ends one of three ways, every one of which returns the shell: `SYS_EXIT` through `int 0x80`, a CPU fault (reaped by `idt.c`, kernel keeps running), or `task_kill` from the shell.

The ABI (`kernel/syscall.h`, `kernel/syscall.c`, new) is x86 Linux's, borrowed not invented: `eax` = number, `ebx`/`ecx`/`edx` = arguments, result in `eax`, negative errno on failure, and the numbers are Linux i386's own (`__NR_exit` = 1, `__NR_write` = 4). Exactly two calls, the minimum that proves a round trip: `sys_exit(code)` records the status and goes through `task_exit_with`, the same free-stack-then-`int $32` path v28/v31 built; `sys_write(fd, buf, len)` copies from a user pointer to the console and serial, after `paging_user_range_ok` (new) has confirmed every page of `[buf, buf+len)` is actually mapped user-accessible, the same job Linux's `access_ok` does before `copy_from_user`, so a program can't hand the kernel a kernel address and have it echoed back. Dispatch is one table keyed by `eax` with null slots returning `-ENOSYS`, the `sys_call_table` shape. Nothing beyond that on purpose: no libc shim, no ELF loading, no wider surface.

`isr.S` grew `syscall_entry` (pusha, then DS/ES/FS/GS saved, kernel selectors loaded, `syscall_dispatch(frame *)`, everything restored, iret) and the 32 exception stubs were reshaped to the OSDev/James Molloy "push a dummy error code" form so `isr_handler(vector, err, eip, cs, eflags)` can read CS at a fixed slot. `idt.c` uses CS's RPL: ring 0 still reports and halts (a real kernel bug), ring 3 reports "ring-3 task hit general-protection (eip ...) -- task killed, kernel continues" and calls `task_exit_with(-vector)`. The gate is `0xEE` (present, DPL 3, interrupt gate): DPL 3 because with the exception gates' `0x8E` a user-mode `int $0x80` is itself a #GP, which would now look like a crashing program rather than a misconfigured gate; an interrupt gate rather than Linux's trap gate so IF is off for the whole handler, which makes `sys_exit`'s free-then-switch window safe by construction instead of by a `cli` it has to remember.

Two traps this tag exists for, both reasoned through against the Intel SDM before the first boot, neither visible in "it compiled": (1) `iret` to an outer privilege level silently nulls any of DS/ES/FS/GS still holding a selector more privileged than the new CPL, so a ring-3 task resumed by the timer with the kernel's `0x10` still loaded would #GP on its very next memory access. Fix: the four data segments are part of every task's saved frame now, pushed by `irq0`/every IRQ stub after `pusha` and popped before `popa` (`irq_stubs.S`), fabricated by `task_create_frame` (0x10 for ring 0, 0x23 for ring 3), the frame index `task_kill` patches moved from 8 to 12 (`F_EIP`). (2) One static TSS `esp0` can't serve two ring-3 tasks: the second's trap frame would land on top of the first's saved one. Fix: `schedule()` calls the new `gdt_set_kernel_stack` on every switch, pointing `esp0` at the next task's own kmalloc'd block, which is now a ring-3 task's kernel stack (its user stack is a separate user page), the Linux/xv6 shape; a ring-0 task never triggers the switch, so the same line is harmless for it. `task_kill` on a ring-3 task retargets the frame to ring 0 (kernel CS and data selectors) before pointing EIP at `task_exit`, since `task_exit` lives on a supervisor-only page and resuming there at CPL 3 would be a page fault, reaped by accident rather than an exit.

A real, pre-existing kernel bug found on the way, not by reasoning but by a triple fault: with the boot-time regression block below, `tasktest` reset the machine before printing a single `A`. QEMU's own `-d int,cpu_reset` trace (independent of anything the kernel printed): page fault at `EIP=c0105144` (objdump: the first `pop %gs` after `mov %eax, %esp` in `irq0`) reading `CR2=003d5fd0` under `CR3=003d7000`, then a double fault because the stack itself was on that page. Frozen at the fault with `-no-reboot` and read via the monitor: task_a's page directory at `0x3d7000` held `0x00000000 0x00000000 0x00000000 0xc01115f0` in PDE 0-3, and `nm` says `c01115f0` is `task_b`, i.e. task_b's freshly fabricated stack frame (zeroed registers, EIP) had been written over task_a's directory, while PDE 768 (kernel high half) was still `0x00371023`, which is exactly why the code kept running until it touched a low address. Cause, `kernel/kheap.c`'s `grow_heap`: it did `heap_limit = frame + 4096` without checking the new pmm frame was contiguous with the old limit, and `task_create` interleaves a 4KB stack kmalloc with `paging_new_task_directory`'s three pmm frames, so the second task's block was carved straight across the first task's directory. `kfree`'s coalescing carried the same silent assumption (merges list neighbours as if memory-adjacent). Reproduced on untouched v62 built in `/tmp` with the same boot-time block: identical fault, `SP=003d5fe0`, 16 bytes higher, the old 11-word frame. So `tasktest` has been triple-faulting on main at least when run first after boot; a wider frame only moved it. Fixed in the same commit: a non-contiguous frame parks the stranded tail as its own free block when a header plus `MIN_SPLIT_PAYLOAD` fit, otherwise lets it go, and restarts the carve at the new frame; coalescing now merges only when the lower block's payload ends exactly at the higher one's header. `heaptest`'s three checks still pass, including the adjacent-coalesce one.

Verified headless (standing policy, no `-display cocoa` window), the same three-independent-proofs standard the v3 ring-3 work set, via the boot-time direct-call trick `check.sh` and v54/v56/v59 all use (a temporary block in `kmain` running real shell commands through `run()`, plus a temporary one-line serial mirror in `putc`, both removed before commit, `git diff` on `kernel/kernel.c` shows exactly the three intended lines). Two boots in opposite order so each proof-of-execution value got its own monitor read. Proof one, the kernel's own handlers naming what happened, from the serial log: `syscall: write(1) from ring 3: hello from ring 3 via int 0x80` (the string came out of user memory through the gate, not a kernel constant), `syscall: exit from ring 3`, `exit code 0x0000002a`, `int 0x80 round trip (write returned 31, exit 42): ok`; `!! ring-3 task hit general-protection (eip 0xc037a009) -- task killed, kernel continues` (page base + 9 is exactly the `outb` byte after the 3-byte `mov` and 6-byte marker store), then `ring 3 ran, faulted, and was reaped; kernel still alive: ok`; and for the spinner `ring 3 ran under preemption (counter moved): ok` / `kill: ring-3 task really stopped, slot reaped: ok`. Proof two, a direct QEMU-monitor read of the marker's physical address, taken from `nm` on the exact booted binary (recorded honestly: the first read used `0x380000` remembered from an earlier build, the temporary strings had shifted `.bss` to `0x379000`, and it read zeros; the script now derives the address per boot): order syscall/spin/fault ends with `xp /1xw 0x379000` = `0xabcdef01` (the v3 payload's own marker, written at ring 3 before its #GP); order fault/spin/suite/syscall ends with `0xabcd001f`, the kernel's 31-byte return value tagged and stored from ring 3 after `int 0x80` returned, a number only the kernel knew, sitting in user memory. Proof three, the regression suite unchanged in the same boot after both ring-3 exits and one ring-3 kill: `heaptest` (`alloc ok, no reuse` / `coalesced adjacent free blocks: ok` / `split leftover reused: ok`), `tasktest` (`ABABABABABABABABABAB` / `done`), `preempttest` (`preempted without yield: ok`), `mem` (`126988K free / 129920K total`), `killtest` (`kill: task really stopped: ok`), `reaptest` (`reap: freed slot really reused: ok`), `ring3test` all three modes ok, and `check.sh`'s own `0x9000` = `0xb007c0de` still there at the end. `make kernel.elf` clean (only the pre-existing `gui_draw_hello_script` warning), `./check.sh` -> `PASS: booted, reached gui_run`, `./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` -> `112 file-path references checked ... clean`.

Honest gaps: one user code page and one user stack page exist, so one ring-3 task at a time (`ring3_test` waits for its own); task 0 (the shell) can't be reaped and a ring-3 fault with `task_current() == 0` still halts, which can't happen today since nothing enters ring 3 from the shell's own context anymore; `sys_write` is bounded to 255 bytes per call; interrupts are off for the duration of a syscall (fine for two tiny calls, a real trap gate plus reentrancy audit is the day something blocking lands here); `landing/v86/kernel.elf` deliberately left unsynced (headless pass, and the idle tour never types `tasktest`/`ring3test`), same call v54/v55 made. The `[Joshua]` threat-model item below is now unblocked and untouched, a scope call not made here.

Rebased onto concurrent v63/0.60.1 (dock hover, which also grew `paging.c`'s `MAX_EXTRA_TABLES`) before push and re-verified headless on the rebased binary, same boot-time block: the marker moved to phys `0x385000` with the larger `.bss`, `xp /1xw 0x385000` = `0xabcd001f`, every line of the suite above identical, `check.sh` PASS. VERSION bumped 0.60.1 -> 0.61.0 (MINOR, new capability: syscall gate + scheduled ring-3 tasks) and tagged `jt-v64`.

## v65 / 0.62.0, day/night tint + weather particle overlay (Sep 2026)
Direct request, the two real pieces the "Weather-reactive wallpaper" note above left open past v60's wind-amplitude half: (1) the baked wallpaper photo now color-grades by real hour of day, (2) rain streaks / snow dots for the condition codes that warrant one, beyond wind amplitude alone. Explicitly did NOT touch the LLM-generated-live-wallpaper idea in that same note, still a real separate follow-up needing a runtime-replaceable framebuffer this kernel doesn't have.

**(1) Day/night tint.** Real time source reused, not invented: `daynight_update()` reads CMOS register 4 through the exact same BCD decode `gui_draw_menubar()`/`show_time()` already use (`(h & 0x0F) + ((h >> 4) * 10)`), rate-limited to once a real second so the wind loop's ~20fps wallpaper redraws don't hammer CMOS I/O. `daynight_calc(hour, ...)` is kept a pure function of an explicit hour (no live CMOS/ticks() read inside it), the same shape `wind_pct_for_weather_code` already has, specifically so `daynighttest` can drive it with fixed hours. Boundaries, a plain judgment call stated as one (no sunrise/sunset table here): 08:00-18:00 full daylight plus a small +10% brighten, 18:00-21:00 dusk deepening, 05:00-08:00 dawn lightening, 21:00-05:00 the real deep-night floor. `gui_daynight_tint_pct` lerps (this file's own existing `gui_lerp`) toward `0x00201009`, "the wallpaper's own espresso-brown" (already used under that exact name elsewhere in this file) at night and toward the documented Silver `0x00DDDDDD` by day, on purpose staying inside CLAUDE.md's Mojave palette: never black, never blue.

Real bug caught and fixed before it shipped, not after: `wind_base` (the swaying crown region's perf cache, built once per boot and never rebuilt) used to be populated by reading back the already-drawn, already-tinted framebuffer via `window_get_pixel_phys`. That would have baked whatever tint was in effect at the one-time cache build into the sky for the rest of the session, the ground/dock rows below the horizon re-tinting live on every redraw while the sky silently froze at boot's hour. Fixed before ever shipping it: `wind_base` now stores RAW samples (`gui_wallpaper_row`/`gui_wallpaper_px` called directly, no tint), and every reader (`gui_wallpaper_sample`, `gui_draw_wallpaper_rows_sway_ex`'s own wind-cache branch) tints fresh against the current hour on every read instead. `gui_wallpaper_color` (the dock shadow / tray-corner AA blend target) tints too, so the tray's edge always agrees with the real photo pixels next to it.

**(2) Weather particle overlay.** `weather_fx_kind(code)` reuses `weather_word()`'s own threshold boundaries exactly (same `code10/10` input, same cutoffs), so the overlay always matches the condition word the menu bar/dropdown already show: Clear/Cloudy/Fog get nothing, Rain/Showers/Storm get rain streaks, Snow gets snow dots. `WEATHER_PARTICLE_COUNT` (36) fixed-size array, advanced and drawn once per wind tick (`weather_fx_tick`, called from the same ~20fps loop in `gui_run` that already drives `wind_phase`, inside the same `dt` budget check that self-disables wind on a slow machine), same cost class as the existing wind sampler, no new subsystem. Rain streaks (`0x00C9C0B4`, light silvery-sand) and snow dots (`0x00EFEBE4`, the dock tray's own cream) both stay inside the Mojave palette, never a saturated or cold-blue tone. Honest scope limit, stated rather than hidden: particles only ever draw inside `WIND_TOP_ROW..WIND_HORIZON_ROW`, the same swaying sky band the wind tick already repaints in full every frame (which is what erases last frame's particles, no separate dirty-rect/restore logic needed); extending them below the horizon into the dock/ground band would need `gui_redraw_dock_band`'s own band-cache plumbing, real follow-up, not attempted here. Tied to `wind_enabled` by the same necessity: a NIC-less boot (v86) never calls `weather_fetch`, so `weather_have` stays 0 and the overlay is correctly a no-op, same "nothing fabricated" contract v56/v60 already set for every other weather-derived effect in this file.

**Standing QA (CLAUDE.md's 4b).** Two new permanent shell test commands, `daynighttest` and `weatherfxtest`, both added to `help`'s listing. `daynighttest` calls the pure `daynight_calc`/`gui_daynight_tint_pct` directly with fixed hours 2 (deep night) and 14 (real midday) against a fixed warm test color (`0x00C97A3E`), and asserts three real, discriminating things: the two hours produce a different color, the night one is genuinely darker (lower RGB sum), and neither ever goes cold-blue (blue channel never exceeds red). `weatherfxtest` calls `weather_fx_kind` directly against Clear/Fog/Rain/Snow/Storm codes, then seeds a real particle set and proves `weather_fx_tick_kind` actually moves particle 0 under a live Rain classification and does NOT move it under `WEATHER_FX_NONE` (saving/restoring the real particle globals around the test so a live overlay in progress isn't disturbed).

Both discriminating, not just "prints ok," proven by temporarily reverting each fix and rebuilding, exactly CLAUDE.md's 4b bar: with `daynight_calc` stubbed to always return `night=0, day=0`, the same boot-time direct-call harness (pinning both test hours, dumping `c2`/`c14` to `0x9010`/`0x9014`, reading back via the QEMU monitor's `xp` under `-display none`) read `c2 == c14 == 0x00c97a3e` (the raw, untinted test color), which `daynighttest`'s own `c2 != c14` check would report as FAILED; restored, real hour math gives `c2 = 0x005c361c` (R92 G54 B28) at hour 2 and `c14 = 0x00cb834d` (R203 G131 B77) at hour 14, hand-verified against the lerp formula by hand (e.g. R: `201 + (32-201)*65/100 = 92`), matching exactly. With `weather_fx_tick_kind`'s `if (kind == WEATHER_FX_NONE) return;` guard commented out, the same harness's `still_on_clear` flag flipped from `1` to `0` (particle 0 moved even under Clear), which `weatherfxtest` would report as FAILED; restored, it read `1` again. All three regression checks (`daynighttest`'s revert, `weatherfxtest`'s revert, and the real-code pass) done via the exact same temporary boot-time block (`kmain`, right after the existing `0x9000` marker, dumping to `0x9010`/`0x9014`/`0x9018`/`0x901c`/`0x9020`), fully reverted before commit, `git diff` on `kernel/kernel.c` shows no trace of it.

Real visible evidence too, not just the pure-function proof: a second temporary pass forced `daynight_hour = 2` inside `daynight_update()` and faked `weather_code10 = 610; weather_have = 1;` (a Rain reading, no NIC in this run) right after `settings_load()` in `kmain`, both reverted before commit. Booted headless (`-display none -vga std`, QMP over TCP, the same pattern `tools/checks/dockhover-check.py` already uses, chosen over HMP's `pmemsave` specifically because HMP mis-parses an unquoted `size /path` as division, this file's own earlier note), waited 6s for several real wind ticks to seed and advance particles, `pmemsave`d the real `0xfd000000` physical framebuffer, converted BGRA->PNG in Python. Actually looked at, not assumed: the whole scene reads genuinely night-dark and warm (the tree near-silhouette, the sky's oranges dimmed toward brown/purple, no blue cast), and a pixel scan of the sky band found 195 pixels at the exact rain-streak color `(201,192,180)` = `0x00C9C0B4`, clustered along a row near the horizon, real proof the overlay is actually drawn on screen, not just computed.

Verified for real: `make kernel.elf` clean (only the pre-existing `gui_draw_hello_script` unused-function warning), `./check.sh` -> `PASS: booted, reached gui_run`, `./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` -> the one pre-existing stale AGENTS-doc reference already on main (confirmed via a scoped git stash before touching anything, not introduced by this pass), otherwise clean. Headless the entire pass, no `-display cocoa` window opened (the worktree-agent standing instruction; the one visible window stays the main session's own). Rebased onto `origin/main` (still at `f205660`, docs-only, when this pass finished, no concurrent kernel.c landings) before tagging. VERSION bumped 0.61.0 -> 0.62.0 (MINOR, two new real capabilities) and tagged `jt-v65`.

## v66 / 0.62.1, QA audit: 6 real gaps found in v56-v64, real tests added (Sep 2026)
Direct request per CLAUDE.md's new 4b: audit the 9 features shipped today (v56 weather dropdown, v56.1 notif-panel cursor fix, v57 kheap splitting, v58 dock shadow/corner AA, v59 Mail + dock reorder, v60 weather-reactive wind, v62 VMware absolute mouse, v63 dock hover fix, v64 syscall ABI) for which ones only ever got a one-off framebuffer dump or screenshot during their own pass, versus which have a real, permanent, discriminating test left in the suite. Read the actual code for every one, not assumed from the roadmap's own claims.

**Already real, confirmed by reading, not trusted blind.** `heaptest`'s third check (v57, "split leftover reused: ok") is a real block-split proof, still in `kernel.c`, unchanged. `tools/checks/vmmouse-check.sh` (v62) asserts real QMP-injected packet values against the kernel's own serial log in both the backdoor and `vmport=off` fallback paths, re-run here and still PASS. `tools/checks/dockhover-check.py` (v63) asserts real pixel state (tile presence, which slot is lifted) against a live QMP-driven hover sweep, re-run here and still PASS. `ring3test`'s default mode (v64) already exercises the real `int 0x80` gate end to end (write returns 31 through the gate, exit code 42, both checked against exact values only the kernel could have produced), not just the ring-3 privilege mechanism in isolation.

**Six real gaps, each fixed with a genuinely discriminating test, each proven by temporarily reverting the underlying fix, rebuilding, confirming the new test actually fails, then restoring it and confirming it passes again** (the same bar v65's own `daynighttest`/`weatherfxtest` pair set the same day):
- **`weatherpaneltest` (v56).** That pass's own verification was a boot-time block reading pixel values into fixed RAM, reverted before commit; nothing left behind. Two checks: `weather_word()`'s WMO-code bucket mapping (every boundary, not one code per bucket) and `gui_draw_weather_panel()` actually painting its own bg/border chrome plus real antialiased ink once `weather_have` is set. Reverting `gui_draw_weather_panel()` to a no-op failed both pixel checks; a wrong `weather_word` boundary failed the word check alone.
- **`cursortest` (v56.1).** The notif-panel bitmap-font bug (cursor save/restore reading/writing real antialiased text through the LOGICAL layer, `window_get_pixel`/`window_pixel`, instead of the physical accessors) was root-caused and fixed that pass via a scripted headless sweep + `pmemsave`, then fully reverted, nothing permanent left to catch the same bug class recurring on any other never-repainted AA surface. The test renders real DejaVu glyphs (`font_set_aa(gui_aa_char)`, since this test doesn't call `gui_run()` to register the hook itself) at a scale-2 window, captures the true per-physical-pixel content independently via `window_get_pixel_phys`, runs the real `gui_cursor_save`/`gui_draw_cursor`/`gui_cursor_restore` sequence, and confirms every physical sub-pixel came back byte-exact. Reverting `gui_cursor_save`/`restore` to the old logical accessors (kept the array, just swapped the read/write calls and loop bounds back to their pre-v56.1 shape) produced 188 mismatched physical pixels; restored, 0.
- **`mailtest` (v59).** Mail shipped with zero regression coverage, no `mailtest`-shaped command existed at all (checked, not assumed) and no precedent from Reminders/Calendar to copy either, since `check-calendar.sh` only covers date math, nothing VFS-backed. Round-trips three known messages through `mail_save()` -> `MAIL.TXT` -> a forced `mail_load()` (state wiped first, `mail_loaded` reset, so this is a real disk read, not the array still sitting in RAM; runs against the `ramfs` backend explicitly via `vfs_switch`, restored after, so the test is real and repeatable with no FAT disk image attached), confirming every field and the read flag survived the `|`-delimited parse. Also exercises `mail_delete_at()`, pulled out of `gui_launch_mail`'s inline `'d'` handler into its own function specifically so the test and the real UI share one code path rather than a re-typed copy that could drift from it. Dropping the `read`-flag copy out of the shift loop failed the delete check while leaving the round-trip check green, confirming the two checks are actually independent.
- **`windweathertest` (v60).** That pass's verification was a boot-time dump of three `gui_wind_shift()` samples under faked weather codes, reverted before commit. Pins `wind_pct_for_weather_code()`'s seven WMO buckets against the exact percentages the v60 entry's own table names, every boundary. Moving the Fog bucket's return value by 5 failed it immediately.
- **`dockstyletest` (v58 top half + v61 top half).** `dockhover-check.py` only ever covered the v63 hover-magnify bug, a different defect; the v58 shadow/corner-AA fix and v61's top-edge-seam fix were both verified once against a real pixel dump and never covered again. Calls `gui_rounded_rect_on_wallpaper` and `gui_draw_icon_shadow` directly with real dock geometry (no QEMU pixel-dump script needed, both are ordinary functions): the top edge of a rect painted over real wallpaper must show a multi-row blend, not the pre-v61 bug's exact 1px solid-color cut (temporarily disabling the blend branch reproduced the old cut and failed the check); the icon shadow's vertical centreline must show at least 7 distinct color levels, close to its own physical `ry+1`, not the pre-v58 bug's coarse ~5 logical levels each doubled into a block (temporarily reverting the function to its old logical-layer math, `size/9` instead of `(size*sc)/9` and `window_pixel` instead of `window_pixel_phys`, reproduced the old blockiness and failed the check).
- **`ring3test badptr` mode (v64).** `sys_write`'s `paging_user_range_ok` check (this kernel's `access_ok`) had no test proving it actually rejects a bad pointer, only that valid writes succeed (the default mode). New `ring3_badptr_code` payload (`ring3_asm.S`) calls `SYS_WRITE` with `buf=NULL` and records the raw kernel return value; the test asserts it's exactly `-EFAULT` (`0xFFFFFFF2`) and the task still exits cleanly with its own distinct code (7), proving the kernel rejected the pointer rather than crashing or silently reading through it. Temporarily short-circuiting the check in `sys_write` (`if (0 && !paging_user_range_ok(...))`) let the NULL read through: the kernel returned the real byte count instead of `-EFAULT` (2, since physical address 0 happened to be mapped and readable in this build, not a crash), and the badptr test correctly failed while every other new test stayed green in the same boot, confirming the checks don't false-positive off each other.

**Honest remaining gaps, not covered here, stated rather than hidden:** v58's corner-AA fix (blending each rounded corner toward the real per-pixel wallpaper sample instead of a wrong centre-column sample) and v61's diagonal ray-aliasing fix (`gui_icon_weather`'s proportional `ray_r`) still have no dedicated regression test. Both are real, narrower defects than the two covered above; a future pass should either extend `dockstyletest` with a wallpaper backdrop that varies enough horizontally to make centre-vs-per-pixel sampling actually distinguishable, or add a direct-render check on `gui_icon_weather` at two very different sizes comparing ray width proportionally.

Verified for real, not assumed: `make kernel.elf` clean (only the pre-existing `gui_draw_hello_script` unused-function warning), `./check.sh` -> `PASS: booted, reached gui_run`, `./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` -> the same one pre-existing stale AGENTS-doc reference v65 already found and left on `main` (confirmed via `git show origin/main:roadmap.md`, not introduced by this pass), otherwise clean, `./tools/checks/vmmouse-check.sh` and `tools/checks/dockhover-check.py` both still PASS unchanged. All six new tests run together in one boot via the standard temporary `kmain` block + one-line `putc` serial mirror (the same trick v54/v56/v59/v64/v65 all use), fully reverted before commit, `git diff` on `kernel/kernel.c` shows only the six real permanent additions plus the `mail_delete_at` extraction. Headless the entire pass (worktree-agent standing instruction), no `-display cocoa` window opened.

Rebased onto concurrent `origin/main` (`v65`/`0.62.0`, day/night tint + weather particle overlay, which also touched `kernel.c`'s `run()` dispatcher) before push; the merge conflict was two new `else if` chains both inserted at the same point in the command table (v65's `daynighttest`/`weatherfxtest`, this pass's six), resolved by keeping both, re-verified clean after. VERSION bumped 0.62.0 -> 0.62.1 (PATCH: test coverage only, no new capability) and tagged `jt-v66`.

## Later idea: a real JavaScript interpreter, honestly scoped (Sep 2026)
Direct question: "full JavaScript support and even v8 engine like Chrome so we can play shit in the browser eventually?" Real answer, recorded accurately rather than oversold: actual V8 is not portable here, full stop. It's millions of lines of C++, needs a JIT, a generational garbage collector, pthreads, mmap, a real libc, and a real POSIX-shaped OS underneath it, the same category of infrastructure as `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own "no libc, no dependencies beyond clang/ld.lld/qemu" constraint rules out by design. Porting real V8 to this kernel isn't a roadmap item, it's a different, much larger project (realistically Linux/BSD-kernel-scale effort on its own).

A real, buildable version of the same idea exists at a much smaller scale, matching the "deliberately tiny" precedent `html.c`'s own parser already set for this kernel (v8's own real browser work, unrelated numbering coincidence to the JS engine everyone calls V8): a small embeddable JS interpreter in the shape of mujs or an early QuickJS, not spec-complete, not JIT-compiled, a straightforward parser plus a tree-walking evaluator over a small object/number/string model. Still a genuinely large, multi-version undertaking on its own (a real tokenizer/parser, a real number model matching JS's float64 semantics, a real prototype-chain object model, closures), not a quick add-on to `html.c`.

Realistic prerequisite chain, most-blocking first: this kernel's existing `kheap.c` allocator (already real, already growing past its old wall) is enough to back a tree-walking interpreter's own allocations, no new memory subsystem needed first. The real blocker is scope and time, not a missing kernel primitive the way audio input needs a driver that doesn't exist. Not started, v100+ tier by the same honesty standard as the mic/camera/WiFi notes above, no code written against this yet.

## Later idea: Mail multi-account + real SMTP/IMAP, honestly scoped (Sep 2026)
Direct question after seeing the real Mail app (v59) work: multiple accounts, real SMTP/IMAP so it can send/receive against a real provider. Real answer, checked against the actual code rather than assumed: this kernel has zero TLS/SSL/crypto anywhere (`grep -ri tls\|ssl\|crypto drivers/net.c drivers/http.c` returns nothing), and every real mail provider that matters (Gmail, iCloud, Outlook) requires STARTTLS or implicit TLS on the wire, most also require OAuth instead of a plain password. Neither exists here.

What's real and buildable without new crypto: multi-account is just a data-model extension of `kernel/mail.h`'s existing `MAIL.TXT`-backed struct array, the same VFS-backed pattern Reminders/Calendar already prove out, genuinely easy. Plaintext SMTP (port 25, no STARTTLS, `AUTH PLAIN`/`AUTH LOGIN` over an unencrypted socket) is a real, buildable protocol on top of the TCP stack that already works (`net.c`, proven since v7-v10's real DNS+TCP+HTTP round trip), but it only works against a mail server that still accepts unencrypted SMTP, which real consumer providers dropped years ago. A real receive path (IMAP or POP3) is the same shape, same blocker.

Realistic prerequisite chain: a real TLS implementation (even a minimal one, e.g. following a small embeddable reference like BearSSL's own design) is the actual blocker, a large undertaking on the same order as the JS-interpreter note above, not a mail-specific problem, once built it would also unlock HTTPS for `web`/`browse` (currently plaintext HTTP only) and any future OAuth flow. Not started, v100+ tier by the same honesty standard as the other notes in this file, no code written against this yet. Until then, Mail stays what it honestly is: a real local notes-shaped app with mail chrome, not a mail client.

## Real, buildable now: a better chat app, ahead of the v100 voice north star (Sep 2026)
Direct request: "get LLMs working better within our chat app, and get a voice app going too... might be a bigger scope." Splitting that honestly: voice needs the real hardware-driver prerequisite chain the v100 north star above already scopes (HDA/AC97, USB audio), genuinely not buildable yet. The chat half is different, real, and buildable today with zero new kernel infrastructure, since the local Ollama HTTP path (`chat`, v10) already works over the existing TCP/HTTP stack, no TLS blocker the way SMTP/HTTPS hit.

Real limitations in today's `chat` command, checked against the actual code (`kernel/kernel.c`'s `chat` handler) rather than assumed:
- Uses `/api/generate`, a single-shot completion endpoint with no conversation history. Every call is a fresh prompt; the model has no memory of the previous turn. Ollama's `/api/chat` endpoint is the real fix, it takes a message array and the server keeps context.
- `req_body` is a fixed 768-byte buffer, the escaped user message alone is capped at 512 bytes; a longer question silently truncates.
- `resp`/`answer` are fixed at 4096/2048 bytes; a longer model reply truncates the same way.
- `stream:false`: the whole reply has to finish generating server-side before anything prints, no real streaming output the way a real chat UI has.
- Shell-only (`chat <message>` typed at the prompt), no GUI window. Mail/Calendar/Reminders (v52/v54/v59) already proved the pattern for a real desktop app with its own window and persisted state; chat has none of that, it's a one-shot command with nothing kept between calls.

Realistic buildable scope, most valuable first: (1) switch to `/api/chat` and keep a real message-history buffer (VFS-backed, same `vfs_replace_file`/`vfs_read_file` write-through pattern Mail/Calendar/Reminders already use, so a conversation actually persists across boots the same way a Mail inbox does); (2) grow the request/response buffers to something realistic for an actual conversation, not a one-line question; (3) a real GUI Chat app (dock icon, its own window, scrollback) instead of a shell-only command, following the exact same app-shape precedent the last three built-in apps set. Streaming output is real but lower priority, needs chunked-transfer-encoding handling in `http.c` that doesn't exist yet, a smaller but real sub-scope of its own.

## v85 / 0.70.0, the chat app scope above, shipped (Sep 2026)
All three pieces landed, plus one real prerequisite bug found mid-build that the "zero new kernel infrastructure" framing above turned out to be wrong about, and fixed honestly rather than worked around.

**New `kernel/chat.h`**, same shape as `mail.h`/`contacts.h`: `CHAT.TXT`, `role|content` per line, an 8-message bounded ring (oldest drops once full, `chat_push`). `chat_send` builds a real `/api/chat` request (`{"model":...,"stream":false,"messages":[...]}`) from the whole history, not just the newest turn, so the model actually has memory across calls now, the real fix for v10's single-shot `/api/generate` gap. `gui_launch_chat_app` is a real scrollback window (`gui_draw_app_titlebar` chrome, `n` sends, `c` clears, esc closes), replacing the old `gui_launch_chat` one-question popup that drew its own bespoke titlebar instead of the shared one, an inconsistency fixed in the same pass. The shell `chat <message>` command and the GUI app now call the same `chat_send`, one history, one code path, not two.

**Buffer growth hit a real second wall, not assumed away.** Growing `kernel.c`'s own request buffer would have been a no-op: `drivers/http.c`'s `http_post` hard-capped the whole outbound HTTP request at a fixed 1024-byte stack buffer regardless of what any caller passed it, and `drivers/net.c`'s `tcp_get` rejected any request over one 536-byte TCP segment outright (`TCP_MAX_PAYLOAD`), found by actually tracing the send path rather than trusting a bigger caller-side buffer alone. Both fixed for real: `http_post` now heap-sizes its request buffer to the caller's real `body_len` (same pattern `http_get` already used for its own response buffer), and `tcp_get` now sends a request as multiple back-to-back `TCP_MAX_PAYLOAD` segments instead of refusing anything past one, correctly sequenced, no per-chunk ACK wait (a local QEMU/SLIRP link, the same no-retransmission assumption the rest of this file already makes). Without this, the roadmap's own "no new kernel infra needed" claim above would have been false; this is the honest correction. New caps: `req_body` 6144 bytes (was 768), `resp` 8192 (was 4096), `answer` 4096 (was 2048), per-message content 640 bytes (was a 512-byte escaped-input cap that silently truncated).

**Global LLM config in Settings**, extending `SETTINGS.TXT` past its old int-only `wind=`/`dock=`/`wall=` shape to also carry `llmmodel=`/`llmhost=`/`llmport=`. Both the shell `chat` command and the GUI app read the same `llm_model`/`llm_host`/`llm_port` globals, one source of truth instead of the two independently hardcoded `"llama3.1:8b"`/`"10.0.2.2"`/`11434` copies `chat` and the old `gui_launch_chat` each carried. Direct feedback mid-build: a free-text model field could be typo'd to point at a model that isn't installed, silently failing every call. Real fix, checked against `ollama list` on the actual host rather than guessed: Settings' LLM-model row cycles a bounded list of the two real chat models installed (`llama3.1:8b`, the existing default; `qwen3:8b`), `nomic-embed-text` deliberately left off since it's embedding-only, not a chat model. A stale/hand-edited `SETTINGS.TXT` naming anything else falls back to the default on load, the same "can't select a theme that doesn't exist" clamp `wall_theme` already keeps. Host/port stay free text (edited via a new `settings_prompt_line`, the same contacts.h/mail.h prompt shape), since there's no fixed list of real hosts to validate against.

**Verification, per 4b/4f.** New `chattest` shell command (serial-mirrored for headless reads, `tools/checks/chat-check.sh`): round-trips history through `CHAT.TXT`, proves a 600-char message survives past the old 511-byte effective cap, proves `chat_build_request` carries both turns (not just the latest, the real regression the old single-shot design would produce), proves the ring drops the oldest message past `CHAT_MAX`. Proven discriminating: temporarily shrinking `CHAT_CONTENT_MAX` back to 400 and rebuilding made `chat-check.sh` FAIL exactly as expected; restored, PASS. Full regression suite re-run clean: `check.sh`, `tools/checks/check-refs.sh`, `tools/png-host-check.sh`, `tools/checks/png-check.sh`, `tools/checks/textspacing-check.sh`, `tools/checks/vmmouse-check.sh`, `tools/checks/appclose-check.py` (Chat opens/closes from its dock icon like every other app), `tools/checks/dockhover-check.py`, all PASS. Security-minded look at the new network-input surface: `json_extract_string`'s bounds check is parametrized by the caller's `maxlen`, already safe against the larger `resp`/`answer` buffers with no change needed; `chat_sanitize_copy` strips `|`/`\n`/`\r` from anything written to `CHAT.TXT` (a model reply comes over the network and could contain either, corrupting the field-delimited format on-disk otherwise); every buffer-building loop in `chat_build_request`/`http_post`/`tcp_get`'s new chunking is bound-checked against its own cap, not just `strcpy`'d. Real screenshots (headless QMP + `pmemsave`, worktree-agent-safe, no `-display cocoa` popped): the new Chat window opens from its dock icon with the standard `gui_draw_app_titlebar` chrome, and Settings shows the two new LLM rows (`LLM model: llama3.1:8b`, `LLM host:port: 10.0.2.2:11434`) in the same row style as Wind/Dock size/Wallpaper.

`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s Subsystems table and README's Piece|Where table both updated with the new `kernel/chat.h` row/count, per 4c. Not done: real streaming output (still needs chunked-transfer-encoding support in `http.c`, a real sub-scope of its own, correctly flagged lower-priority above) and a live `/api/tags` probe to validate the model list against what's actually installed on the host at runtime rather than a hardcoded two-model cycle (a real, scoped-out next step if the host's installed-model set ever needs to grow past these two).

## Later idea: a native Stocks app + why Epiphany itself can't run here (Sep 2026)
Direct request: add a native Stocks app, and "test epiphany on our bare metal" (Epiphany, `~/Documents/Code/epiphany`, the real finance/portfolio dashboard in the fleet). Two different answers, checked against real code, not assumed.

**Epiphany itself: not possible here, same category as the SMTP/V8 gaps above, not a smaller version of the problem.** Epiphany's real stack (per its own CLAUDE.md) is Supabase auth + Stripe + a real web/SwiftUI frontend, every one of those HTTPS-only, most needing OAuth or a real session token exchange on top. This kernel has zero TLS anywhere (confirmed by the same `grep` check the Mail/SMTP note above already ran), so Epiphany can't authenticate, can't reach Supabase, can't reach Stripe, full stop, the same wall the fleet's own TUI rollout already hit for exactly this reason: Epiphany was explicitly excluded from that lighter (Foundation-only, stateless CLI) port for having "auth-gated personal/financial/health/legal data, nothing to show a stateless CLI without building a login flow." A kernel-native port is a strictly harder version of a problem the TUI rollout already declined to solve. Not queued, not a real roadmap item, the honest answer is no until real TLS exists.

**A native Stocks app is a real, different, lighter idea, shaped like the existing Weather app.** `weather_fetch` (`kernel/kernel.c`) already proves the real shape works: `http_get("api.open-meteo.com", ..., 80, ...)`, a genuine public API that answers on plain HTTP, port 80, no key. A Stocks app in the same shape (real quote data pulled over plain HTTP, no auth, no portfolio/account state, no Epiphany backend involved at all) is real and buildable *if* a real market-data provider with a plain-HTTP, keyless endpoint exists, the same constraint that made Weather possible. Honest gap, actually checked, not left open: real `curl` tests against Stooq, Yahoo Finance's chart API, Finnhub, and Google Finance all force a redirect to HTTPS (Yahoo's first response looked promising, a plain `429` with no redirect, but that was rate-limiting hiding the real behavior; a clean retry showed the same forced `301` to `https://` the others give). No plain-HTTP, keyless stock-quote source found among the obvious real candidates. Real next step, if this gets revisited: either keep looking for a more obscure real provider, or accept a Stocks app can only show delayed/static demo data baked in at build time, the same shape several already-ported fleet apps use for their own static content. Not started, not v100+ tier since it needs no new kernel subsystem, just a confirmed real data source, which is the actual blocker.

## Later idea: the rest of the stock-macOS app gap, honestly categorized (Sep 2026)
Direct question after Mail/Calendar/Reminders/Weather shipped: what else is missing versus a real stock macOS app roster (Music, Contacts, Photos, Maps/Navigation named directly). Checked against real kernel capability, not wishlisted.

**Buildable now, no new subsystem needed:** Contacts (same VFS-backed `*.TXT` pattern Mail/Reminders/Calendar already prove, a plain name/detail list, zero network dependency). Calculator (numen's real parser was already flagged portable back in the v6 native-app-porting note, pure logic, no network, no hardware). A World Clock wrapping the RTC that's existed since v1.

**Blocked, real reasons, not vague:** Photos needs an image decoder (JPEG/PNG) this kernel has never had, the same gap the "AI-generated live wallpaper" idea already named honestly ("no image codec exists or is planned"), a real new subsystem, not a quick app. Music needs real audio output, the same HDA/AC97 driver prerequisite the v100 voice north star is already blocked on, not a separate gap, the same one. Maps/Navigation is double-blocked: no real GPS/location today (Weather's lat/long is a hardcoded constant, not live geolocation), and rendering real map tiles needs the same missing image decoder Photos needs; even past that, real tile servers mostly force HTTPS the same way the Stocks-app data-source check just proved for market data. Messages/FaceTime need a real messaging backend or camera/mic hardware, blocked the same categories as Mail's SMTP (TLS/OAuth) or worse (real hardware drivers).

Not started. Contacts + Calculator are real, low-risk, buildable-today items, queued as their own task. Photos/Music/Maps/Messages all trace back to one of three named prerequisites already logged elsewhere in this file (image decoder, audio driver, TLS), not separate scope, solving one of those three unlocks more than one app at once.

## v67 / 0.62.2, "stuck on Notes" root-caused: one app's mouse loop, not a kernel-wide freeze (Sep 2026)
Direct request. Reported from the landing page's live v86 demo as "that notes app stuck glitch", then re-reported as "all apps ... I think it was a kernel bug", with Reminders "failing to open at all". The main session had just opened and closed Mail and Calendar on the native app cleanly, so the working theory going in was split: either a universal kernel bug or something v86-specific. The evidence supports neither as stated. It IS kernel-side (not a browser/JS race), but it is NOT all apps, and it reproduces natively with no browser involved.

**Reproduction, headless, on the real native kernel first.** New `tools/checks/appclose-check.py`, the same QMP absolute-pointer + `pmemsave` shape as `dockhover-check.py`: click every dock slot in order, assert the window chrome's red close button is on screen, click that button, assert it's gone. Against v66: Apps, Files, Mail, Calendar, Reminders, Terminal, Weather, Trash all open and close; **Notes and Chat stay open after their X is clicked**, and the reported shape itself ("Notes open, click Reminders in the dock") leaves the screen stuck on Notes. That last one is the whole "all apps" report: `gui_launch_from_dock` deliberately keeps the desktop and dock visible around the app window, so with a modal Notes that no click can close, every later dock click looks dead, Reminders included. Nothing was wrong with Reminders.

**Root cause, two real bugs, both read straight out of the code once the sweep named the apps.** (1) `kernel/editor.h`'s input loop predates v62 and was never migrated: it read `mouse_get_delta` only and never `mouse_get_absolute`. Under the vmmouse backdoor (live on QEMU's default pc machine, i.e. the native app, and in v86) `mouse.c` deliberately discards PS/2 deltas and reports zero motion, so Notes' pointer never left the dock tile that opened it, and its close hitbox (viewport-local 38x32 at the top-left, which isn't even where the windowed chrome draws the X) was unreachable from any real pointer. Every other app polls through `gui_wait_close`/`get_key_or_click`, which go through `gui_app_mouse_tick` + `mouse_click_edge` and were fine, exactly matching the live Mail/Calendar pass. (2) `gui_launch_chat`'s message box read `get_key()` only, click-blind by construction, so a visitor with no keyboard (a phone, or the idle tour, which types into Chat and then taps once to close) could open it and never leave. A third, independent way to be stuck on Notes on the landing page specifically: v86 boots with no FAT disk, so every save fails, and the old "first close warns, second close goes through" rule meant the tour's single closing tap after typing real text sat on a "Save failed" screen forever.

**Fix.** Notes takes its pointer from `gui_app_mouse_tick` (screen coordinates, absolute-aware, the one sprite every other app already draws) and its clicks from `mouse_click_edge` (survives a tap's press+release landing in one poll); a click anywhere outside the text area (window chrome, the X, the desktop) closes it, the same "click closes" contract the rest of the GUI keeps for a keyboardless visitor, and the toolbar keeps working inside. A new `gui_app_cursor_hide` lifts the sprite before each full-viewport repaint so the backup under it can't be restored stale over fresh text. Chat's box and the Mail/Calendar/Reminders text prompts accept a click as cancel. A failed save no longer holds Notes open: `editor_buffer` is static and stays loaded, reopening shows the text, the status line says it's RAM-only until a save works. Also fixed while in there, visible on the first real dump: the editor's text area and status line were laid out for a hardcoded 800x600, so in the dock-launched 804x345 viewport the status line (y=570) was clipped clean off and the caret's scroll logic counted lines nothing drew; both now size from `window_height()`. And `editor_draw` drew a second cursor at a viewport-local position on top of the real screen-space one.

**Evidence, all headless.** `appclose-check.py` after the fix: all 10 dock slots open and close, the Notes-then-Reminders scenario is "not stuck", Mail opens and closes again at the end (input intact). Real framebuffer PNGs of the fixed Notes window: typed "hello" in place, one pointer sprite, status line visible at the bottom of the viewport, X closes it, reopen shows the text with the failed-save status. v86 path: `mobiletest.mjs` grew a permanent step (real iPhone 13 emulation on the real page, `landing/v86/kernel.elf` synced) that opens Notes by tap, types through v86's own `keyboard_send_text`, taps the window's X, and asserts the desktop is back. Run against a locally served copy of the landing page with the v66 `kernel.elf` swapped in: `closed by its X=false`, FAIL; with the fixed one: PASS, tap-to-app 523ms. Both harnesses therefore discriminate, per 4b. `check.sh`, `apptest.sh` (the full-screen `testapps` keyboard path, which also runs the editor's non-windowed branch), `vmmouse-check.sh`, `dockhover-check.py` all still PASS. Rebased onto the concurrent `origin/main` (CLAUDE.md's 4c + roadmap-only commits, no `kernel.c` overlap) before push.

**Honest answer to the two theories.** "Kernel bug": yes, in the sense that the fix is entirely kernel-side and it reproduces on plain QEMU with no browser; no, in the sense that it was never a scheduler/IRQ/driver freeze, the kernel was polling fine the whole time, one app's loop was asking the wrong driver function. "v86-specific": no, though v86 made it worse (no disk, so the save-failed hold added a second trap), and it's where a visitor with no keyboard first meets it, since Esc always worked. Not touched here, logged in the multi-window scope below: a dock click while an app is open still just closes the app, it doesn't also open the clicked one.

**Still open, stated rather than hidden:** `gui_draw_app_titlebar` draws the app's own title (e.g. "Notes *") inside the viewport under the chrome's identical title, a visible duplicate in every windowed app since the chrome landed; cosmetic, untouched here to keep this a pure fix.

## v68 / 0.62.3, restraint pass: one flyout-panel chrome instead of two, and a real icon-glyph bleed bug (Sep 2026)
Direct request: "make the OS UI more beautiful, Johnny Ive philosophy," pointed at the existing static chrome (menu bar, dock, window chrome, the notif/weather dropdowns), explicitly not window management (a separate queued task) and not the wallpaper/weather effects v65 just shipped. Real Ive restraint means finding what's inconsistent or unearned and removing it, not adding a look; both real findings below are exactly that shape, found by actually reading every `gui_draw_*` panel function in `kernel.c` and pmemsave-dumping the live desktop before touching anything, not by assumption.

**Finding: two chrome languages on one desktop.** The dock tray, the weather app's own card, and the Apps folder glass all draw as a soft rounded rect blended straight into whatever's behind them (`gui_rounded_rect_on_wallpaper`, zero separate stroke line). The Apple menu, the clock's notif panel, and the weather dropdown (`gui_draw_apple_menu`/`gui_draw_notif_panel`/`gui_draw_weather_panel`) were the one place still built the old way: a flat, hard-cornered `window_rect` fill plus a manually drawn 1px border on all four sides, confirmed with a real headless capture (`-display none`, QMP-driven, `pmemsave` + BGRA->PNG) showing a plain gray rectangle with square corners sitting directly against the dock's rounded ones a few hundred pixels away, two different surfaces reading as two different objects on the same desktop. Fixed by removing the bespoke rectangle-and-border code from all three and replacing it with the one call the dock already uses, `gui_rounded_rect_on_wallpaper(x0, y0, w, h, bg, GUI_FLYOUT_RADIUS)`: one new shared constant (`GUI_FLYOUT_RADIUS 14`), reused three times, not three different numbers, not a new primitive. This is genuinely restraint, not decoration: the three panels lose their separate hard border entirely and pick up nothing except the curve everything else already had.

Fixing it uncovered a real second-order bug the same pass had to close, not left half-done: the Apple menu's row-hover highlight (`window_rect`, inset 2px from the panel edge) had a hard square corner of its own, and once the panel's own top/bottom corners went round, hovering the first or last row (`About Joshua Tree` / `Shut Down`) showed the highlight's flat corner poking out past the panel's new curve into the menu bar, a worse seam than the one this pass just removed, caught by re-screenshotting with `hover_item` forced to 0 rather than assuming the fix was cosmetically complete. Real fix, not a bigger radius on the highlight: `GUI_MENU_PAD_V` (8px) padding above the first row and below the last, solved from the same circle math `gui_rounded_rect_on_wallpaper` already uses (the row where a 2px-inset flat edge first stays inside a 14px curve), applied to both the draw loop and `gui_menu_hit_test` so clicking still lands where it now visually sits.

**Finding: several dock icon glyphs bleed past their own rounded corner.** Reported directly, from a real live-desktop screenshot: a light halo around Mail/Calendar/Weather/Terminal's icon edges, inconsistent across icons. Root-caused, not guessed at, with a real 4x-zoomed headless capture of every dock icon: `gui_render_icon_cached` renders each icon into a supersampled buffer (`gui_rounded_rect_gradient` carving the rounded silhouette, background pixels outside it set to the real `under`/tray color), then draws the glyph via `gui_draw_capsule`/`gui_fill_circle`, whose own antialiasing only knows to blend toward `bg` (the icon's flat base color), never toward the real background outside the curve. `gui_icon_weather`'s 8 sun rays are sized, by design, to reach toward every corner; a direct pixel sample of the icon's own top-left corner (a 4x4 physical-pixel block a correctly clipped icon leaves untouched) came back solid `(255,255,255)`, the ray's own unclipped white, not the tray's real `(239,235,228)` cream. Mail's checkmark and Calendar's binder-ring dots showed the same, fainter, for the same reason (their strokes reach close to a corner too); Files, Trash, and the Apps grid, whose glyphs stay centered, showed none, which is what confirmed this is geometry reaching past the curve and not a general AA shortfall.

Fixed once, for every icon, rather than retuning eight separate glyph functions: `gui_render_icon_cached` now re-applies the exact corner math `gui_rounded_rect_gradient` already uses as a final mask, after every glyph has drawn, forcing any supersample pixel outside the true curve back to `under` (full pixels beyond the radius) or re-blending it toward `under` inside the existing AA band (pixels in the transition zone), the identical arithmetic, not a new rule, just run a second time as a mask instead of only once as a first pass.

**Standing QA (CLAUDE.md's 4b).** `tools/checks/iconhalo-check.py`, new, the `dockhover-check.py` shape: boots headless (`-display none -vga std`), waits for the desktop's first frame (no click needed, the dock renders on boot), `pmemsave`s the real framebuffer, and asserts the Weather icon's own top-left 4x4 physical-pixel corner block is pure tray color. Proven discriminating, not just "prints ok": with the new corner-clip loop in `gui_render_icon_cached` wrapped in `#if 0` and the kernel rebuilt, the check read `16/16 off-tray pixels` and correctly printed FAIL (sample `(255,255,255)`, the pre-fix bleed exactly as found); restored (`#if 1`) and rebuilt, it reads `0/16` and PASS. The panel chrome fix is a pure aesthetic/consistency call with no test oracle beyond "does it look like one object," so per CLAUDE.md's 4b that one gets the documented before/after screenshot comparison above instead of a permanent test, not a coverage gap, a judgment call recorded as one.

Verified for real: `make kernel.elf` clean (only the pre-existing `gui_draw_hello_script` unused-function warning), `./check.sh` -> `PASS: booted, reached gui_run`, `./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean except the one pre-existing stale doc-file mention already on `main` before this pass (not introduced here, same one v65/v66 already logged), `./tools/checks/dockhover-check.py` and `./tools/checks/vmmouse-check.sh` both still PASS (dock geometry and paging untouched by this pass). Every screenshot was a real `pmemsave` off a real headless boot, no `-display cocoa` window opened (the worktree-agent standing policy; the one visible window stayed the main session's own live demo). Explicitly out of scope, left alone on purpose: window management/multi-window/app-open-close (a separate queued task), the wallpaper and weather particle effects v65 just shipped, and the still-open "general icon softness under 2x zoom" / diagonal-edge-aliasing item this pass did not re-attempt, a different, already-tracked defect from the corner-bleed bug fixed here.

This pass started against `origin/main` at `37f0702` (right after v65) and rebased twice while in flight as concurrent agents kept landing real work on `main`: first onto `2d9918e` (v66/0.62.1's QA audit plus four roadmap-only entries), renumbering this entry v66->v67; then onto the "stuck on Notes" app-close fix directly above (its own real v67/0.62.2, `8179d31`), renumbering again, v67->v68 and 0.62.2->0.62.3, to not collide with the tag already on `main`. `kernel.c` merged clean both times, zero overlap (the QA audit only touched `run()`'s dispatcher; the app-close fix only touched `gui_app_mouse_tick`/`editor.h`/`gui_launch_chat`, all well clear of the panel/icon functions this pass edited); both `roadmap.md` conflicts were independent appends, resolved by keeping both sides. VERSION bumped 0.62.2 -> 0.62.3 (PATCH, real fixes and reused-infrastructure polish, no new capability) and tagged `jt-v68`.

## v69 / 0.63.0, dock close-and-open: a dock click while an app is open switches to that app (Sep 2026)
Direct request, the literal wording: "it should just stack the windows", clarified as the same close-and-open every other app already does. `gui_launch_from_dock` keeps the desktop and dock visible around every app window, so to anyone looking, a click on another dock tile while an app is open means "open that one". Until now it only closed the current app (the "click anywhere closes" contract) and the tile had to be clicked a second time. Now: a new `gui_close_was_click` flag records whether the last input an app's wait loop handed out was a click or a key (set at the four sites that turn a mouse edge into an app-visible event: `gui_getch_or_click`, `get_key_or_click`, `gui_wait_close`, and Notes' own loop; cleared whenever a key is handed out instead). When an app returns, `gui_launch_from_dock` checks it: closed on a click that sits on a dock tile (`gui_dock_hit_test` at the pointer's real screen position) means open that tile's app in its place, from the pointer's real position, no second click. Esc never switches, only a click can name a tile. Implemented as a tail jump, not recursion, so hopping across the dock all day never grows the kernel stack. `gui_run` also picks the pointer up from where the app's own loop left it (`app_cursor_x/y`) instead of the stale launching-click position it used to redraw at.

Test: `tools/checks/appclose-check.py`'s scenario step now asserts the switch, not just the close: Notes open, click Terminal's tile, and the viewport centre must be Terminal's own `0x1A1512` with the window chrome present (Terminal is the target because its content colour tells it apart from Notes, the desktop, and every other app at one pixel). Proven discriminating per 4b: with the switch branch disabled (`if (0 && gui_close_was_click)`), rebuilt, the run reports "Notes closed, Terminal NOT opened" and FAILs; restored, "Terminal opened in its place", PASS. Full sweep still PASS (all 10 dock slots open and close, input intact after). `check.sh`, `apptest.sh`, `vmmouse-check.sh`, `dockhover-check.py`, and `mobiletest.mjs` against a locally served landing page with the synced `landing/v86/kernel.elf` all PASS. Headless throughout. MINOR bump (0.62.3 -> 0.63.0, rebased over the concurrent v68 / 0.62.3 restraint pass, which also touched `kernel.c`; the label moved from v68 to v69 for the same reason): a new capability, small but real, and deliberately shipped as its own version so the freeze fix (v67) and windowing work stay separate, per the request.

## Multi-window, honestly scoped: what exists, what this pass shipped, what real windows still need (Sep 2026)
Direct request: "real windows, multiple apps open at once, each in its own window, each independently closable via its own X, not a single current-app model." Read `drivers/window.c` before assuming anything. What it actually provides today: one render-target abstraction (`window_pixel`/`window_rect`/`window_clear` at logical resolution over an s-times physical mode), one clip viewport (`window_set_viewport`, which is how v40+'s dock-launched app window works: the app draws full-screen coordinates and the viewport clips them into the chrome's content rect), one offscreen "screen band" for partial repaints, and one redirectable supersample target for icons. It is a framebuffer wrapper, not a window system: no window list, no z-order, no per-window backing store, no focus, no input routing. The chrome (traffic lights, title) is drawn once by `gui_launch_from_dock` straight into the framebuffer. And every app in this kernel is a blocking function with its own input loop (`gui_wait_close`, `get_key_or_click`, Notes' own loop, Terminal's, Keyrate's) polling the one global keyboard ring and the one global mouse state, returning only when it's done.

What this pass shipped toward it: v67 (every app closes from its own X, from the pointer alone, tested per app) and v69 (the dock stays live under an open window and switches apps). Both are prerequisites any windowing system would need first, both real and tested, neither is multi-window.

What real multi-window needs, in order, each a real step, none of them small, the reason it was NOT rushed into this same pass (the request explicitly preferred honest scope over a half-working system):
- [ ] [Fable] **Window objects with backing stores in `drivers/window.c`.** A `struct window { x, y, w, h, title, z, focused, u32 *buf }` list, each window drawn into its OWN heap buffer, never straight into the framebuffer. Size is the real constraint: AA text is drawn at physical resolution (`window_pixel_phys`), so a backing store for today's 804x345 logical content rect at scale 2 is 1608x690x4 = 4.4MB; three windows is 13MB of a 32MB v86 guest, so either a smaller default window, logical-resolution stores with physical-res text re-rendered at compose time, or a per-window scale choice. Design call before code. Prior art with exactly this shape: ToaruOS's compositor (`yutani`, per-window shared-memory buffers composited in z-order), Haiku's `app_server`, and the OSDev wiki's "GUI" page; borrow the buffer-per-window + damage-rectangle model, don't invent one. **Phase 1 partial, v0.73.0 (Sep 2026)**: a real window list exists and two windows (Files, Weather) genuinely open/draw/close independently and simultaneously, see that entry below for the full real evidence -- but it lives in `kernel/kernel.c` next to the app-dispatch table it needs (not `drivers/window.c`, a deliberate deviation from this sketch, see that entry's reasoning), has no per-window backing store yet (both still draw straight into the shared framebuffer through the existing viewport clip, same as the old single-window model), and only 2 of the 18 apps are wired to it. **Phase 2, v0.74.0 (Sep 2026)**: real click-to-focus (clicking a visible background window raises it, `gui_multiwin_hit_test`) and real z-order compositing (`gui_multiwin_draw_all` and the hit test now share one list, so draw order and input agree) both shipped, see that entry below for the full real pixel evidence. The backing-store box above stays unchecked; that's still real, unstarted work for whichever future pass needs true per-window buffers (today's compositing is correct z-order over a shared framebuffer, not yet double-buffered/backing-stored), same for the 2-window cap and converting apps beyond Files/Weather off their blocking loop.
- [ ] [Fable] **A compositor in `gui_run`.** Desktop, then every window's store in z-order, then the pointer, with damage tracking so a keystroke in one window repaints that window's rect only. This kernel has no vsync and no double buffer (the v40/v45.1 tearing and flashing entries are exactly that pain), so the compositor also becomes the place a real back buffer finally lives; v41's `window_open_scaled` mode set is the only real cost model to test it against.
- [ ] [Fable] **Input routing by focus.** Today `kbd_pop`/`mouse_click_edge` are global and pulled by whichever loop is running. Multi-window needs a focused window (topmost under the last click), key events delivered only to it, click events hit-tested against the z-ordered list (chrome first: its X closes THAT window, its title bar drags it, then the content rect in that window's local coordinates). The v62 absolute pointer makes hit-testing trivial; the routing is the work.
- [ ] [Fable] **Apps stop blocking.** This is the big one and the honest reason it's multiple sessions, not one: 18 apps' loops have to become `open()` / `draw(win)` / `on_key(win, k)` / `on_click(win, x, y)` handlers over a per-app state struct so the compositor can interleave them, OR each app runs as its own kernel task (task.c already preempts, and v64 gave tasks a lifecycle) with the current viewport/target/AA-hook state in `window.c` made per-task and saved/restored on every context switch, plus per-task input queues the router fills. The handler conversion is the safer shape (no per-task draw-state hazard, no stack-per-window memory) and it staggers cleanly: the nine read-only `gui_launch_html` viewers plus Weather/Files/About share `gui_wait_close` and convert as one; Reminders/Trash/Settings/Calendar/Mail share the `sleep_ticks` + `get_key_or_click` list shape and convert as a second batch; Notes, Terminal, Keyrate, Chat each have a real bespoke loop and convert last, one at a time, each behind `appclose-check.py` extended to open two windows and close each from its own X.
- [ ] [Haiku] Once that exists, the two currently-unlit traffic lights (`gui_draw_app_titlebar`'s own comment says why they're unlit today: no windowing state to minimize or maximize into) become real, and the chrome's duplicate in-viewport title goes away.

Estimate, active work: 3-5 sessions, in that order, each shippable on its own (backing stores + compositor first can land behind today's one-window behaviour with zero visible change, a real test being "the framebuffer is byte-identical to before"). Not started here. The freeze bug (v67) is fixed and shipped independently of all of this, as requested.

## Session task queue, intelligently ordered (Sep 2026)
Real queue, reordered by actual priority/risk/dependency rather than the order requests arrived in, persisted here so it survives past any one session's context. Refreshed at the end of a long session that shipped v56 through v72.
