Wind the session down around 90% usage rather than pushing to the limit, a big task started too late just gets killed mid-flight and leaves a half-finished worktree.

Real, honest null result, spot-checked and confirmed by the managing session (not just trusted): `ne2k_clamp_len`/`rtl8139_clamp_len` both real and correctly guarded, `tcp_match`'s `ip_total` bound at `drivers/net.c:462` real and correct. This queue entry was just never marked done.

This queue entry was stale.

List view: up/down select, enter details, esc closes. `stockstest` regression test per 4b, docs updated per 4c.
9. ~~**New, real find from the v73 QA pass**: `reaptest` run cold page-faults at `0x00400008`.~~ Root-caused and fixed in v82/0.68.2: `PAGING_PRIVATE_PDE` (2) collided with the general-purpose extra-mapping pool once kheap grew past 8MB, so a new task's page directory could inherit and then destroy a mapping at that PDE. Moved to PDE 19, one past the pool's real reachable range (`BASE_MAP_TABLES` + `MAX_EXTRA_TABLES` = 18). Re-confirmed still fixed this session (a Haiku agent re-investigated this exact item independently, found the v82 fix already in place and working, made no changes -- an honest null result, not re-litigated).

10. ~~**New, real find from the v74 pass**: `regress.sh`'s heap/task checks fail after `gui_run()` exits.~~ Root-caused and fixed in v0.76.3: `paging_map_region()` handed out page-table slots for large mappings (the GUI framebuffer among them) but `window_close()` never freed the slot back to the pool, only the page-directory entry, so a few GUI open/close laps exhausted all 16 slots and starved further heap/task growth. Fixed with a real free-list (`paging_unmap_region()`, slot reuse in `paging_map_region()`, `window_close()`/`gui_run()` cleanup calls); see that entry below for full evidence.

Re-order honestly if a direct request jumps the queue, this isn't a fixed contract.

## Real direction, not a roadmap item yet: free OS, monetize custom hardware (Sep 2026)
Direct statement from Joshua on the "what's the business plan" question: the OS stays free, monetization is custom hardware built to run it. Real, coherent shape (same model a lot of hobbyist single-board-computer kits use), worth recording accurately since it reframes what "real hardware" already means elsewhere in this file, not a new, separate idea.

Honest technical gap this implies, checked against what's actually built: everything this kernel currently drives is QEMU's emulated hardware specifically, not a generic PC target. `rtl8139.c` speaks the emulated Realtek NIC QEMU presents, `vmmouse.c` speaks a VMware-specific backdoor port real hardware doesn't have, `vbe.c` speaks QEMU's Bochs VBE interface. None of these map directly to a real board's real chipsets without real driver work, the same class of gap the WiFi note above already named ("driver-level WiFi support only becomes meaningful once this kernel targets real hardware with a real 802.11 card... a genuinely large project on its own"). A custom-hardware business direction means that real-hardware port stops being a someday-idea and becomes the actual target for future driver work, worth keeping in mind when scoping future audio/network/input work: build it portable to a real chipset where realistic, not only against QEMU's emulated one, even while QEMU stays the day-to-day dev target.

Not a coding task, not queued as a task, a real direction recorded honestly for future prioritization calls.

## Real direction, not a roadmap item yet: what 1.0.0 would actually mean here (Sep 2026)

Direct question: now that VERSION is well past 0.75.0, what does reaching 1.0.0 actually require, not just "a bigger number"? Answered from this file's own semver rule, not invented fresh: "MAJOR only for a real breaking change (none yet; this kernel has no external callers to break)." That line is the real criterion. Semver's 1.0.0 signals a stable public contract exists, one an outside consumer can rely on without expecting it to shift underneath them. Today there is no such consumer: every app, the GUI, and the kernel are one compiled binary, nothing outside this repo depends on any fixed interface this kernel exposes, so there is nothing yet for a MAJOR bump to protect and nothing for a 1.0.0 to honestly promise.

The real, concrete trigger already sitting in this file: a syscall ABI + ring-3 user-mode integration (named in v28's own deferred note and the "Later product ideas" section, tagged `[Fable]` from the start -- "exactly privilege isolation plus an exact calling convention, the two things this legend already names"). That is the first point in this project's own arc where a genuine external caller becomes possible: a real ring-3 program, written once against a fixed syscall interface, that this kernel would need to keep working across future versions without a MAJOR bump. Before that exists, "don't break user programs" has no user programs to break. After it ships and gets even one real reference program running against it, 1.0.0 has an honest, checkable meaning for the first time: the syscall ABI is the stable contract, and MAJOR is reserved for actually breaking it.

This is a real technical answer to "what would 1.0.0 require," not a decision to ship it now or on any particular timeline -- whether/when to actually call something 1.0.0 once that contract exists is a real product/vision call, the same `[Joshua]` category this file already reserves for the "what is a Joshua Tree user account protecting against" question and the free-OS/custom-hardware direction above, not something to declare unilaterally from an architecture note. Recorded here so a future pass doesn't re-litigate this from scratch or, worse, bump MAJOR/1.0.0 on version-number momentum alone with no real contract behind it.

So: the "switch wallpapers in Settings" mechanism is real and buildable now (a style/source enum persisted the same way wind/dock-scale already are). The "location-dynamic satellite image" half is blocked on exactly task #9's prerequisite chain (image decoder) plus a real geolocation source and a real HTTPS-capable tile/satellite provider, not separate new scope. Also recorded verbatim, not yet understood well enough to scope honestly: "eventually we can make the wallpaper epiphany", noted here as stated rather than guessed at, revisit with Joshua directly before acting on it.

## Real find: IP-based geolocation works over plain HTTP, no TLS needed (Sep 2026)
Direct follow-up after Joshua asked about real GPS. Honest correction first: this kernel runs inside QEMU, which has no GPS hardware at all, emulated or real, a GNSS receiver is a real, separate piece of hardware even a future real-hardware target would need, not something "real hardware" alone unlocks. GPS is not the realistic path here.

IP-based geolocation is, and it's now confirmed real, not just theorized: `curl http://ip-api.com/json/` answers `200` directly on plain HTTP (no forced HTTPS redirect, unlike every stock/satellite-tile source checked earlier in this file) with a real JSON body, genuine city/region/lat/lon for whatever network connection is asking. This is the same shape as `weather_fetch`'s existing `http_get` call, a real, small, buildable addition.

This directly unblocks two things already logged as gapped: Weather's hardcoded `lat=49.28,lon=-123.12` constant becomes real, and one of the three prerequisites the satellite-wallpaper idea named (real geolocation) is now solved, leaving only the image-decoder and a real HTTPS-capable tile/satellite source still blocked. Shipped in v71 / 0.65.0, see the entry below.

## v71 / 0.65.0, two live bugs Joshua saw on the running desktop, plus the third one underneath them (Sep 2026)
Direct request, both reported from the real running app, not from a test. Bug 1: the menu bar said drizzle while Joshua's phone, in Langley, said 18 and cloudy with no rain. Bug 2: a dashed white bar across the full width of the screen, sitting exactly on the wallpaper's skyline, on a plain boot-and-idle desktop. They turned out to be linked, and fixing them uncovered a third real bug in the NIC driver that had been hiding since v7.

**Bug 1, location.** `weather_fetch` hardcoded `latitude=49.28&longitude=-123.12`, downtown Vancouver, in its Open-Meteo URL. Open-Meteo was never wrong, WMO 51 (light drizzle) really was happening at those coordinates; the coordinates were the bug. Now `geo_fetch` asks `ip-api.com/json/` over plain HTTP once per boot (the "Real find" entry above already confirmed it answers without TLS), pulls `lat`/`lon` via a new `json_extract_number_text` in `drivers/json.c` (copies the number's exact text, `49.0983`, no float parse and format round trip in a freestanding build with no float support to lose digits in) plus `city` via the existing string grabber, and `weather_fetch` splices that text straight into the URL. No fallback constant on purpose: no real location means no weather fetch, the same "nothing fabricated" contract v56/v60/v65 already hold for a NIC-less boot, retried on the next ten-minute cycle. The v56 dropdown's location line now shows the looked-up city and the exact coordinates the URL was built from, one extra row when a city came back. Day/night tint, wind amplitude and the particle overlay all key off the same fetch, so they are location-accurate for free.

Proof it is really dynamic, headless, over real QEMU user networking: `weather_fetch` mirrors `geo=`, `wxurl=` and `wx=` to serial, and the new `tools/checks/geo-check.sh` boots with `-net nic,model=rtl8139 -net user`, reads those lines, and compares them against the host's own `curl http://ip-api.com/json/` answer fetched in the same run. Real values from the verification boot: kernel `geo=49.0983,-122.6498` (Langley), host ip-api `49.0983,-122.6498`, kernel URL `/v1/forecast?latitude=49.0983&longitude=-122.6498&current=temperature_2m,weather_code`, kernel weather text `18° Cloudy`, the same 18 and Cloudy Joshua's phone showed. Discriminating: with `geo_fetch` temporarily forced back to the old `49.28`/`-123.12` constant the script failed on the location compare (`kernel geo: 49.28,-123.12` vs host `49.0983,-122.6498`); restored, PASS. The offline parser half is `geotest`, a new shell command: a fixed ip-api-shaped body with the traps in it (`"latitude"` and `"longitude"` keys earlier in the text that `"lat"`/`"lon"` must not match inside, a negative longitude, a string-valued key that must be rejected, a missing key), asserting byte-exact extraction.

**Bug 2, the dashed horizon bar, root-caused with real pixels, not guessed.** Reproduced headlessly first: a temporary fake Rain reading (`weather_code10 = 610; weather_have = 1`) after `settings_load`, boot `-display none`, ten idle seconds, `pmemsave` the real `0xfd000000` framebuffer, scan for the exact rain-streak colour `0x00C9C0B4`: 314 streak pixels on physical rows 790..804 (logical 395..402, right below `WIND_HORIZON_ROW` 395), only 12 above it, on even rows only, a dashed line across the whole width when cropped and looked at. The cause is in v65's `weather_fx_tick_kind`: each particle was advanced (`p->y += speed`, 4..8) and DRAWN, and only then checked against `WIND_HORIZON_ROW` and wrapped to the top. So a raindrop at row 394 got stamped at 395..402, below the horizon, in rows the wind tick never repaints (the sway band repaint is what erases last frame's particles, and it stops at the horizon by design). Every streak that crossed the horizon left a permanent four-pixel diagonal at a random x, accumulating into exactly the bar Joshua saw. Snow leaked one row the same way. It only shows when the overlay is on, which is why bug 1's phantom drizzle is what made bug 2 visible. Fix: the wrap check runs before the draw, and every particle pixel goes through `weather_fx_plot`, which clips to `WIND_TOP_ROW*sc..WIND_HORIZON_ROW*sc` and the window width, enforcing per pixel the contract the v65 entry only stated. Re-ran the same dump with the fix: 0 streak pixels below the horizon, the same 12 live ones above.

Permanent test per CLAUDE.md's 4b: `weatherfxcliptest` opens the desktop's own 960x540 scale-2 window, clears it flat, parks every particle at row 394 with speed 8, ticks Rain once and Snow once, and scans physical rows from the horizon down for any non-flat pixel, also asserting real particle pixels landed inside the band so a fix that simply stopped drawing would fail too. Discriminating, proven both ways in the same boot-time harness (`kmain` direct calls plus a temporary `putc` serial mirror, the v66 pattern, fully reverted): with the exact pre-v71 particle code restored, `216` pixels leaked below `WIND_HORIZON_ROW`, FAILED; with the fix, `0`, ok.

**Bug 3, found underneath bug 1: `rtl8139_receive` ran one packet behind after any burst.** The first headless `geo-check.sh` run resolved the location fine and then the Open-Meteo call returned -1. Pristine HEAD with a single fetch landed 370 bytes, so it was the second connection on one NIC init that failed, and temporary stage logging pinned it to the second `dns_resolve` timing out. `rtl8139_receive` gated on ISR bit 0 (ROK) and write-1-cleared it after reading one packet. ROK is a "something arrived" latch, not a ring-occupancy flag: when two frames land between polls (SYN-ACK plus data, or the server's FIN after `tcp_get`'s best-effort close that never waits for it), the second sits in the ring with ROK already clear and only gets read when a third frame sets ROK again. The second DNS reply arrived, the poll handed back the previous server's stale FIN instead, cleared ROK, and the real reply sat unread until the timeout. Every earlier caller (`web`, `weathertest`, `chat`, the old single-fetch `weather_fetch`) called `rtl8139_init` right before its one connection, resetting the ring and hiding this for sixty-odd versions. Fix borrowed from prior art per CLAUDE.md's step 3, not invented: gate on the Command Register's BUFE bit (bit 0, RX buffer empty), which reflects the real ring state, exactly the OSDev RTL8139 page's receive loop and Linux `rtl8139_rx`'s `while ((RTL_R8(ChipCmd) & RxBufEmpty) == 0)`. ROK is still cleared so the ISR doesn't stay latched. With the BUFE gate both back-to-back lookups landed (`wxn=366`, `wx=18° Cloudy`); `geo-check.sh` is the permanent regression test for this too, its `wx=` assertion needs both connections to land and fails on the old ROK gate.

**Real find, not fixed here, logged honestly:** running the whole shell suite through the same boot-time harness showed three tests already FAILED on pristine `v70` HEAD, before this pass touched anything, order-independent (they fail run first, too): `weatherpaneltest`'s two panel checks (likely v68's switch to `gui_rounded_rect_on_wallpaper` with `GUI_FLYOUT_RADIUS`, its corner pixel and `(x0+4, y0+4)` probes predate the rounded chrome), `dockstyletest`'s "dock tray top edge" check (single-row seam reported), and `calctest` (`10/2 failed: got 0`, v70's Calculator). Each needs its own root-cause pass with a discrimination proof, not a drive-by edit inside a weather fix; left open for the next pass. Everything else in the suite was green in the same boot: `geotest`, `weatherfxcliptest`, `weatherfxtest`, `weathertest`, `daynighttest`, `windweathertest`, `cursortest`, `dockstyletest`'s shadow check, `mailtest`, `heaptest`, `contactstest`.

Verified for real: `make kernel.elf` clean (only the two pre-existing warnings, `gui_draw_hello_script` unused and v70's `contactstest` `pass`), `./check.sh` PASS, `./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean, `tools/checks/dockhover-check.py` PASS, `tools/checks/vmmouse-check.sh` PASS, `tools/checks/geo-check.sh` PASS with the real Langley coordinates above. Headless the entire pass, no `-display cocoa` window opened (the main session owns the one visible window). `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s `json.c`, `rtl8139.c` and Weather rows updated in the same pass (4c). VERSION 0.64.0 -> 0.65.0 (MINOR: real geolocation is a new capability; the two fixes ride with it), tagged `jt-v71`.

## v73 / 0.65.2, the three pristine-v70 test failures, root-caused and fixed (Sep 2026)
Bounded QA pass, not open-ended bug hunting: v71's entry above logged three tests failing on pristine v70 HEAD, order-independent, never root-caused. All three now have real root causes, real fixes (or, for two of them, a real fix to the test itself once the production code turned out correct), and a real discrimination proof for each (temporarily broke the fix, confirmed the original failure text reproduced byte-for-byte, restored, confirmed green again).

**`calctest`, real bug, real fix.** `10/2 failed: got 0` traced to `kernel/calculator.h`'s `calc_unary`: it peeks one token ahead with `calc_next(lex)` to check for a unary minus, and on a miss rewound with a flat `lex->pos--`, undoing exactly one character. That's correct by accident for every 1-character token (a single digit, or any operator/paren), which is why "2+3", "2+3*4", "(2+3)*4" and "-2+3" (every number in them 1 digit) all passed and shipped clean in v70. It's wrong for a multi-character token: "10/2" tokenizes "10" as `NUM` with `num_val=10` and `lex->pos` left at 2 (just past it); `pos--` only rewinds to 1, so `calc_primary`'s re-lex starts mid-token at the lone `'0'` and reads `NUM 0` instead of `NUM 10`. `0 / 2` evaluates to exactly 0, the reported bug. Real fix: save the lexer's position before peeking and restore that exact saved value instead of assuming a 1-character step, so the rewind is correct regardless of the peeked token's width. `calc_term` and `calc_expr` had the identical peek-then-`pos--` shape for their own `+`/`-`/`*`/`/` lookaheads (not exercised by today's failure, since every boundary token there is a 1-char operator or `TOK_END`, but the same latent bug), fixed the same way for real correctness rather than leaving a second copy of a bug already found once. Discrimination proof: reverted `calc_unary`'s rewind back to the flat `pos--` alone, rebuilt clean, booted headless, `calctest` printed the exact original failure text (`10/2 failed: got 0`, `FAILED`); restored the save/restore fix, rebuilt, `calculator parser: ok`.

**`weatherpaneltest`, both checks: no production bug, a stale test.** Root-caused with a real DEBUG serial dump of the actual pixel values read back (not guessed): `gui_draw_weather_panel` was painting its chrome correctly the whole time. Two separate reasons both checks read back the pre-draw clear color instead of the panel's real fill:
(1) the "no data" check's `(x0 + 4, y0 + 4)` corner probe predates v66's `GUI_FLYOUT_RADIUS = 14` rounding of this panel. At radius 14 with `gui_rounded_rect_on_wallpaper`'s 3px AA band, the point 4px in from each edge sits at distance `sqrt(200) ≈ 14.14` from the corner arc's centre `(14,14)`, past the 14px radius, so the draw function's own `continue` (documented: "outside: leave the wallpaper alone") never touches it. Confirmed directly: the probe read back `0x00111111`, this test's own pre-draw clear color, untouched.
(2) the "live data" check's `(x0, y0)` probe looked for a `border = 0x001C1C1E` color that v66's flyout-chrome unification deliberately removed from this exact panel (see `GUI_FLYOUT_RADIUS`'s own comment: these three flyouts "were the one place still... with a manually drawn 1px border on all four sides... zero separate stroke line" is the stated point of that pass). There has been no border to find at any pixel of this panel since v66, `(x0, y0)` included; that corner is also outside the radius per (1) regardless.
Real fix landed in the test, not the panel: both probes moved to a genuine interior point clear of the corner radius and the top-edge AA band (`x0+40, y0+20` / `x0+40, y0+40`), and the live-data check now verifies real chrome fill instead of a border that was never coming back. Discrimination proof: restored the exact old `(x0+4,y0+4)`/`(x0,y0)`/`border` logic, rebuilt, booted headless, got the exact original failure text (`weather panel (no data): FAILED`, `weather panel (live data): FAILED`); restored the fixed probes, rebuilt, both read `ok`.

**`dockstyletest` "top edge", real bug in the test's own assertion, not the draw code.** Root-caused with a real DEBUG dump of the actual channel values: `bg_before=0x170b06`, `row0=0x170b06` (equal), `row1=0x5f5650`, `row3=DOCK_TRAY_COLOR (0xefebe4)`. The failing assertion was `row0 != bg_before`. At `py=0` (the tray's very top physical row), `gui_rounded_rect_on_wallpaper`'s own edge-blend math has `t = band - py = band` exactly, and `gui_lerp(a, b, t, max)` returns `b` outright when `t == max` -- row0 is, by design, 100% the real backdrop color at that exact pixel (`gui_wallpaper_sample`), the softest point of the AA fringe before it solidifies to full fill by row `band`. `row1 = 0x5f5650` matches `gui_lerp(DOCK_TRAY_COLOR, 0x170b06, 2, 3)` exactly, computed by hand per channel, confirming the blend math is correct. `row0 == bg_before` isn't a seam bug; it's the expected outcome of a correct edge blend landing on a wallpaper gradient that happens to be locally flat over the 2 physical rows this check compares -- asserting they must differ fails exactly when the fix is working, at whatever screen position the gradient happens to be flattest. The two assertions that actually encode the real v61 regression (a hard, unblended top row) are `row0 != DOCK_TRAY_COLOR` and `row1 != row0`: reverting v61's fix makes every non-corner edge pixel `col = color` unconditionally, so row0 AND row1 both come back as the raw tray color, failing both independently of the `bg_before` comparison. Real fix: dropped the incorrect `row0 != bg_before` condition, kept the two that actually discriminate. Discrimination proof: restored the `bg_before` sample and the four-way `&&`, rebuilt, booted headless, `dockstyletest` printed the exact original failure text (`dock tray top edge: FAILED (single-row seam)`); removed it again, rebuilt, `ok`.

**Real, new, honestly out-of-scope find surfaced by this pass's own verification method, not fixed here:** running the full non-interactive shell-test list back-to-back from `kmain` (the same boot-time direct-call trick used throughout this pass) exhausted the fixed task table partway through (`preempttest`, `reaptest`, `ring3test`, `killtest` each reported "no free task slots" or failed when chained immediately after other task-spawning tests in one boot with no cleanup between them, which isn't how they're normally invoked). Isolating `reaptest` alone, called from `kmain` before `gui_run()`/the shell loop starts (zero prior scheduler activity, unlike a real interactive session that's been idling and ticking for a while first), produced a real ring-0 page-fault at `0x00400008` rather than a clean pass or fail. This is genuinely new information, not a known regression from anything touched in this pass (no task/scheduler file was edited here), and not one of the three named pristine-v70 failures this pass was scoped to. Logged honestly in the queue above rather than chased down inside a QA pass already at its real scope boundary.

Verified for real: `make kernel.elf` clean (same two pre-existing warnings as v71/v72, `gui_draw_hello_script` unused and `rtl8139.c`'s unused `inw`), `./check.sh` PASS, `./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean (219 references). Full non-interactive regression list re-run headless in one boot after the fixes (order matters for the task-table finding above, so grouped by kind): `heaptest`, `heapgrow`, `weathertest`, `daynighttest`, `weatherfxtest`, `weatherfxcliptest`, `geotest`, `weatherpaneltest`, `cursortest`, `mailtest`, `dockstyletest`, `contactstest`, `calctest` all green in the same run as the three fixed tests, confirming no regression anywhere else in the suite. Headless the entire pass, no `-display cocoa` window opened (the main session owns the one visible window). VERSION 0.65.1 -> 0.65.2 (PATCH: bug fixes only, no new capability), tagged `jt-v73`.

## Landing demo: idle-tour QA and an honest sequential rework (Sep 2026)
Direct request, two parts: heavy QA on the live demo's app-to-app switching specifically (a different, JS-side question from v67/v69's native app-close/dock-switch fixes), then rework the idle tour into the honest, buildable "show off every app" shape, sequential open/use/close/next rather than a fake simultaneous multi-window this kernel doesn't have (confirmed again against the "Multi-window, honestly scoped" entry above: real window stacking is still 3-5 sessions of unstarted work, v67/v69 only shipped the two prerequisites). Pure `landing/embed.js` change, `kernel.c` untouched, no `VERSION` bump.

**Part 1, QA, two real bugs found, both root-caused with evidence, neither previously caught.** Built a throwaway watcher (the `mobiletest.mjs` shape: real headless Playwright, no injected interaction) and let the real deployed idle tour run unattended for 150s, about 3 full loops, screenshotting every real state change. Both bugs escaped every prior pass because verification always drove the FOCUSED, real-visitor path (`mobiletest.mjs`'s own taps); nothing had ever watched the unattended autoplay run to completion and checked what actually rendered mid-dwell.

1. **Every close click landed on the dock tile that had just opened the app**, since the tour never moved the cursor between opening and closing an app. That was harmless before v69; since v69 shipped (a click on a dock tile while an app is open switches straight to that tile's app instead of returning to a neutral desktop), the SAME close click now instantly reopened the app just closed, and the tour only really advanced once the next step's own open click landed on the following app's tile and got read as ITS close. Confirmed live: every transition showed a real ~1.9s flash-reopen of the app being left (e.g. Files -> Terminal took four separate screen states across ~7.5s instead of two). Harmless in that it never got stuck, but doubled the real work every switch did and put a visible glitch in the demo.
2. **Deeper, and pre-dating this pass** (real since v51, when typed-text tour steps were first added, not introduced by this rework): `libv86.js` gates `keyboard_send_text`/`simulate_char` behind `keyboard_adapter.emu_enabled`, the same flag real DOM `keydown` respects (`g()` calls `b(t)`, and `b(t)` returns `false` whenever `!x.emu_enabled`, read straight out of the vendored file). `embed.js` already force-enables `mouse_adapter.emu_enabled` for the tour (since v44) but never did the keyboard equivalent, so **every scripted keystroke the idle tour has ever sent while unfocused has been silently swallowed at the source**, since v51. Invisible until now because Terminal's static "Joshua Tree terminal. Type help." banner prints unconditionally on open (real content, but not proof any typed input landed) and no prior pass ever screenshotted mid-dwell during a genuinely unattended run. Confirmed two ways: a debug-instrumented run showed `keyboard_send_text` resolving with no error on every call while the on-screen app never changed at all; the identical call, sent instead through a real focus tap first (`focused=true`, the path a real visitor takes), worked immediately. Fixed by also setting `keyboard_adapter.emu_enabled = true` alongside the existing mouse line.

**Part 2, the rework.** The tour now sequentially cycles the 8 real apps actually pinned to the dock (`GUI_DOCK_DEFAULT` in `kernel.c`: Files, Mail, Calendar, Notes, Reminders, Terminal, Chat, Weather), each opened by a real dock click, driven for a real ~15s dwell, closed via a real click on its own window's fixed close-button position (`(94,56)`, the same LOGICAL coordinate `mobiletest.mjs` already taps for its Notes-close check, identical for every dock-launched app since that rect never depends on which icon opened it), then the next app opens; after Weather it restarts from Files. Closing via that fixed position instead of the dock tile is also the real fix for bug #1 above, not a separate patch: `(94,56)` is never inside `gui_dock_hit_test`'s own rect, so the close click genuinely reaches open desktop every time and v69's tail-jump never fires during the tour.

Every app in this kernel closes on ANY click while open, confirmed per app in `kernel.c`/`mail.h`/`calendar.h`/`reminders.h`, so real interaction during the dwell is keyboard-only (a click would close the app instantly): Terminal runs `help` then `uptime`; Notes and Chat type real sentences (unchanged since v51, now actually landing per the fix above); Mail composes a real 3rd message through its own `c` + three Enter-confirmed fields; Calendar steps two real months forward and moves the day cursor (`d`/`]`/Enter), the honest substitute for the literal "click a Calendar day" the request suggested: there is no per-cell mouse hit-test in `gui_launch_calendar`, only keys move the day cursor, and a click there closes Calendar same as everywhere else, confirmed by reading the loop directly; Reminders adds a real item through its own `a` + Enter flow. Files and Weather have no interactive affordance in this kernel beyond `gui_wait_close`, so they get a real dwell with nothing scripted, honest rather than faked.

Deliberately out of the tour: the other 11 real apps (Contacts, Calculator, and 9 ported fleet-app viewers) live behind the Apps-folder launchpad tile, and `gui_launch_apps()` opens them with `gui_launch()` directly, not `gui_launch_from_dock`, so they run full-screen with no window chrome and no closable X at all, confirmed by reading `kernel.c`. Scripting a close click for a button that doesn't exist would misrepresent what this kernel can do, so those 11 stay out rather than getting a fake teardown.

**Standing QA (CLAUDE.md's 4b).** New `tools/checks/tourinput-check.mjs`: watches the real, unmodified idle tour open Mail on its own (no tap, no focus) and asserts a real 3rd list row of text pixels appears once the compose script has had time to run, the one visible signature only a landed keystroke can produce. Proven discriminating, not just "prints ok": reverted the `keyboard_adapter.emu_enabled` line, reran, got the exact predicted failure (`row2 (only real if compose landed)=0`, FAIL); restored it, reran, `row2=2424` vs `row1=1628`, PASS.

Verified for real, headless throughout (no `-display cocoa` window opened, the worktree-agent standing policy): `node --check landing/v86/embed.js`; `mobiletest.mjs` against a locally served copy of the landing page (existing `landing/v86/kernel.elf`, untouched since `kernel.c` wasn't) still PASS, confirming no regression to the real focused-visitor path; a full 200s/~2-loop unattended watch (the same throwaway shape as Part 1's QA) showed all 8 apps opening, running their real script, and closing cleanly every cycle, dwell times landing consistently at ~15.0-15.2s per app, no JS errors, `mouseOn`/`focused` state healthy the entire run. Rebased onto `origin/main` at `b71c9b3` (v73/0.65.2, no overlap: that pass only touched `kernel/calculator.h` and three test files) before push.

## v78 / 0.67.2, a Jony Ive restraint pass: the sibling of v77's spacing bug, found by real screenshot, not assumed (Sep 2026)
Direct follow-up request: since v68's restraint pass, three things landed (v75's busier real map wallpaper, three new apps -- Stocks/Contacts/Calculator, v77's font-advance fix at 4 sites). Asked to re-ground in a real fresh screenshot and look for real removal/unification opportunities across all three, not add decoration.

**Real screenshot evidence first**, per CLAUDE.md's 4b standard, headless (worktree-agent policy: never pop a live QEMU window). Two throwaway "restraint-screenshot"/"restraint-mapshot" scripts (the dockhover-check.py QMP + `pmemsave` shape, deleted again once done, not left in the tree) dumped the real desktop twice: once cold-boot (baked-photo wallpaper, calmer sunset silhouette, the default until the map fetch lands), once with `-net user` after the real `wall=` serial line confirmed the OpenTopoMap fetch landed (real Langley/Brookwood map tiles, roads, contours, the "Langley Township"/"Langley City" labels visible).

**1. Busier map wallpaper vs. chrome legibility: real finding, no fix needed.** Read `gui_draw_menubar` and the dock/panel draw paths directly, then confirmed against the real map screenshot. The menu bar is v48's "Liquid Glass" trick, a per-row blend of the wallpaper color toward white at a fixed 7/10 ratio (`gui_lerp(gui_wallpaper_color(row), 0x00FFFFFF, 7, 10)`), already mostly-white by design specifically so the wallpaper *underneath* it can get busier without the text ever losing contrast; the real screenshot confirms it, "16° Cloudy" and the clock read cleanly over the map same as over the photo. The dock tray and every flyout panel (`DOCK_TRAY_COLOR`, `gui_rounded_rect_on_wallpaper`'s `bg` fills) are fully opaque solid colors, not blended with the wallpaper at all beyond the already-tested edge AA band from v58/v61/v73's own dockstyletest coverage, so a busier backdrop can't touch their legibility by construction. No real scrim/contrast bug. The bigger, real "Liquid Glass" ask (translucent chrome, not just a white-heavy blend) is already logged in the entry above this one ("Real follow-up requests on the map wallpaper") as genuine design work needing real visual iteration, correctly out of scope for a blind restraint pass, not re-litigated here.

**2. Stocks/Contacts/Calculator window chrome: real finding, already unified, nothing to fix.** Grepped all three (`kernel/stocks.h`, `kernel/contacts.h`, `kernel/calculator.h`) for their own draw code against the shared helper every other full-screen app (Notes, Mail, Calendar, Reminders, Files, Terminal, Chat, Weather, Trash, Settings, About) uses: all three call `gui_draw_app_titlebar(...)` for their real macOS-traffic-light title bar, the one shared helper, no bespoke border/shadow/corner-radius code anywhere in any of the three files (`window_rect` calls inside them are plain flat list-row/input-box fills, not window chrome). Confirmed by reading `gui_draw_app_titlebar` itself (`kernel.c:2952`) and its callers list: 20 call sites across 9 files, Stocks/Contacts/Calculator among them, none diverged. Nothing to unify, already correctly reusing the v66-era shared helper from the moment they shipped (v70/v71/v76).

**3. Real leftover fixed-8px-cell bug, found and fixed.** Grepped every `strlen(...)` in the tree (one call site total, the implementation itself in `lib/libc.c`) and every `* 8`/`*8` used near text layout: v77's four fixed sites (clock, weather text + hit-box, dock hover label, launchpad label) are clean, but `render_wrapped_text` (`kernel.c`, used by every ported fleet-app HTML viewer -- Curbfind, Bookrank, Quotestreak, Plan, Lexly, Toroid, Sparkjar, Homeqi, Fieldbook -- and by Chat's AI answers) still drew every glyph through the single-glyph `font_draw_char` API at a hardcoded 8px advance and measured word-wrap width as `wlen * 8`, exactly v77's bug shape, just unreachable through the four sites v77 actually audited. Confirmed on a real framebuffer dump: launched the real Curbfind app view (a boot-time direct-call probe into `gui_launch_html`, the CLAUDE.md-documented mechanism, reverted before commit) and pmemsaved the screen -- "Curbfind" rendered as "**Curbf ind**", and the full description sentence came out "Br owse Cr ai gsl i st l i st i ngs wi t hout t he cl ut t er..." once AA was actually wired up (the probe initially missed calling `font_set_aa`, the setup `gui_run()` normally does, which silently fell back to the always-correct bitmap font and produced a false negative worth recording honestly rather than hiding: the first probe run showed byte-identical before/after screenshots, a `diff bbox: None`, until `font_set_aa(gui_aa_char, gui_aa_advance)` was added to match what `gui_run()` really does).

Fixed the same way v77 fixed the other four: `render_wrapped_text` now measures each word with `font_string_width` (copied into a bounded local buffer, `WORD_MAX=255`) and draws it in one `font_draw_string` call, a real proportional pen, instead of `wlen*8`/`font_draw_char`-in-a-loop; space width comes from `font_string_width(" ")` instead of a flat 8. Word-longer-than-255 chars still draws (no cap on `font_draw_string` itself), it just can't be measured past that bound for the wrap decision, an honest limit the old code shared (it never measured unbounded words either).

**Discriminating regression test, per 4b.** New shell command `wraptest` (the exact `texttest` shape): renders "Curbfind" through the real `render_wrapped_text` path at scale 2, measures ink-column runs via `window_get_pixel_phys`, asserts 8 separate letter-runs with a tight gap spread. Proven both directions in the same session: with the fix, 8 runs, spread 3px (min 1, max 4), "spacing uniform: ok"; with `render_wrapped_text` temporarily reverted to the flat `wlen*8`/`font_draw_char` version, 8 runs but spread 11px (min 2, max 13), "spacing uneven (the 'Curbf ind' bug): FAILED" -- restored, green again. `tools/checks/textspacing-check.sh` (v77's own script) now also types `wraptest` and demands its "spacing uniform: ok" line alongside `texttest`'s two, so both bugs stay covered by the same CI step (`.github/workflows/check.yml`, 4d) going forward.

**Verified for real.** `make clean && make` clean (same two pre-existing warnings, `gui_draw_hello_script`/`gui_icon_stocks` unused). `./check.sh` PASS. `./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean (291 references). `./tools/checks/textspacing-check.sh` PASS (both `texttest` and `wraptest` green). `tools/checks/dockhover-check.py` PASS, `tools/checks/vmmouse-check.sh` PASS (both untouched by this pass, confirmed no regression). `heaptest`, `tasktest`, `calctest`, `contactstest`, `mailtest` all still `ok` via `tools/shell-run.sh`. Headless the entire pass, no `-display cocoa` window opened, the worktree-agent standing policy. `landing/v86/kernel.elf` resynced since this changes what the live embed renders for Curbfind/Bookrank/Quotestreak/Plan/Lexly/Toroid/Sparkjar/Homeqi/Fieldbook and Chat. Both throwaway probe scripts used to get the real evidence above were deleted before commit, not left as permanent tooling since `dockhover-check.py`'s QMP+pmemsave shape already covers the technique and neither probe is discriminating on its own (they're screenshot tools, not assertions). VERSION 0.67.1 -> 0.67.2 (PATCH: one real bug fix, no new capability; areas 1 and 2 were real findings of "already fine," not changes).

## v77 / 0.67.1, "Cl oudy": the menu bar's letter-spacing bug, root-caused from the glyph table (Sep 2026)
Joshua's macro photo of the live menu bar showed "Cloudy" as "Cl oudy", a fat gap between the l and the o and none between the C and the l. It was never intermittent or per-render; it only looked that way because it depends on which letter came before the gap. Root cause, read straight out of `drivers/editor_fonts.h`'s DejaVu Sans 24px metrics, not guessed: since v44 every GUI string went through `font_draw_char` one glyph at a time at a fixed 8-logical (16-physical) px advance, and `gui_aa_char` left-aligned each proportional glyph inside that 16px cell and never read the table's `advance` field. `C` advances 17 (wider than the cell, clipped at 16, so its right edge is chopped and it butts straight against whatever follows), `l` advances 7 (leaving 8 dead physical px before the next cell), `o` 15, `u` 15, `d` 15, `y` 14. So the gap after every narrow glyph (`l`, `i`, `j`, `t`, `f`, `'`, `.`) was ~8px and the gap after a wide one was zero or negative: exactly an extra gap only between some pairs. Same defect silently chopped `W` (advance 24) and `m` (23) by 8-9px in every label that had one.

Fix, the standard shape every proportional renderer since QuickDraw's `DrawString` uses: `font_draw_string` now keeps a real pen on the AA path and advances it by each glyph's own metric (`font_set_aa` takes a second hook, `gui_aa_advance`, and the draw hook takes the cell width it may fill and clip to). Single-glyph callers (`font_draw_char` from the terminal's character grid, Keyrate's typing line) keep their fixed cell, a char grid is exactly where monospace placement is right. New `font_string_width` returns the width a string will actually occupy, and the four alignment sites that did `strlen*8` (right-aligned clock, weather text and its click hit-box, the two centered dock/launchpad labels) use it, so nothing drifts off its anchor now that proportional width and `strlen*8` differ. The bitmap VGA-font path is untouched.

Verified against real pixels, not reasoning: new `texttest` shell command renders "Cloudy" and a lone "W" through the real `font_draw_string` + `gui_aa_char` path at scale 2, scans every physical column through `window_get_pixel_phys`, and prints the ink runs. With the fix temporarily reverted (advance hard-set to 16, the old cell): runs `1-15 18-20 33-45 50-61 65-77 80-93`, C-l gap 2px, l-o gap 12px, spread 10, `W` ink width 16, both checks FAILED. Fix restored: runs `1-15 19-21 25-37 41-52 55-67 69-82`, gaps 3/3/3/2/1 (spread 2, the glyphs' own sidebearings), `W` ink width 23, both ok. Discriminating per 4b, proven both directions in the same session. `tools/checks/textspacing-check.sh` (the `png-check.sh` shape: boot headless, `sendkey` `texttest`, read the verdict off serial) wraps it and `.github/workflows/check.yml` runs it on every push (4d). `make kernel.elf` clean apart from the pre-existing unused-function warnings, `./check.sh` PASS, `./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean (271 references). Headless the whole pass, no `-display cocoa` window. `landing/v86/kernel.elf` resynced since this changes what every visitor sees in the embed. PATCH bump (0.67.0 -> 0.67.1): pure fix, no new capability.

## v74 / 0.66.0, minimal PNG decoder, and the 4MB kernel-image cliff it uncovered (Sep 2026)
Queue item 1, "prerequisite bridge, part 1", done. `drivers/png.c` + `png.h`: 8-bit RGB (color type 2) and RGBA (type 6), non-interlaced, any number of IDAT chunks, ancillary chunks CRC-checked and skipped. Anything outside that (palette, grayscale, 16-bit, Adam7) returns `PNG_E_UNSUPPORTED` cleanly instead of misdecoding. Underneath: a real RFC 1950/1951 inflater, all three block kinds (stored, fixed Huffman, dynamic Huffman), canonical-Huffman decode in the zlib "puff" shape (count/symbol tables, no recursion, no large stack), plus RFC 2083's five scanline filters. Chunk CRC-32 and the zlib Adler-32 trailer are both verified. Output is one kmalloc'd buffer in exactly `wallpaper_rgb`'s layout (tight rows, top-down, R,G,B[,A]), so queue item 2 can hand a decoded tile straight to the wallpaper path.

**Verified pixel-for-pixel against a real host decode, not "it compiled".** `tools/gen/gen_png_testdata.py` cuts three crops out of `drivers/wallpaper.h` and writes real PNG files by hand (not through PIL's encoder, on purpose): every scanline's filter type is chosen explicitly and cycled None/Sub/Up/Average/Paeth so all five reconstruction paths are provably hit, the zlib level is chosen per image so each DEFLATE block kind gets real coverage (320x180 RGB at level 9 = dynamic Huffman, 64x36 RGBA at level 0 = stored blocks, 6x5 RGB = fixed Huffman), and the block kind is read back out of the produced stream's header bits and asserted, not assumed. The IDAT stream is split across three chunks so the concatenation path is real too. The generator then decodes the exact bytes it embedded with PIL (the host-side reference decoder), asserts they equal the crop, and records an FNV-1a hash of the decoded bytes in `drivers/png_testdata.h`. In-kernel, `pngtest` decodes all three, compares every pixel to `wallpaper_rgb` at the crop's own offset (the expected pixels are the wallpaper itself, no second copy), hashes the output, and prints the hash on serial; `tools/checks/png-check.sh` boots headless (`-display none`, the monitor `sendkey` path `regress.sh` already uses) and demands the kernel's hash equal the host's for each image. Result: `PASS: 3 images decoded in-kernel match the host decode byte-for-byte (rgb_dyn, rgba_stored, rgb_fixed), corruption rejected`. Two fault injections finish it: one flipped IDAT byte must fail on the chunk CRC (`PNG_E_CRC`), and the same flip with the CRC recomputed must still fail inside inflate/Adler-32 (`PNG_E_ZLIB`) rather than decode to garbage. A truncated file returns `PNG_E_TRUNCATED`.

**Discrimination proof, per 4b, on both harnesses.** Temporarily changed Paeth's tie rule (`pb <= pc` to `pb < pc`, the exact kind of off-by-one a filter implementation gets wrong): host harness reported 6046 mismatched pixels on the dynamic image and 108 on the RGBA one (hashes `8e2f6ec3` / `a325ba92` against the host's `74931e2a` / `7841b89b`), in-kernel `png-check.sh` failed with those same two wrong hashes; restored, both green again. The fixed-Huffman 6x5 image happened to stay correct with the broken tie rule (no tie occurred in 30 pixels), a useful reminder that the tiny image alone would not have caught it, the 320x180 one did.

**Real bug found underneath, not in the decoder: the kernel image crossed 4MB.** First boot with the fixtures linked in page-faulted right after `irq_install`, before `pngtest` could run. Root cause: `_kernel_end` went from `0x3f2000` (pristine HEAD, 56KB under the line) to `0x40f000`, and both the temporary map in `boot/boot.S` and the permanent one in `paging.c` covered exactly one 4MB page table (`paging.c`'s own comment: "add more page tables when something is allocated above 0x400000"). The first `.bss` write past 4MB hit unmapped memory. The 1.5MB baked wallpaper is most of the image; the PNG fixtures (~108KB of rodata) were just the straw. Fix, not a workaround: the base map is now two back-to-back tables (8MB) in both `boot.S` (2048-entry fill, PDEs 0/1 and 768/769) and `paging.c` (`BASE_MAP_TABLES`, `base_page_tables[2][1024]`), `IDENTITY_MAP_LIMIT` in `kheap.c` and `paging.c` moved to 8MB to match. A second latent bug came out of reading the same code: `paging_set_user` and `paging_user_range_ok` indexed the first table only, `(addr % 4MB) / 4K`, which silently flips the U/S bit on the wrong page for any address in the second 4MB; `ring3.c`'s user code/stack pages are static `.bss` arrays, exactly the class of data that just moved. Both now resolve the base table from the address (`base_table_index`, either alias). `PAGING_PRIVATE_PDE` is 2 (8MB), so the per-task private slot still sits just past the base map, no overlap. Shrinking the fixtures to squeak back under 4MB was considered and rejected: the next agent adding any static data would have hit the same cliff with a less obvious symptom.

**Verified for real.** `make kernel.elf` clean (same two pre-existing warnings as v71-v73), `./check.sh` PASS, `./tools/png-host-check.sh` PASS, `./tools/checks/png-check.sh` PASS, `./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean. The paging change is the risky part, so the whole task/ring-3/heap list was re-run from a fresh boot via v73's boot-time direct-call trick (temporary `kmain` detour, reverted before commit, `git diff` is the feature only): `heaptest` (reuse, coalesce, split leftover), `tasktest` (`ABABABABABABABABABAB`), `preempttest`, `ring3test` (int 0x80 write returned 31, exit 42), `ring3test fault` (GP at a ring-3 EIP, task killed, kernel continues), `killtest`, `isotest` (separate address spaces), `heapgrow` (past 0x400000, writable), all `ok`. Headless the entire pass, no `-display cocoa` window. `landing/v86/kernel.elf` re-synced with the new build. CI (`check.yml`, per 4d) gained both PNG steps and installs `python3-pil` for the generator. New tool `tools/shell-run.sh`: boot headless, type any shell commands, print the VGA screen and serial, the one-liner that surfaced queue item 10 above. MINOR bump (0.65.2 -> 0.66.0): new capability.

## v75 / 0.67.0, real map wallpaper of the real town, and the honest satellite finding (Sep 2026)
Queue item 2. The chain is now real end to end: `geo_fetch` (v71) gives the lat/lon, twelve map tiles come down over plain HTTP, `png_decode` (v74, extended here) turns them into pixels, and the desktop paints from them. Default on, the baked photo shows until the fetch lands and stays if it fails, never a blank desktop. Switchable in Settings (a third row, label says "Photo", the looked-up city name once the map is up, or "Map (fetching, photo until then)" while it isn't) and from the shell (`wallpaper photo|map|fetch`), persisted as `wall=` in `SETTINGS.TXT` exactly like `wind=`/`dock=`.

**The gate first, same standard as the Stocks-app check, real `curl` against every candidate before a line of code.** Forced 301 to HTTPS, unusable here: `tile.openstreetmap.org` and every OSM mirror subdomain, `tile.openstreetmap.de`, `openstreetmap.fr`, Esri World Imagery (`server`/`services.arcgisonline.com`), NASA GIBS (`gibs.earthdata.nasa.gov`, `gibs-a`), Stadia, terrestris WMS. Dead: `tiles.wmflabs.org`, `tile.stamen.com`, `map1.vis.earthdata.nasa.gov`. Real `200 image/*` on plain HTTP: Google `mt0.google.com/vt/lyrs=s` (satellite, JPEG), Bing `ecn.t0.tiles.virtualearth.net` (satellite, JPEG), CartoCDN basemaps (PNG), Thunderforest (PNG), OsmAnd's `tile.osmand.net/hd` (PNG, 512px), OpenTopoMap (PNG). So the honest answer to "satellite": the only satellite imagery that answers without TLS is JPEG, and this kernel has a PNG decoder only. Not faked: what shipped is a **map**, named as such everywhere (Settings row, shell, code), and a baseline JPEG decoder is now the one real prerequisite left for the satellite version, recorded in the queue below.

**Second real finding, invisible to curl, found on the first framebuffer dump.** CartoCDN and Thunderforest both answer keyless requests with a real, valid, full-size PNG that has a giant "API KEY REQUIRED carto.com/basemaps/apikey" watermark rendered diagonally across the tile (Carto's Fastly image layer does it; a `Referer` header doesn't help). The headers say 200, `image/png`, sane byte counts; only rendering it showed it. The first working build of this pass used Carto and put that watermark on the desktop, byte-for-byte correctly. OpenTopoMap (CC-BY-SA, free with attribution for light use, 256px tiles, 8-bit paletted, ~40KB each, no key, no User-Agent check, `Content-Length` and no chunked encoding on an HTTP/1.0 GET) is clean, and its topographic style (hillshade, contours, rivers) suits a desert-named OS better than a flat street map anyway. OsmAnd's tiles are clean too but it's an undocumented proxy for their own app, not a published service.

**Three real kernel gaps the chain exposed, each fixed at the root.** (1) `drivers/png.c` only did color types 2 and 6; every real plain-HTTP tile server serves color type 3 (indexed). Added PLTE parsing (1..256 entries, before IDAT, `PNG_E_FORMAT` if an indexed image has no palette or a sample indexes past it) and an RGB expansion on output, so the caller still gets `wallpaper_rgb`'s layout. (2) `http_get` used a `char raw[2048]` stack buffer for the whole reply, headers included; every body was silently capped at ~2KB, fine for the JSON one-liners it had served so far, hopeless for a tile. Now kmalloc'd at `body_maxlen + 2048`. (3) `tcp_get` used a fixed local port 44000 and ISN 0x1000 for every connection. Tiles 0, 1, 2 fetched and decoded fine, tile 3 failed at HTTP every run (`wallerr=http 3` on serial): the fourth SYN on an identical 4-tuple to the same server hit a connection SLIRP still held half-closed, since this side sends its FIN and, by design, never waits for the final ACK. Fresh ephemeral port per connection (44000..59999), ISN varied by it. Never bit before because every caller so far spoke to a different host once per boot.

**Tile math without libm.** OSM wiki "Slippy map tilenames": `x = (lon+180)/360 * 2^z`, `y = (1 - ln(tan(lat) + sec(lat)) / pi) / 2 * 2^z`. `ln`, `tan`, `sqrt` and `pi` come from the x87 directly (`fyl2x` with `fldln2`, `fptan`, `fsqrt`, `fldpi`, four one-line inline-asm helpers), the same FPU the calculator's doubles already run on. Zoom 12, a 4x3 mosaic (1024x768) whose top-left tile is `floor(x - 1.5), floor(y - 1.0)` so the town lands in the middle, then a 960x540 crop centered on it, clamped. Each tile is decoded and copied straight into the destination, never assembled into a full mosaic first: peak heap is the 1.5MB destination plus one decoded tile.

**How the rest of the desktop copes.** The two real readers (`gui_wallpaper_color`, `gui_wallpaper_row`) index a `wall_src` pointer instead of the array; `wall_apply` swaps it and drops the wind crown cache and the dock band cache so no frame is a stale composite of the old pixels. `gui_wind_shift` returns 0 on a map (streets don't sway) but the wind tick keeps running, because that tick is where v65's rain/snow particles are drawn; the day/night tint is untouched, it sits at the one choke point every wallpaper draw already funnels through.

**Verified for real, per 4b, all headless.** `tools/checks/wallpaper-check.py` (new, permanent): boots with SLIRP, reads the `wall=z,tx,ty,cx,cy fnv` line the kernel mirrors to serial, recomputes the tile coordinates from the host's OWN ip-api answer (kernel `651,1403` = host `651,1403`), downloads the same twelve tiles, composes the identical crop with PIL and demands the FNV-1a of the finished 960x540 buffer match: `6b223a76` on both sides, byte-for-byte. Then pmemsaves the framebuffer and checks the wind band's mean color is the map tinted for the current hour (replicating `daynight_calc`), within 0.4 of the expected value and 126 away from the photo. Discrimination proved: with PLTE support reverted the kernel logs `wallerr=png -4` and the script fails; restored, it passes. `tools/checks/wallfx-check.py` (new, permanent): escapes to the shell, forces rain through a new `weatherfx rain|snow|off` knob, `wallpaper fetch`, back into `gui`, dumps the screen: 169 pixels of the raw rain-streak color over the map with rain on, 0 with `weatherfx off`, and the band mean is still the tinted map (the 7:01 PM frame is visibly dusk-tinted against the 6:55 PM daylight one, real tint over real fetched pixels). `pngtest` and `tools/png-host-check.sh` gained a fourth fixture, `pal_dyn` (96x64, 40-color median-cut palette, dynamic Huffman, compared by host hash `0xca59e561` since its pixels are palette lookups) plus a "PLTE renamed away" fault case that must return `PNG_E_FORMAT`. `./check.sh` PASS, `tools/checks/png-check.sh` PASS (4 images), `tools/checks/geo-check.sh` PASS, `tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean. CI (4d): a new non-gating `network` job runs geo-check, wallpaper-check and wallfx-check for real on every push.

**Open, honestly.** Satellite imagery needs a baseline JPEG decoder (Google/Bing tiles are the only plain-HTTP satellite sources found; both JPEG). The map is fetched once per session, twelve ~40KB tiles, a few seconds after the desktop is up; caching the composed 1.5MB buffer to `WALL.RGB` on the FAT disk would make the second boot instant, not done here. OpenTopoMap asks for attribution; the Settings row names the city, not the source, a small real gap worth a line of text in the weather dropdown or an About screen.

## v76 / 0.72.0, baseline JPEG decoder, the prerequisite v75 named (Sep 2026)
Direct request: build `drivers/jpeg.c`, "part 1" of the same size/ambition as v74's PNG bridge, to close the exact gap v75's honest finding logged above ("the only satellite imagery that answers without TLS is JPEG, and this kernel has a PNG decoder only"). Scope confirmed against real data before writing a line of decoder: `curl`'d `mt0.google.com/vt/lyrs=s` and `ecn.t0.tiles.virtualearth.net` fresh, parsed both SOF headers by hand (`FFC0`, precision 8, 3 components, component 1 sampling `2x2`, components 2/3 `1x1`, i.e. real baseline DCT, 8-bit, YCbCr, 4:2:0), confirming both real tile sources are squarely in scope before committing to it, not assumed.

**Scope, enforced the same way png.c enforces its own.** Baseline (SOF0) only; progressive (SOF2), extended-sequential (SOF1), lossless (SOF3) and arithmetic coding (SOF9-15) all return `JPEG_E_UNSUPPORTED`. 8-bit precision only (`JPEG_E_UNSUPPORTED` on a 12-bit DQT's 16-bit quant values or a non-8 SOF precision byte). 1 (grayscale) or 3 (YCbCr) components; CMYK (4-component) rejected. 4:4:4 or 4:2:0 subsampling only; 4:2:2 and anything else rejected (the two real tiles above are both 4:2:0, confirming this pass unlocks the real feature). Any `DRI` (restart interval) segment present is rejected outright rather than handling RST markers in the entropy stream, a deliberate, documented "reject cleanly rather than mishandle" call per the task's own scope note. Non-interleaved/multi-scan baseline (`Ns` in SOS != frame component count) is out of scope too, rare in practice and universally absent from single-shot web tile servers.

**Real decoder underneath, same complexity class as the PNG work.** Marker walk (SOI/APPn/DQT/SOF0/DHT/DRI/SOS/EOI, fill-byte and stuffed-0xFF handling). Canonical Huffman decode of DC/AC coefficients — unlike DEFLATE's dynamic-Huffman code-length runs, JPEG's DHT already lists symbols pre-sorted by (length, order), so `count[]`/`symbol[]` can be read straight off the wire with no build/sort step, a real simplification worth noting rather than reinventing PNG's `huff_build`. DC values decoded via category + sign-extended "receive" (spec table F.12); AC via zigzag run-length with ZRL/EOB. Dequantization is defensively clamped (`COEF_CLAMP`, ±2048) before the IDCT, the same overflow-guard discipline as png.c's stride/raw_len checks, so a corrupt or adversarial quant table can't overflow the int32 IDCT math. The IDCT itself is a real, correct separable 2D transform (an 8x8 fixed-point cosine table, `T[x][u] = round(4096 * 0.5 * C(u) * cos((2x+1)uπ/16))`, computed once offline in Python and baked in as a `static const` table, the same "no runtime float" constraint png.c never had to think about because this kernel builds `-mno-sse -mno-mmx` with no FPU init — DEFLATE and PNG filters are pure integer, JPEG's DCT math is the first subsystem here that would normally reach for floats, so baking a precomputed table was the real, deliberate way around that, not an afterthought). Not libjpeg's AAN/islow fast algorithms (no perf requirement here, same call the task gave explicit permission for). YCbCr->RGB via ITU-R BT.601 integer coefficients. Chroma upsampling is nearest-neighbor (not libjpeg's fancy/triangle filter), a real, documented simplification, not an oversight: it's the source of most of the tolerance the host check documents below.

**Verified two ways, split because JPEG is lossy (PNG's bit-exact bar doesn't apply).** `tools/gen/gen_jpeg_testdata.py` crops five real photographic regions from the same baked wallpaper `png_testdata.h` already uses (a real NPS photo, not synthetic single-color fixtures), re-encodes each through PIL at varied quality/subsampling (`photo_q90_420` 96x64 4:2:0 q90, `photo_q75_444` 64x48 4:4:4 q75, `photo_q50_420` 80x56 4:2:0 q50, `photo_q95_444` 48x48 4:4:4 q95, `gray_q85_420` 72x56 grayscale q85), and embeds both the JPEG bytes and PIL's own reference decode. `tools/checks/jpeg-host-check.sh` compiles `drivers/jpeg.c` natively (shims in `tools/jpeg-host/`, mirroring `tools/png-host/`) and compares this decoder's output against the PIL reference within a documented tolerance (max per-channel diff <= 24, mean abs diff <= 6), chosen by actually running the harness and observing real diffs cluster at chroma edges (nearest-neighbor vs. libjpeg's fancy upsampling) and high-frequency detail (independent IDCT rounding) — both genuine, expected lossy-decoder disagreement, not a decode bug. Measured result: `photo_q90_420` maxdiff=17 meandiff=1.16, `photo_q75_444` maxdiff=1 meandiff=0.04, `photo_q50_420` maxdiff=8 meandiff=1.45, `photo_q95_444` maxdiff=7 meandiff=1.68, `gray_q85_420` maxdiff=2 meandiff=0.66 — every fixture inside tolerance with real margin. Three fault injections: a file with no SOI marker (`JPEG_E_SIGNATURE`), a truncated file (`JPEG_E_TRUNCATED`), and a flipped byte inside the entropy-coded scan (must either fail cleanly or, when a flipped bit happens to still decode as *some* valid Huffman path, at least prove the output actually changed rather than silently ignoring the corruption). In-kernel, the new `jpegtest` shell command decodes the same five fixtures and prints an FNV-1a hash per image on serial; `tools/checks/jpeg-check.sh` runs `tools/jpeg-host` fresh (not a value baked into a generated header, since that could go stale) and demands the kernel's hash match the *live* native build exactly — the honest hash-for-hash bar here isn't kernel-vs-PIL (impossible for a lossy format without inventing a fake tolerance for a hash comparison), it's kernel-vs-itself-compiled-differently, which is a real, discriminating test: this decoder is fully deterministic, integer-only C, so a freestanding cross-compiled build and a native host build of the identical source are expected to agree bit-for-bit, and a mismatch would mean a real cross-compile/environment bug. Result: `PASS: 5 real photographic JPEGs decoded in-kernel match the native host build of the identical decoder hash-for-hash (photo_q90_420, photo_q75_444, photo_q50_420, photo_q95_444, gray_q85_420), corruption rejected`.

**Regression sweep, per 4b/4f.** `./check.sh` PASS. `tools/checks/png-host-check.sh` and `tools/checks/png-check.sh` both still PASS unchanged (jpeg.c is a new file, doesn't touch png.c, verified anyway rather than assumed). `make kernel.elf` clean (same two pre-existing unused-function warnings as prior versions, nothing new). Security-minded look at the one new real untrusted-input boundary this adds (a network-fetched tile, same class of input `http_get`'s v75 buffer-cap bug came from): every marker-length, DHT-table-size, DQT-table-id, MCU-count and plane-size computation is bounds/overflow-checked before use (mirroring png.c's stride/raw_len guards), the IDCT's coefficient clamp prevents a malicious quant table from driving the int32 accumulator out of range, and the Huffman decoder returns an error rather than reading past `JMAXBITS` on a corrupt code. No screenshot taken this pass (no attached display in this session; the decoder isn't wired into the wallpaper feature yet, so there's no new on-screen chrome to check against the Mojave palette regardless). MINOR bump (0.71.9 -> 0.72.0): new capability.

**Deferred, honestly, not attempted this pass.** Progressive JPEG, arithmetic coding, 12-bit precision, CMYK, 4:2:2 subsampling, restart-marker support, non-interleaved multi-scan baseline: all explicitly out of scope per the task, all return `JPEG_E_UNSUPPORTED` cleanly rather than misdecoding, none needed to unlock the real Google/Bing satellite tiles (confirmed baseline/4:2:0 above). Libjpeg-grade fast IDCT (AAN/islow) and fancy chroma upsampling: not attempted, no perf requirement, the straightforward separable IDCT and nearest-neighbor upsampling are real and correct, just not fast or maximally accurate, and the host-check tolerance documents exactly what that costs. **Not wired into the map wallpaper feature.** That's real, separate follow-up work: swapping `png_decode` for `jpeg_decode` on the Google/Bing tile paths, handling the different tile URL scheme (`x/y/z` slippy-map math already exists from v75, just needs a JPEG-serving host), and deciding whether satellite becomes a third `wall_theme` option alongside v82's four-state picker or replaces the map option outright. Queued, not started.

## Real macro-photo evidence, icon sharpness — root-caused and fixed (v81 / 0.68.1, Sep 2026)
Follow-up to the standing macro-photo complaint below: real headless capture (boot-time QEMU `-display none` + `pmemsave` of the real 1920x1080 physical framebuffer, a one-off scratch script in `/tmp` per this repo's own scratch convention, not committed, the same technique `iconhalo-check.py`/`dockhover-check.py` use, then a 4x/6x/10x nearest-neighbor zoom crop, not eyeballed) confirmed and separated the two complaints from the entry below:

- **The 3x3 Apps-folder grid glyph itself (`gui_icon_apps` in kernel.c, `window_rect` fills): clean.** It renders inside `gui_render_icon_cached`'s 6x-oversampled supersample buffer (`ICON_SS_SCALE`) same as every other glyph, then box-downsamples with the rest of the icon. Zoomed capture shows crisp, correctly-anti-aliased square edges (a real visible gray fringe pixel at each boundary, not a hard binary cut). Confirms this path was never broken; v58/v61/v68's fixes to other icons were real and this glyph was already fine, matching the code (same `window_rect`->`window_pixel` call chain every other primitive in the file uses, which does respect `window_push_target`'s supersample redirect).

- **The dock tray's own rounded corner (`gui_rounded_rect_on_wallpaper` in kernel.c): the real, confirmed bug.** Root cause: this function is a *different code path* from every icon's own corner. Icon corners (`gui_rounded_rect_gradient`) draw inside the same 6x-oversampled buffer as the glyphs and get real box-filtered AA. The tray itself never goes through that pipeline (it spans the full dock width; a full 6x offscreen buffer for it would be real wasted memory for two corners), and instead drew straight to physical framebuffer pixels, ONE distance sample per output pixel, thresholded into a 3-physical-pixel linear ramp (`band = 3`, from v44.1). One sample per pixel is not a coverage fraction, so pixels sitting exactly on the true circular boundary rendered as either full tray-shadow-corner color or full wallpaper color with zero blend, a hard binary staircase, exactly what the phone's macro photo showed. Confirmed with an exact pixel: physical (549, 918), sitting on the arc, read pure `(21, 10, 6)` pre-fix (zero coverage) and a real blended `(62, 53, 48)` post-fix.

  Fix (kernel.c, `gui_rounded_rect_on_wallpaper`): the same box-filter idea `ICON_SS_SCALE` already uses, applied analytically per-pixel instead of via an offscreen buffer (no memory cost, still one pass over the tray's own pixels): 4x4 = 16 subsamples per physical pixel, each tested against the true circle radius, averaged into a real coverage fraction, that fraction blends the tray color against the real wallpaper pixel sampled behind it (`gui_wallpaper_sample`, unchanged from v44.3). Before/after crops at the same physical coordinates (`/tmp/jt-arc-only-before.png` / `-after.png` during this pass) show the staircase replaced by a genuine multi-level gradient.

  New regression test, per the standing QA requirement: `tools/checks/traycorner-check.py`, same shape as `iconhalo-check.py` (boot headless, `pmemsave` the real framebuffer, assert on the one pixel above). Confirmed discriminating during this pass: reverting the subsample loop back to the old single-sample/band threshold makes the check read pure `(21, 10, 6)` again and FAIL; with the fix restored it reads a real intermediate blend and PASSes.

This is real, verified, shipped headroom, not the resolution ceiling: the grid glyph was already correct, and the tray corner's staircase had an identifiable, different-code-path root cause with a real fix, not a guess. Icon-sharpness as a standing focus area isn't necessarily closed after this. Any *future* jagged-edge report should first check whether it's inside the 6x-supersampled icon-glyph pipeline (already solid) or one of the handful of things (like the tray, before this fix) that draw straight to physical pixels outside it.

## Real macro-photo evidence, icon sharpness still open (Sep 2026)
Direct photo from Joshua's phone, real macro zoom on the Apps-folder dock icon: dock chrome improved since yesterday (v68's icon-glyph corner-bleed fix, v72's cleaner rendering), but both the circular tray-icon chrome edge and the 3x3 grid glyph itself still show real jagged/pixel-stair edges under zoom, not smooth AA curves. Confirms the standing icon-sharpness item is real and not yet fully resolved despite v58/v61/v68's real fixes to specific defects. Next icon-sharpness pass (task queue item) should start from this real photo, not guess at what's still rough. **Resolved above (v81/0.68.1): the grid glyph was already correct; the tray corner had a real, different-code-path AA bug, now fixed and regression-tested.**

## Real follow-up requests on the map wallpaper (Sep 2026)
Direct feedback after v75 shipped: (1) zoom in more, neighborhood-level (~10-15km), Joshua lives in Brookwood not just broadly Langley City. (2) Make the chrome (dock/panels) more Apple "Liquid Glass" inspired, translucent/frosted glass aesthetic.

Honest constraint on (1): ip-api.com's geolocation is typically city/ISP-node accurate, not household-precise, ~several km of real error is normal for IP geolocation. Zooming the slippy-map tiles deeper (zoom 12 today, could go to 14-15) is real and buildable, but landing precisely on "Brookwood" specifically may not happen even at max zoom if the underlying geolocation itself doesn't resolve that tightly. Ship the deeper zoom, don't promise neighborhood-name accuracy.

(2) is real UI/UX design work on the existing chrome (dock, notif/weather panels, Settings), not wallpaper-fetch logic. v66 already unified panel corner-radius/blend; "Liquid Glass" implies real translucency/blur over the wallpaper, a bigger rendering change. Needs real visual iteration (screenshot, judge, adjust), best done by the main session directly or its own dedicated pass, not a blind one-shot.

## v80 / 0.68.0, map wallpaper color grade, real Mojave tint on OpenTopoMap tiles (Sep 2026)
Direct request: v75's real map wallpaper (OpenTopoMap tiles) ships in its own neutral cartographer's palette (grays for roads, greens for parks, blues for water), which sits next to the sand/granite/leather-brown dock and panels as a visibly foreign, cold rectangle. Fixed with a real per-pixel color grade specific to the map, without touching the baked photo wallpaper's already-correct palette.

**Why a multiply, not a lerp.** `gui_daynight_tint` (v65) blends every pixel toward one flat target color (espresso-brown at night, Silver at day) as its percentage climbs, which is correct for a time-of-day dim/brighten but wrong for a base-palette grade: pushed far enough, a lerp collapses every distinct map color toward the same target, exactly the "washes out detail" failure this request called out. A real map still needs its road-gray-vs-water-blue contrast and its near-black street-label glyphs on near-white label backgrounds to survive. `gui_map_tint` (`kernel/kernel.c`) instead scales each channel by a fixed ratio (R x272/256, +6.25%; G x248/256, -3.1%; B x216/256, -15.6%, matching wall_bot's own Orange-blended-with-Black brown recipe a few hundred lines below), a multiplicative warm-white-balance shift. Two pixels that started different stay different (their ratio is preserved to rounding) while the whole image shifts warm. Deliberately mild, capped at a 15.6% single-channel move, so near-white label backgrounds stay near-white and near-black glyph strokes stay near-black.

**Where it's applied.** The two real per-pixel choke points that read `wall_src` directly: `gui_wallpaper_color` (icon-shadow/dock-tray AA blend targets) and `gui_wallpaper_px` (the actual on-screen blit, which also feeds the `wind_base` raw-sample cache). Both gate on `wall_src != wallpaper_rgb`, so the baked photo wallpaper is never touched, only the fetched map is. Runs before `gui_daynight_tint`, same order the photo already uses: the base palette grade first, the hour-of-day dim/brighten layered on top of it.

**Confirmed the existing day/night tint already covers the map**, per this repo's own roadmap notes: both `gui_wallpaper_color` and `gui_wallpaper_px` call `gui_daynight_tint`/`gui_daynight_tint_pct` unconditionally regardless of what `wall_src` points at, so v65's hour-based tint was already applying to map pixels before this pass; this pass adds the missing *baseline* palette grade underneath it, not a new day/night mechanism.

**Verified for real, per 4b.** New shell test `maptinttest` (`kernel/kernel.c`, same pure-function pattern as `daynighttest`): three discriminating checks against fixed OpenTopoMap-plausible colors (a road gray, a water blue, a near-white label background, a near-black label glyph) -- a neutral gray actually shifts warm (R ends up > B, having started equal), the gray and the water blue stay distinct after grading (proves multiply, not collapse-to-one-target), and the label background/glyph both stay on their own end of the contrast range (background R > 200, glyph R < 40). Discrimination proved directly, not assumed: a temporary boot-time direct call (`kmain`, reverted before commit, the same trick `check.sh`'s own comment documents for verifying output when `sendkey ret` can't be trusted) ran the identical check to real serial output with the fix in place (`TEMPVERIFY: maptint ok`) and again with `gui_map_tint` temporarily reverted to `return rgb;` (`TEMPVERIFY: maptint FAILED`), then restored.

**Real before/after evidence, this Mac's own attached display, not QEMU's broken screendump.** Booted `-display cocoa` with SLIRP networking so the real geo lookup + OpenTopoMap fetch landed, brought frontmost via `osascript`, captured with `screencapture`. Before (map tint reverted to identity): flat gray/tan roads, postcard-green parkland, cold blue water, visibly a different visual system than the brownish-silver dock directly below it. After (fix restored): the same real tile data now reads warm brown/olive/muted-blue, road and water and label contrast all still legible, and the map genuinely reads as part of the same Mojave desktop as the dock icons sitting on top of it.

**Regression + security pass, per 4f (this is a MINOR/.0 release).** Full `make clean` + `./check.sh` + `./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` rerun clean. `./regress.sh` (heaptest/tasktest/preempttest + FAT mkdir/write/cat over real sendkey input) hit its three pre-existing sendkey-delivery failures on a from-scratch baseline build too (confirmed by stashing this change and rerunning), the known `sendkey ret` harness limitation this repo's own CLAUDE.md documents, not a regression from this change. Security-minded look: `gui_map_tint` takes an already-decoded RGB value (post PNG-decode, post the v75 PLTE/format-validation path) and does clamped integer channel math only, no new parsing of untrusted network/tile data, no new buffer indexing, `r` is explicitly clamped to 255 and `g`/`b` can't overflow since both multipliers are under 256/256; no new attack surface introduced.

**VERSION bumped to 0.68.0** (MINOR: a real new capability, the map-specific color grade, not a bug fix).

## v82 (0.68.2), reaptest root-caused and fixed, plus a real app-QA sweep (Sep 2026)

**reaptest, real root cause, not "task.c lines 193-212" as first guessed.** Reproduced live (`tools/shell-run.sh reaptest`, typed into the real interactive shell after `gui_run()` has been running, not the boot-time direct-call trick, which never showed the bug at all): `task_create()` started returning -1 for every single new task, not just the 6th, the instant `reaptest` (or anything else creating a task) ran after the desktop had been up for a while. Instrumented `paging_new_task_directory()` and `task_create_frame()` with temporary serial traces (`TASKDBG`, removed before commit) and got the real failure directly: `paging_new_task_directory failed`, specifically `dir_phys/table_phys >= IDENTITY_MAP_LIMIT` (8MB). Traced further with a `schedule()`-side trace: the very first context switch into a freshly created task page-faulted (`v=0e` then `v=08` double fault, `qemu -d int`) with `CR2 == that task's own saved ESP`, and that ESP fell inside PDE 2 (8-12MB).

The real bug: `PAGING_PRIVATE_PDE` (kernel/paging.h, the fixed slot every task's private page table lives at) was `2`, exactly the same PDE `paging_map_region()`'s general-purpose extra-mapping pool (kernel/paging.c, `extra_page_tables`) hands out FIRST once anything -- kheap growth included -- needs memory past the 8MB base map. That happens during ordinary desktop use, not a pathological case: wallpaper/weather/font/window buffers grow the heap well past 8MB within seconds of `gui_run()` starting. `paging_new_task_directory()` clones the kernel's shared `page_directory` by value, so a brand-new task starts out sharing whatever kheap had already mapped at PDE 2 (very possibly including its own just-`kmalloc`'d kernel stack, which is exactly what happened), then unconditionally overwrites `dir[PAGING_PRIVATE_PDE]` with its own private table, destroying that shared mapping in its own copy only. The very next context switch into that task then double-faults popping its own ESP.

Fixed by moving `PAGING_PRIVATE_PDE` from `2` to `19`, one PDE past the general pool's entire reachable range (`BASE_MAP_TABLES` (2) + `MAX_EXTRA_TABLES` (16) = PDEs 0..17), with a comment explaining why and telling the next person to bump it again if either constant grows enough to reach it. Verified discriminating, not just re-asserted: reverted to `2` on a genuinely clean rebuild (`make clean` -- see the Makefile fix below, without it the revert silently ran the old, already-fixed `.o`) and reproduced the identical double-fault/reboot every time; restored `19` and confirmed `reaptest` passes (`reap: freed slot really reused: ok`) five runs in a row, plus the full existing task-subsystem suite unaffected: `tasktest`, `preempttest`, `killtest`, `ring3test`, `isotest` all still pass, and `tools/checks/appclose-check.py`'s full dock sweep still passes end to end.

**Real, standing gap found and fixed along the way, not a one-off:** the Makefile had no header-dependency tracking at all -- editing `paging.h` alone left `paging.o` and every other object that includes it stale, and `make kernel.elf` happily linked the old code with no warning. This is exactly how the first attempt at reverting `PAGING_PRIVATE_PDE` to prove discrimination silently did nothing. Fixed with `-MMD -MP` (clang emits a real per-object `.d` file listing every header it actually included) plus `-include $(OBJS:.o=.d)` in the Makefile, `.d` files gitignored. Verified for real: touched `paging.h` alone, confirmed `paging.o`'s mtime advanced on the next `make` where before this fix it wouldn't have.

**App QA sweep, real interaction + real disk-persistence verification, extending `tools/checks/appclose-check.py`'s open/close-via-X pattern:** new `tools/app-interact-check.py` (plus `tools/mkdisk.sh`, a real freshly-formatted FAT16 test image, the same `hdiutil`/`newfs_msdos` mechanism `sync_dotfiles.sh` already uses) boots with a real disk attached, drives every built-in app the way a real user would -- types in Notes, adds a Reminder, picks a Calendar day and saves an event, reads and composes Mail, adds and deletes a Contact, evaluates `2+2` in Calculator, views Stocks -- closes each via its real close button/Esc, and after the session ends, mounts the disk image **on the host** and greps each app's real `.TXT` file for the marker text just typed through the kernel's own `vfs_replace_file`, not just checking the UI didn't crash. Confirmed real, discriminating writes for Notes, Reminders, Mail, and Calendar (`NOTES.TXT`, `REMINDER.TXT`, `MAIL.TXT`, `EVENTS.TXT` all verified against the real mounted disk). Desktop stays responsive after the full sweep (Mail reopens and closes cleanly at the end), confirming v67's fixed class of bug still holds for every app, not just Notes/Chat.

Two real, honest findings from writing this test, both understood, not glossed over:
