1. `REMINDERS.TXT` (reminders.h's own filename) never appears under that literal name on the real disk. Root cause: `drivers/fat.c`'s `to_fat_name()` implements classic FAT 8.3 short names only (documented in its own header comment, "no long filenames"), silently truncating any base name past 8 characters -- `"REMINDERS"` (9 chars) becomes `"REMINDER"` on disk. Not a bug in the sense of broken behavior (the kernel reads and writes this same truncated name consistently, so Reminders round-trips its own data correctly within a session), but a real, silent surprise for anyone inspecting the disk expecting the name the source code uses. Worth a real fix later (rename the source constant to an already-8-char-safe name, e.g. `REMIND.TXT`), not done here, out of this pass's scope.
2. Contacts: add + delete + persist is real and correct (verified directly, isolated, screenshot-by-screenshot: `contacts_add()`/`contacts_delete_at()`/`contacts_save()` all fire correctly and the resulting `CONTACTS.TXT` matches exactly what was typed), but the full five-app-deep sweep intermittently fails to find the Contacts marker on disk afterward even though the on-screen list shows the add succeeding. Not root-caused in this pass: reproduced 2/2 on a clean run, but isolating just the Contacts flow alone (same kernel, same disk, same keystrokes) passed every time it was tried, and this host also has real, separate QEMU-headless flakiness under load (`ConnectionResetError` mid-run, unrelated processes competing for CPU, confirmed via `ps`) that makes a deep multi-app session a worse environment to trust than an isolated one. Recorded honestly as unresolved rather than forced to a false PASS or silently dropped from the suite; a real next step is a Contacts-only repeat of the exact five-app-deep sequence with `-d int`/serial tracing once this host is quieter. **Confirmed still pre-existing, not a regression, during v83's pass below (reran on both the pre-fix and post-fix kernel, same FAIL either way, same host, unrelated to that pass's change).**

## v83 (0.68.3), a second real staircase found by following v81's own guidance (Sep 2026)

Direct request, explicitly following the guidance v81 left in its own entry above: check for other UI elements drawing straight to physical pixels outside the 6x-supersampled icon-glyph pipeline, rather than re-checking glyphs v79/v81 already confirmed clean.

**Where it was found.** `gui_rounded_rect_on_wallpaper`'s own call sites (dock tray, Settings window chrome, the Apps-folder flyout, the notif panel, the weather dropdown panel) all funnel through that one already-fixed function (v79), so every one of those corners inherited the v79 subsample-coverage fix automatically, nothing new to do there. Grepping for every other primitive that draws a circle or rounded shape turned up `gui_fill_circle` and `gui_fill_circle_gradient` (kernel.c). Both compute their AA ramp in LOGICAL pixel units through `AA_BAND`/`gui_lerp`, same style `gui_rounded_rect_on_wallpaper` used before v79. Checking every call site: `gui_fill_circle_gradient` and the great majority of `gui_fill_circle` calls only ever run inside `gui_render_icon_cached`'s offscreen `ICON_SS_SCALE` buffer (a real target pushed via `window_push_target`), where `window_pixel` writes 1:1 into that buffer and the later box-downsample does the genuine AA -- those were never broken, the same conclusion v81 reached for the glyphs generally. But `gui_fill_circle` has two call sites with no target pushed at all: `gui_draw_app_titlebar`'s traffic-light dots (drawn on **every single windowed app** -- Weather, Mail, Calendar, Contacts, Calculator, Stocks, Notes, Settings, all of them, since they all share this one function) and Settings' own separate traffic-light draw.

**Root cause, confirmed with real pixel evidence, not assumed from reading the code.** At `window_scale() == 2` (every real dock-launched app; the scale-1 `testapps` shell diagnostic apptest.sh drives never actually exercises this, since it opens its own 800x600 scale-1 window, which is exactly why this had gone unnoticed by that suite), plain `window_pixel` replicates each LOGICAL pixel it's given into a flat, uninterpolated `scale x scale` PHYSICAL block -- no interpolation between blocks. `gui_fill_circle`'s AA ramp produces a handful of real, distinct grey levels in logical space, but each one lands on screen as a hard-edged physical block, not a smooth gradient: a genuine macro-visible staircase, same root shape as v79's tray-corner bug, different mechanism (block-replication of a correct logical ramp, vs. v79's single-sample-per-physical-pixel threshold), same "draws outside the true-coverage path" root cause v81 asked to keep watching for.

Captured for real: booted headless (`-display none`, QMP), drove the actual real mouse path (`input-send-event` abs move + `btn` click, the same v62 vmmouse mechanism `dockhover-check.py`/`app-interact-check.py` already use) to click the Weather dock icon on the real desktop, `pmemsave`d the physical framebuffer once the window was open, and zoomed the close (red) traffic-light dot 10x. Pre-fix: visibly stepped concentric terraces around the dot's edge, not a gradient (screenshots kept at `/tmp/jt-tb2-red-zoom.png`/`-after.png` during this pass). Exact pixel: physical (187, 98), on the dot's own AA fringe, read pure `(255, 95, 87)` pre-fix (the dot's un-blended fill color, zero coverage blend) and a real intermediate `(250, 167, 161)` post-fix.

**Fix (`kernel.c`, `gui_fill_circle`).** Same idea as v79's tray-corner fix, applied where it's actually needed: when `!window_has_target()` (no offscreen buffer active, i.e. this is a direct/physical chrome draw, not one of the icon-glyph calls) and `window_scale() > 1`, compute coverage in PHYSICAL pixels instead of logical: 4x4 = 16 subsamples per physical pixel, each tested against the true circle radius, averaged into a real coverage fraction, blended against the caller's own `into` background color via `window_pixel_phys`. Every icon-glyph call site is unaffected (`window_has_target()` is true there, unchanged original logical-space path, `AA_BAND` still widened to 18 for that buffer exactly as before). `gui_fill_circle_gradient` was left alone: confirmed by exhaustive grep that every one of its call sites already sits inside the icon-glyph supersample pipeline, so there was nothing broken there to fix, and adding an unused guard would be scope creep with no discriminating test to prove.

**New regression test, per the standing QA requirement:** `tools/titlebar-aa-check.py`, same shape as `traycorner-check.py`: boots headless, clicks the real Weather dock icon via the real QMP mouse path, `pmemsave`s the framebuffer, and asserts physical pixel (187, 98) is a real intermediate blend. Confirmed discriminating during this pass, the same way as always: reverted `gui_fill_circle` back to its old logical-space-only form on a from-scratch `make clean` rebuild, reran, got the exact pre-fix pixel `(255, 95, 87)` back and a real FAIL; restored the fix, reran, real PASS. `tools/checks/traycorner-check.py` (v79's own regression test) still passes unchanged, confirming this pass didn't touch that already-fixed path. `tools/checks/dockhover-check.py` still passes. `tools/app-interact-check.py` still hits the same pre-existing, already-documented (v82 above) intermittent Contacts-disk-marker FAIL on both the pre-fix and post-fix kernel identically -- confirmed not a regression, same root cause v82 already recorded, out of scope for this pass. `./check.sh` and `./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` both clean on a `make clean` rebuild.

**Honest scope of what's left.** This is the second, and by the exhaustive grep done this pass, the last real instance of the "draws straight to physical pixels outside the supersampled pipeline" pattern found in this codebase: every rounded-rect call site funnels through the already-fixed `gui_rounded_rect_on_wallpaper`, every `gui_fill_circle_gradient` call and the great majority of `gui_fill_circle` calls run inside the already-correct icon-glyph pipeline, and the two remaining direct calls (both feeding the exact same `gui_draw_app_titlebar`/Settings traffic-light code) are now fixed and regression-tested. Icon-sharpness as a standing focus area may genuinely be at its real ceiling for this codebase's rounded/circular primitives specifically; any further report should look at other primitive shapes (text glyph edges, straight-line edges, gradients) rather than assuming another rounded-corner instance is still hiding, since this pass's own exhaustive grep didn't find one.

## v84 (0.69.0), wallpaper themes: Photo / Map Warm / Map Cool / Map Raw (Sep 2026)

Direct request: multiple wallpaper themes/styles, selectable in Settings, building on v75's photo/map toggle and v80's `gui_map_tint` warm color grade. Scoped deliberately inside real current constraints (no JPEG/satellite decoder exists in this kernel, that stays a separate queued roadmap item, not built against): `wall_mode`'s binary photo/map flag becomes `wall_theme`, a real 4-state picker (`WALL_PHOTO`/`WALL_WARM`/`WALL_COOL`/`WALL_RAW`) --  Photo (v75's baked NPS photo, unchanged), Map Warm (v80's existing Mojave grade, still the default), Map Cool (new: `gui_map_tint_cool`, the opposite channel balance from Warm -- blue pushed up instead of down, red pushed down instead of up -- plus a real ~15% contrast stretch Warm deliberately never applies, for the "higher-contrast... for legibility" half of the direct request), Map Raw (the fetched map with no color grade at all, for comparing against OpenTopoMap's own native palette). Every per-pixel reader of the map (`gui_wallpaper_color`, `gui_wallpaper_px`) now goes through one real choke point, `gui_wall_tint`, instead of each call site re-deriving its own "is this the photo" guard. Settings' wallpaper row cycles all 4 (was a toggle); the shell's `wallpaper photo|warm|cool|raw|fetch` keeps `map` as a working alias for `warm` so nothing already scripted against v75's two-state name breaks. `SETTINGS.TXT`'s `wall=` key now persists 0-3, range-clamped on load the same discipline `dock=`'s 5-25 clamp already uses.

**Real bug found and fixed while capturing evidence for this pass, not shipped blind.** `wall_apply`'s cache invalidation (`wall_caches_drop`, which frees the `wind_base` tinted-sample cache `gui_draw_desktop` lazily rebuilds) was keyed on `wall_src`'s pointer changing. Warm -> Cool -> Raw all pass `want_map=1` against the identical `wall_map` buffer, so the pointer never actually changes and the early-return skipped `wall_caches_drop()` entirely: real headless framebuffer captures (QEMU `pmemsave` of the actual physical framebuffer, real network fetch of real OpenTopoMap tiles, not a mock) showed all three map themes rendering an *identical* mean color over the wind band (57.6/72.8/89.1 three times over) the first time this was checked, not three visibly distinct grades. Root-caused to the pointer-only cache key, fixed by adding `wall_last_theme` so a theme-only change (same buffer, different grade) still drops the stale cache. Re-captured after the fix: Photo 36.9/32.9/44.3, Warm 57.6/72.8/89.1, Cool 72.4/72.0/76.0 (visibly flatter and higher-contrast, exactly the legibility intent), Raw 64.9/74.6/86.1 (OpenTopoMap's own palette) -- four real, distinct numbers, and eyeballing the four saved PNGs confirms it: Cool reads noticeably flatter/punchier than Warm, Raw reads as a cooler, more neutral cartographer gray than either tinted theme, Photo is the unrelated NPS photo. Menubar chrome (Mojave gray bar, title, dock icon) stayed visually identical across all four, confirming the theme change is scoped to the wallpaper layer only.

**New regression test, per the standing QA requirement:** `walltest` (shell command, `kernel.c`, the `heaptest`/`maptinttest` pattern, not a standalone script since this is pure in-kernel color/settings logic with no display-driving needed). Five real, discriminating checks: (1) Cool and Warm push a neutral gray in opposite directions (Warm: red above blue; Cool: blue above red) on the identical input, not just "some different number"; (2) Cool(gray) != Warm(gray); (3) exercises the real `gui_wall_tint` dispatch directly (not just the two standalone tint functions) across all 4 themes plus the Photo early-out, so a broken dispatch fails here even if the tint math alone is fine -- proved discriminating by temporarily hardcoding `gui_wall_tint` back to always-Warm (this pass's own real pre-fix shape) and confirming a real FAIL, then restoring and confirming a real PASS; (4) Cool keeps two distinct source colors (road gray, water blue) distinct after grading, proving it's a real per-pixel transform, not a lerp-to-one-target; (5) all 4 theme values round-trip through the real `settings_save`/`settings_load` SETTINGS.TXT path, not a mock.

**Verified:** `check.sh` and `tools/checks/check-refs.sh` (319 refs) both clean on a `make clean` rebuild after rebasing onto `jt-v83`. `walltest`, `maptinttest`, `heaptest`, `daynighttest`, `weatherfxtest`, `mailtest`, `calctest`, `weathertest`, `geotest` all still pass via `tools/shell-run.sh`. `regress.sh`'s disk-image checks (`heaptest`/`tasktest`/`preempttest` through the QEMU+FAT harness) fail identically on unmodified `main` in this sandbox, confirmed via `git stash` before touching anything -- a pre-existing environment gap (this sandbox's `hdiutil`/`newfs_msdos` path), not a regression from this change.

**Security-minded look (4f):** `wall_theme` parsed from `SETTINGS.TXT` is range-clamped (`WALL_PHOTO..WALL_RAW`) before use, same discipline the existing `dock_scale_pct` clamp already established for this same user-writable file; a corrupt or hand-edited `SETTINGS.TXT` can't select an out-of-range theme, and `gui_wall_tint` only ever compares `wall_theme` against the 4 known constants, no new indexing/pointer surface introduced.

## Landing page accessibility audit and polish (Sep 2026)

A real, computed WCAG AA contrast audit of `landing/index.html` across light and dark modes, not eyeballed. Tested real hex values from the CSS palette, computed relative luminance per WCAG formula, verified against 4.5:1 (normal text) and 3:1 (large text) targets.

**Real failure found and fixed:** Secondary text color `--sub:#75726e` on light background `--bg:#faf8f6` yielded 4.16:1 (fail). The darker shade `#6f6c68` yields 4.58:1 (pass), staying within the Mojave desert palette's brownish-gray range and darkening evenly across both light and dark modes' accent/sub hierarchy. Verified all other combinations pass: primary text (15.87:1 light / 15.02:1 dark), accent colors (6.29:1 / 6.91:1), both modes' card backgrounds, footer elements.

**Focus states:** `a:focus-visible, .skip-link:focus` (2px solid outline in `--accent`) present on every interactive element, high contrast on all tested backgrounds.

**Semantics:** Alt text present on the progress.svg chart; boot-logo img correctly `aria-hidden="true"`; skip-link follows standard pattern.

**Motion sensitivity:** All page animations (scroll hint arrow, reveal-on-scroll, drifting glyph field) already respect `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`. Added a check to the v86 demo's autoplay tour: the idle cursor-motion/keyboard-simulation sequence (`embed.js` lines 722-726) now checks `window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)')` and skips tour startup if set, letting motion-sensitive visitors see a static demo instead of automatic cursor movement.

**Font sizes:** Body text 14px, headlines 18px+ (18px tagline, 34-56px h1), acceptable per WCAG for readability.

**Verified:** Valid HTML (Python html.parser), CSS parses cleanly, no new validation errors introduced. No kernel changes needed; landing-only polish fix.

## Real bug report: landing demo tour hangs on Files, stuck hover state (Sep 2026)
Direct report, grade "B+" overall but real, currently-live: the idle tour's sequential app-cycling (v78's rework) opens Files (the dock's Finder-equivalent) and stops there, never advancing to the next app. Separately, the Files dock icon stays visually stuck in a hover/highlighted "title showing" state even after Files is closed and other apps opened afterward. Not yet root-caused. Real next step: reproduce with mobiletest.mjs against the live deployed site, check whether Files has a distinct code path from the other 7 apps the tour already cycles cleanly (Mail/Calendar/Notes/Reminders/Terminal/Chat/Weather per v78's own report), and whether the stuck-hover-highlight state ties back to the same dock-hover state machine v63 already fixed once for a different trigger.

**v86 (0.71.0) update, real investigation, honest partial resolution.** Four related bugs looked at together (this entry's own two plus two more reported since): Files showing "no files" on the browser demo, the tour hang described above, the stuck dock hover described above, and launchpad tiles not opening on click.

- **Files "no files" (real, root-caused, fixed).** Confirmed by reading `drivers/vfs.c`'s `vfs_register`/`vfs_switch`: the first backend registered at boot wins by default, and `kmain` (kernel/kernel.c) registers `fat` before `ramfs`. `fat_mount()` only succeeds when a real disk is attached; native QEMU boots with `dotfiles.img` (`make run`'s own `-drive` flag), but v86's browser embed has no disk wired into its multiboot path at all (confirmed reading `landing/v86/embed.js`: nothing there ever attaches a virtual disk), so `fat_mount()` always fails there and Files genuinely has nothing to show, an honest disk failure, not a display bug. Fixed by seeding `README.TXT`/`NOTES.TXT` into `ramfs.c` (which already existed as the codebase's proven second VFS backend, previously empty) and calling `vfs_switch("ramfs")` in `kmain` whenever `fat_mount()` reports no disk (kernel/kernel.c, right after `ramfs_init()`). Native boots with a real disk are unaffected, this only fires when `fat_mount()` already failed. Regression test: `tools/ramfs-demo-check.sh`, boots headless with no `-drive` flag (the identical no-disk condition v86 always hits) and asserts on the real klog line the fix emits over serial; proven discriminating (temporarily reverted the fix in kernel.c, reran, got the expected `FAIL: no-disk boot never switched to ramfs`, then restored it and got `PASS` again).

- **Launchpad tiles don't open on click (real, root-caused, fixed).** Confirmed by reading `gui_launch_apps` (kernel/kernel.c): every click landing on the Apps-folder grid screen was treated as "close the folder" unconditionally, zero hit test against the tile cells drawn just above it, unlike the dock (hit-tested by `gui_dock_hit_test`). Only the keyboard path (arrows+Enter, or digits `1`-`9` covering just the first 9 of 22 apps) could actually launch anything from there. Fixed by hit-testing the click position (`app_cursor_x/y`, converted from full-screen to the launchpad's own viewport-relative coordinates by subtracting `app_view_x/app_view_y`, a real coordinate-space bug caught and fixed in the same pass before it shipped) against each cell's real drawn bounds, launching that app on a hit and only closing the folder on a miss. Regression test: `tools/launchpad-click-check.py`, QMP-driven headless (the `dockhover-check.py` pattern): opens the Apps folder, clicks squarely on the Curbfind tile, and asserts the outer window's traffic-light red dot is still on screen (meaning an app launched inside the same window) rather than gone (meaning the click just closed the folder back to the desktop). Proven discriminating the same way as the ramfs fix: reverted, reran, got the expected `FAIL`, restored, got `PASS`; a screenshot from this same run (`/tmp/jt-launchpad.png` during this session) shows Curbfind's own view rendering correctly inside the "Apps" window chrome, matching the established Mojave panel style.

- **Stuck dock hover (real narrow bug found and fixed, but NOT confirmed to be the full explanation for a persistent hang).** Reading `gui_run`'s main loop (kernel/kernel.c): `slot_here` (the dock slot under the cursor) is computed once per frame, before the `just_released` branch calls the blocking `gui_launch_from_dock()` and only afterward updates `mx`/`my` to the app's real post-close cursor position; `hover_slot` then reused that stale, pre-launch `slot_here` on the same frame. Fixed by recomputing `slot_here` right after the cursor position is updated, same call already used to build it in the first place. Real, honest caveat: headless pixel verification (QMP abs-pointer + a settled `pmemsave`, the same `dockhover-check.py` pattern) could **not** reproduce a *persistent* stuck-lifted tile either before or after this fix, the effect self-corrects within about one frame regardless (the target only rarely differs from what was already animating, since the tile was usually already fully magnified from being hovered right before the click). A non-discriminating test was written, confirmed non-discriminating, and deliberately **not** kept in the suite rather than shipped as false evidence (house rule 4b's own bar). This fix is real and correct but is not confirmed to explain a hover stuck for more than a frame; the underlying report may need a live recording with real, human-speed mouse movement (not QMP's instant absolute jumps) to reproduce, still open.

- **Tour hangs on Files (investigated, NOT reproduced, likely already not an issue, still open honestly).** Read `landing/v86/embed.js`'s `TOUR_APPS`/`tourLoop` in full: Files has **no** distinct code path from the other 7 toured apps. It carries no interactive `script` (same as Weather), closes through the exact same fixed `CLOSE_X`/`CLOSE_Y` click every app uses, and `gui_launch_files` (kernel/kernel.c) calls the same `gui_wait_close()` every other app-view function calls, nothing special. This refutes the theory that bug #1 (empty file list) broke a Files-specific interaction script, there was never one. Reproduction attempt against the live deployed site (`joshuatree.heyitsmejosh.com`, a 45-second canvas-fingerprint sample across a full Files dwell, close, and the start of Mail) showed the tour advancing cleanly: Files opened at ~t=14s, dwelled the expected ~15s, closed and handed off to Mail at ~t=29-30s, no hang. Given no reproduction and no code-level evidence of a Files-specific defect, this is recorded honestly as **not currently reproducible**, not claimed fixed. Possible explanations for the original report, none confirmed: an intermittent host-load-dependent timing issue (precedent: v70's own `net.c` wait-loop bug was exactly this shape), or it was already resolved by an unrelated change since the report was filed (`b1f0716` predates several since-shipped passes).

**Verification for this whole pass:** `make clean && make`, `./check.sh` (PASS), `tools/checks/check-refs.sh` (344 refs clean), `mobiletest.mjs` and `tourtest.mjs` both rerun locally against the rebuilt `kernel.elf` (both PASS: a dock tap opens an app and Notes closes from its own X; the idle tour opens an app with zero interaction), the three regression scripts above all individually proven discriminating by temporary revert-and-restore. Security-minded look (4f, this is a MINOR release): the only new input-facing surface is the launchpad's click hit test, which only ever selects among the existing fixed 0..21 `GUI_APPS_FOLDER` app indices already reachable via the keyboard path, no new trust boundary. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s `drivers/ramfs.c` row updated to describe the new demo-seeding behavior (no new files added this pass, so no new table rows needed).

**Lower-priority quick check, per direct request, not over-invested in:** `curl -I` with `Accept-Encoding: gzip, br` against the live site confirms `index.html` and `v86.wasm` both come back `content-encoding: br` from Cloudflare; `kernel.elf` (2.8MB) comes back with **no** `content-encoding` header at all, uncompressed. Real, minor gap, not fixed this pass (Cloudflare-side asset config, not a kernel or landing-page code change, and the WASM binary already dwarfs it for transfer weight). A real loading indicator already exists and works: `landing/index.html`'s `#boot-logo` (added v52.5, made unmissable v52.8) shows immediately and fades out on `embed.js`'s own existing graphical-mode check, confirmed by reading the code, not just assumed.

## Landing demo: "doesn't autoplay at all" report, investigated, not reproduced; app count trimmed to match spec (v0.71.4, Sep 2026)
Direct report from Joshua, distinct from the earlier "tour hangs on Files" entry above: this one claimed the idle tour never starts at all, not that it starts and stalls. Read `landing/v86/embed.js` top to bottom against the live file (not from memory): the arming `setInterval` (graphical-mode poll, 6s idle timer, `prefersReducedMotion` gate) and `tourLoop`/`TOUR_APPS` were both intact and match the v0.71 sequential-cycle rework. Checked the two CSS/HTML changes named as suspects before assuming either was guilty: the `#v86-overlay` text content (added then reverted same session, `2989861`/`44372d6`) never touches the tour-arming code path, and `#stage-wrap`'s `position:absolute` -> `width:100%/aspect-ratio:16/9` change (`c66fa50`/`2989861`) only affects `resizeCanvas`'s cover/contain heuristic, not `graphical_mode` detection or the interval. Neither is the cause.

**Reproduction attempt: FAIL to reproduce, recorded honestly.** Ran `tourtest.mjs` (unmodified, no interaction injected) against both a local static server serving this exact worktree and the live deployed site (`joshuatree.heyitsmejosh.com`): both PASS, an app opened on its own with zero interaction, canvas brightness fingerprint jumping from the idle-desktop baseline (1915) to an open-app level (45016) within the expected ~6-10s arm-plus-open window. A second throwaway console-error probe (headless Chromium, 20s dwell, all console/pageerror events captured) showed zero JS errors, only benign preload/AudioContext warnings unrelated to the tour. `system.reduceMotion` was also checked and is not set on this machine, ruling out the `prefers-reduced-motion` gate as an explanation for a report made here. No code-level defect found, no reproduction achieved, so this is **not claimed fixed**, matching the same honest bar the Files-hang entry above set. Real possible explanations, none confirmed: a stale cached copy of `embed.js` in the reporting browser from before `3f851a2`'s sequential-cycle rework (which itself fixed two real non-start-adjacent bugs, the dock-reopen glitch and the swallowed keyboard input, hours before this report), or a host-load-dependent timing miss on a slower connection than any test here reproduced.

**Real, separate spec gap fixed regardless.** Joshua's own description of what the tour *should* do ("opening apps like one or two, or maybe three apps") didn't match what `TOUR_APPS` actually cycled: all 8 real dock apps, forever. Trimmed to 3 (Files, Mail, Calendar), a representative mix of a no-interaction dwell app and two apps with real scripted keystrokes, cycling continuously as before. This is a real, scoped-down loop, not a rewrite of the open/interact/close mechanics from the v0.71 rework, which stayed untouched. New `tools/checks/tourappcount-check.mjs`: a static, discriminating check that reads `embed.js`'s real `TOUR_APPS` array and asserts 1-3 entries; proven both ways, reverting to the old 8-app array (via a temporary `git stash`) reruns the check and gets `FAIL: tour cycles 8 apps, expected 1-3`, restoring the trim gets `PASS: tour cycles 3 apps`. `tourtest.mjs` also rerun after the trim and still PASSes locally.

Verification: `tourtest.mjs` PASS (local + live-prior-to-deploy), `tools/checks/tourappcount-check.mjs` PASS with real fail-then-pass proof, no kernel change so `check.sh`/`make` untouched by this pass. PATCH bump (0.71.3 -> 0.71.4): a fix plus a scope correction, no new kernel capability.

## Investigated: `network_relay_url: "fetch"` for real browser-demo network (dead end, no code shipped, Sep 2026)

Direct investigation into a real lead: `landing/v86/libv86.js` supports `network_relay_url: "fetch"`, a mode where the emulated NIC's TCP/IP is proxied through the browser's own real `fetch()` (class `Xb` in the vendored bundle), meaning the browser terminates TLS instead of the guest kernel. The premise was that this might let `drivers/http.c`'s real HTTP client reach live map tiles/weather in the browser demo (currently impossible, zero real network by design), without needing in-kernel TLS.

**Two real things checked, both against real evidence, not assumed:**

1. **CORS requirement, confirmed by reading `Xb.prototype.fetch`.** It calls a plain `fetch(a, b)` (default `cors` mode, not `no-cors`) and awaits `response.arrayBuffer()` to read the body back into the fake TCP stream; a `no-cors` opaque response wouldn't work anyway since its body reads back empty. So any target host needs real CORS headers or the browser fetch throws and the adapter synthesizes a 502 into the guest's stream. Checked the three real hosts this kernel's drivers actually call, all on port 80 per `kernel/kernel.c` (`ip-api.com`, `api.open-meteo.com`, `a.tile.opentopomap.org`): `curl -D -` showed `ip-api.com` and `a.tile.opentopomap.org` DO send `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, but `api.open-meteo.com` sends **no CORS headers at all**. So even in isolation, the weather call would have failed cross-origin while geo/map calls might not have.

2. **The real, unconditional blocker: v86 never emulates an RTL8139 NIC at all.** `drivers/rtl8139.c` is this kernel's only network driver, written for a real RTL8139 chip. Grepped `landing/v86/libv86.js` for `rtl8139`/`RTL8139`: zero matches anywhere in the file. v86 only emulates `ne2k` (its default `net_device.type`) and `virtio-net`, confirmed reading the PCI device dispatch (`"ne2k"===a.net_device.type?...new Mc(...):"virtio"===a.net_device.type&&...new fd(...)`). This kernel's `rtl8139_init()` probes specifically for RTL8139 PCI IDs, which v86 never presents, regardless of `network_relay_url`.

**Real evidence, not just reasoning: live-tested via headless Playwright.** Experimentally added `network_relay_url: "fetch"` to `landing/v86/embed.js`'s `V86` constructor, served `landing/` locally, booted headless, force-enabled the keyboard/mouse adapters (bypassing the click-to-focus gate), drove the real cursor to the Terminal dock icon (`window.__jt.moveTo`/`.click()`, same bus path the idle tour uses) and typed the real command `wallpaper fetch\n` via `keyboard_send_text`, the exact code path that calls `rtl8139_init()` then `net_init()`/`wall_fetch()`. A real screenshot (`/tmp/after_fetch.png` during this session) shows the kernel's own terminal output: **`> wallpaper fetch` / `no NIC`**. Zero `fetch()` calls to ip-api/open-meteo/opentopomap were observed in Playwright's request/response logs across the whole run, confirming no network attempt ever left the browser, exactly as the code-level finding predicted: the kernel's own PCI probe fails before any TCP/CORS question is even reached.

**Conclusion: real dead end, CORS is moot.** The `network_relay_url: "fetch"` mode itself works as documented (browser-side fetch proxying), but this kernel has no driver for either NIC type v86 can actually emulate (`ne2k`, `virtio-net`), only for RTL8139, which v86 doesn't emulate at all. Making this work for real would mean either porting an ne2k/virtio-net driver into the kernel (a real, scoped new-driver project, not a config change) or getting v86 to emulate RTL8139 (out of this project's control, it's a third-party vendored library). Neither attempted here, out of scope for "does a config flag unlock this." No files changed: the experimental `network_relay_url` line was added to a local copy for the live test above and reverted before commit; `git diff` against `main` is empty except this roadmap entry. The native QEMU path (real `-netdev` bridge, real RTL8139 emulation) remains the only place live map/weather actually work, unchanged.

## v0.71.8, bug-hunt pass: real TCP length-field trust and FAT cyclic-chain hang (Sep 2026)

Direct request, systematic stress-test pass across subsystems (not scoped to a reported bug), 2 real bugs found and fixed, each with a discriminating regression test proven to fail on the pre-fix code and pass on the fix. Scrutinized deeply: memory management (`kernel/pmm.c`/`paging.c`/`kheap.c`), task scheduler (`kernel/task.c`), network stack (`drivers/net.c`/`http.c`/`rtl8139.c`), FAT/VFS (`drivers/fat.c`/`vfs.c`). GUI/framebuffer bounds (`kernel/kernel.c`'s `gui_*`) came back clean: every pixel write ultimately routes through `drivers/window.c`'s `screen_pixel`, which bounds-checks `px`/`py` against `phys_w`/`win_h*scale` (and the active viewport/screen-band) before ever touching `fb`, confirmed by reading every write path (`window_pixel`, `window_pixel_phys`, `window_rect`) rather than assumed; real effort, no finding forced.

**Bug 1: `tcp_match` (`drivers/net.c`) trusted a remote peer's own `ip->total_length` field with no check against how many bytes the frame actually carried.** Found by tracing what a malformed/hostile inbound TCP segment could do to every caller (`tcp_get`, `tcp_probe_port`, `tcp_serve_once`): `ip_total` is entirely attacker-controlled, and `out_paylen = ip_total - ip_hlen - tcp_hlen` was handed straight to callers, which then copied that many bytes starting at `out_payload`. A peer claiming `ip_total=1500` in a real 54-byte frame produced an `out_paylen` ~1440 bytes too large, an out-of-bounds read past the real `rx[1514]` frame buffer and into whatever stack memory happened to follow it, an uninitialized-memory-disclosure risk on top of the OOB read itself (that stack memory gets copied into the caller's response buffer, which callers display/consume). Fixed with one bound in the single shared function every TCP path already funnels through: reject any frame whose own header claims more bytes than `rtl8139_receive` actually delivered (`(u32)sizeof(struct eth_header) + ip_total > n`). Root cause, not a per-call-site patch, per this project's own standing rule (grepped every caller before touching `tcp_match`, all three already share the fix). Test: `tcpmatchtest` (`kernel/kernel.c`, `drivers/net.c`'s `tcp_match_selftest`), no NIC/network needed, builds a real Eth+IP+TCP frame in a local buffer and calls the exact production `tcp_match()` twice, once honest (accepted) and once with a lying `ip->total_length` (must now be rejected). Discrimination proven directly: reverted the bound, rebuilt, `tools/shell-run.sh tcpmatchtest` printed `tcpmatch: FAILED`; restored it, same command printed `tcpmatch: honest frame accepted, lying frame rejected: ok`.

**Bug 2: `dir_get_sector` (`drivers/fat.c`) hung forever on a subdirectory whose FAT cluster chain cycles instead of properly ending.** Real corruption risk, not just a hostile-input one: a write interrupted between "allocate the next cluster" and "link the previous cluster to it" (an abnormal shutdown mid-write, exactly the class of corruption this bug-hunt pass was asked to look for) leaves precisely this shape, a cluster whose own FAT entry points back into its own chain instead of hitting the `<2`/`>=0xFFF8` end-of-chain marker. Every one of the five callers (`fat_list`, `find_entry_in`, `find_free_slot`, `fat_delete`, `fat_chdir("..")`) walks a directory by calling this with an ever-increasing index until it returns 0, and a cycling chain never produces that 0, so `ls`/`cd`/any FAT op into a corrupted subdirectory spun forever, a real denial-of-service hang, not a crash. Fixed with a shared `MAX_DIR_CLUSTER_HOPS` sanity cap (4, root-caused: this kernel's own `fat_mkdir` never grows a directory past one cluster, so every directory this kernel has ever created needs zero hops; the cap only matters for an externally-created or corrupted multi-cluster chain, and `dir_get_sector`'s existing per-call walk-from-head design makes total scan cost quadratic in the cap, so a larger cap trades "hangs forever" for "still grinds for tens of seconds," measured directly: 16 was still visibly working after 45 real seconds against this kernel's own QEMU/ATA path, 4 finishes in a couple). Test: `tools/checks/fatcyclehang-check.sh`, a real FAT16 disk image (`tools/mkdisk.sh`), a real subdirectory created through the kernel's own `mkdir`, then the raw image patched on the host (standing in for the abnormal-shutdown corruption this kernel can't itself produce) so that directory's one cluster is filled with non-terminating fake entries and its own FAT entry points at itself; boots against the corrupted image, runs `cd`/`ls` into it, and checks a marker command sent immediately after actually executes within a bounded wait. Discrimination proven directly: reverted the cap, rebuilt, `ls` never returned within the wait (`FAIL: ls never returned`); restored it, `PASS: ls returned from a cycling FAT chain instead of hanging`.

**Verified.** `./check.sh`: PASS. Full shell regression (`tools/shell-run.sh heaptest tasktest preempttest reaptest ring3test killtest tcpmatchtest`): all pass. `tools/checks/check-refs.sh`: clean, 476 references resolve. `tools/checks/ramfs-demo-check.sh` (adjacent VFS path): PASS, unaffected.

## v0.71.9, icon sharpness pass: the Trash can under a real 10x zoom (Sep 2026)

Standing loop item ("keep sharpening the dock icons"). This pass did what the earlier "re-checked" entry said the next real step had to be: look at actual pixels, not the code. New `tools/checks/iconedge-check.py` boots headless, `pmemsave`s the real 1920x1080 framebuffer (the same QMP shape as `iconhalo-check.py`), crops the dock row, writes a nearest-neighbour 4x PNG, and scores every tile's second-difference luminance energy (a smooth AA ramp scores low, a step spikes). Trash scored highest of the ten (221239) and Apps second (187783); the other eight sat between 40k and 80k. Apps is high because a 3x3 grid is simply many axis-aligned edges, not jaggies, so it was left alone. Trash, zoomed 10x, showed two real defects, both in `gui_icon_trash`, neither guessed:

1. **Three grey stubs under the base.** The ribs were punched in tile colour to within one supersample row of the base, so the shadow pass (same glyph drawn first in 25%-black at +1,+2 physical px) left its own ribs poking out below the real body as three mid-grey blocks. Real pixel values, tile row 57, rib columns: luminance 85 (shadow grey) where the base should read 255. Ribs now stop `s/12` above the base, so the shadow's ribs stay under the real body, and the can gets the solid base rim a real trash icon has. After: rows 53-57 read 255 across the whole width.
2. **A staircased taper.** Both slanted sides were drawn with an integer width per supersample row (`half - row*(half/5)/(h-lid)`), stepping one supersample pixel every ~9 rows; the 3x3 box filter turned that into a half-tone stair pattern down both sides. The taper now runs in 24.8 fixed point and paints one fractional edge pixel per row (`gui_lerp(bg, ICON_FG, frac, 256)`), the same per-pixel box-filter idea the tray corner already uses, no new technique. Honest measurement: the physical edge-pixel value sequence down the left side still saw-tooths (that's a slanted line crossing physical pixel columns, inherent), but the intra-step zigzags the integer stair produced (118, 88, 119, 95 on consecutive rows before) are gone (114, 85, 54, 57, 67, 73 after, monotone within each step). 111 pixels changed between the two captures, 2 per row along the taper, 11 per row in the base band. Small, real, visible in the 10x crop.

## v0.72.1: manual security review (rtl8139_receive's unclamped return value, real OOB read one layer under the already-fixed tcp_match bug)

A manual, focused security review, not the `claude-security` skill (unavailable in this environment, needs the Workflow tool this session doesn't have) and not an automated multi-agent audit: one engineer reading `kernel/syscall.c`, `kernel/ring3.c`, `drivers/png.c`, `drivers/jpeg.c`, `drivers/net.c`/`http.c`/`rtl8139.c`, and `drivers/fat.c`/`vfs.c` against the same real-input threat model the earlier bug-hunt pass tonight used (untrusted map tiles, untrusted TCP/HTTP peers, a corruptible disk image, ring-3 user code). Confirmed both of that pass's fixes are still in place and effective: `tcp_match`'s `ip_total` bound (`net.c` ~line 414, `tcpmatchtest` still passes: honest frame accepted, lying frame rejected) and `fat.c`'s `dir_get_sector` cycle cap (`fatcyclehang-check.sh` still PASSes: `ls` returns from a cycling FAT chain instead of hanging).

**Real finding: `rtl8139_receive` (`drivers/rtl8139.c`) returned the NIC's raw, unclamped claimed frame length instead of how many bytes it actually copied into the caller's buffer.** Found by re-tracing `tcp_match`'s own fix comment ("the actual number of bytes `rtl8139_receive` put in rx") against what `rtl8139_receive` literally returned: `data_len = (u32)length - 4` (the RTL8139 ring header's own length field, minus the CRC), handed straight back as the return value, while the copy loop directly above it only ever wrote `copy_len = min(data_len, maxlen)` bytes into the caller's buffer. Every one of the six call sites in `net.c` (`tcp_get`, `tcp_probe_port`, `tcp_serve_once`, plus ARP/UDP paths) passes `sizeof(rx)` (1514) as `maxlen` and trusts the return value as "how many bytes are actually valid in my `rx[1514]`" -- which is exactly the invariant `tcp_match`'s just-fixed bound check (`sizeof(eth)+ip_total>n`) depends on being true. A frame whose NIC-reported ring length exceeds 1514 (a corrupted/oversized length field) made `rtl8139_receive` claim far more valid bytes than it had written, silently defeating that exact check one layer down and reopening the identical class of out-of-bounds stack read the `tcp_match` fix was written to close -- `tcp_match` would then treat bytes past the real `rx[1514]` array as legitimate IP/TCP payload. A length field under 4 (a runt/corrupt frame, the ring format assumes at least 4 bytes for the trailing CRC) made it worse: `data_len` underflowed to roughly 4 billion, guaranteeing the claim exceeded `maxlen` every time.

Root cause fix, one shared function (grepped every caller first, all six route through this one function, same discipline as the earlier `tcp_match` fix): the length/maxlen clamp was pulled out into a pure, hardware-free helper, `rtl8139_clamp_len(u16 length, u32 maxlen)`, which also guards the sub-4 underflow (`length >= 4 ? length - 4 : 0`), and `rtl8139_receive` now returns `copy_len` (what it actually wrote), not the raw claimed `data_len`.

Test: `rxclamptest` (`kernel/kernel.c`, `drivers/rtl8139.c`'s new `rtl8139_clamp_selftest`), no NIC/hardware needed since the clamp logic was extracted into pure software, same pattern `tcpmatchtest` already established for `tcp_match`. Three cases: an oversized claim (9000) must clamp to `maxlen` (1514), an honest small frame (64) must return 60 (64-4), a runt frame (2, shorter than the CRC itself) must return 0, not underflow. Discrimination proven directly: reverted the clamp to the raw pre-fix return (`return (u32)length - 4;`, unclamped), rebuilt, `tools/shell-run.sh rxclamptest` printed `rxclamp: FAILED`; restored the fix, same command printed `rxclamp: oversized/runt NIC lengths clamp correctly: ok`.

`kernel/syscall.c`/`kernel/ring3.c` (the int 0x80 gate): came back clean. `sys_write` is the only syscall handler that takes a ring-3 pointer/length pair, and it already validates both against the page tables (`paging_user_range_ok`) before touching them, backed by its own discriminating test (`ring3test badptr` in `kernel/ring3.c`, still passing). The IDT gate DPL (`0xEE`) is correctly set to ring 3. No other syscall reads a ring-3 pointer today.

`drivers/png.c`/`drivers/jpeg.c` (untrusted map tiles): came back clean. Both already carry the same overflow-guard discipline the RFC-cited comments describe: PNG's `stride`/`raw_len` are checked for multiplication overflow before either `kmalloc`, IHDR width/height are capped at 16384, every chunk length is bounds-checked against the buffer before use, and PLTE indices are range-checked against the real palette rather than clamped. JPEG's MCU/plane sizing carries the identical overflow-guard pattern, SOF sampling factors and DQT/DHT table sizes are all bounds-checked before use, and progressive/arithmetic/multi-scan variants are rejected outright rather than half-supported.

`drivers/http.c`: came back clean on the specific worry (trusting `Content-Length`) -- it doesn't parse or trust that header at all; it copies `min(actual bytes tcp_get returned past the header split, caller's body_maxlen)`, so a lying `Content-Length` can't cause an overread.

Verified: `./check.sh` PASS, `tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean (515 references), `tools/checks/fatcyclehang-check.sh` PASS, `tcpmatchtest` and `rxclamptest` both `ok`, `make kernel.elf` clean (pre-existing unrelated warnings only), `landing/v86/kernel.elf` auto-synced (identical MD5 to `kernel.elf`, confirming the new Makefile rule works). PATCH bump 0.72.0 -> 0.72.1, a fix with no new capability.

`iconedge-check.py` asserts the base band (rows 55-57, x 24-52 of the Trash tile) is solid glyph white. Discriminating: the same assertion on the pre-fix capture fails with 27/87 non-white pixels; the post-fix build passes 0/87. `./check.sh` PASS, `iconhalo-check.py` PASS, `make` clean. PATCH bump 0.71.8 -> 0.71.9, no new capability. Headless throughout, no QEMU window opened.

## v0.72.3, real menu bar icon bug: AA halo wider than the geometry itself (Sep 2026)

Direct user report ("top left icon in menu bar doesn't look much like a Joshua tree"). Not a subjective call, found and confirmed with a real pixel dump: `gui_draw_logo`'s branch capsules are drawn via `gui_draw_capsule`, which softens every edge with `aa_band` (a fixed 5px halo, never scaled to the primitive it's softening). At menubar scale (`scale=1`), each branch capsule is only 4-7px long with `r=0` (`r = scale > 1 ? scale - 1 : 0`), so a 5px halo on both sides of a 4-7px shape is wider than the shape, every branch's halo overlapped its neighbors', and the whole logo collapsed into two blurry pink blobs, not a tree at all (a real 8x pixel crop before the fix showed exactly that, attached evidence, not assumed).

Fix: `gui_draw_logo` now temporarily shrinks `aa_band` to 1 for the `scale == 1` case only (same override pattern `gui_render_icon_cached` already uses for its own scale, not a new technique), restoring the saved value after. A real 8x pixel crop after the fix shows a trunk and five real branches, unmistakably a tree.

`./check.sh` PASS. PATCH bump 0.72.2 -> 0.72.3, bug fix only. Headless throughout (`-display none` + QMP `pmemsave`), no QEMU window opened, per the standing "verify headless, only relaunch the visible app if asked" rule.

## v0.73.0: real multi-window, phase 1 of the honestly-scoped 3-5 session plan (Sep 2026)

Direct request, phase 1 of the "Multi-window, honestly scoped" entry earlier in this file: a real window list, and two real windows genuinely open and drawn simultaneously, each with its own titlebar/chrome, at least one focused for input, the rest of the multi-session plan (real z-order compositing, click-to-focus hit-testing, converting every app off its own blocking input loop) explicitly deferred.

**Architecture read first, as required.** `gui_launch_from_dock` (`kernel/kernel.c`) draws one fixed chrome rect (x=70,y=40,w=820,h=385, or the Apps folder's larger rect) straight into the shared framebuffer, sets `window_set_viewport` to the content rect, then calls `gui_launch(icon)`, which for every dock app ends in `gui_wait_close()` -- a real blocking loop (`for(;;){ ...; hlt; }`) that only returns once its own click/esc fires. `gui_run`'s main loop calls `gui_launch_from_dock` synchronously from inside its own event handling and does not run again until that call returns, so a second dock click physically cannot be processed while the first app is open: the kernel is inside the first app's own poll loop, not back in `gui_run`. `window_open_scaled(960,540,32,2)` is called exactly once, at the top of `gui_run`, confirming the roadmap's "one single framebuffer surface" finding. This is why multi-window needs a real architectural change, not a chrome tweak: the loop that would draw a second window is the same loop currently frozen inside the first window's own blocking call.

**What shipped.** A real window list (`gui_window_t{icon,x,y,w,h}`, `gui_windows[GUI_MULTIWIN_MAX]` with `GUI_MULTIWIN_MAX=2`, `gui_window_count`) plus a non-blocking open/close/draw path, all in `kernel/kernel.c` next to `gui_launch_from_dock` (not `drivers/window.c` as the original roadmap sketch suggested -- `window.c` is the framebuffer/viewport primitive layer with no concept of "which app owns this rect", and the app-dispatch table this needed to key off of already lives in `kernel.c`; moving app dispatch into the driver layer for this pass would've been an unrequested abstraction). Two apps are wired to it: Files and Weather, the simplest `gui_wait_close`-shaped read-only viewers, exactly the "batch 1" grouping the original roadmap entry proposed. Both were split into a content-only draw function (`gui_draw_files_content`/`gui_draw_weather_content`, no blocking call inside) plus their old entry point (`gui_launch_files`/`gui_launch_weather`, now just `gui_draw_*_content(); gui_wait_close();`, unchanged for the Apps-folder/test-harness single-window path). `gui_multiwin_open(icon)` adds/focuses a window in the list and returns immediately -- `gui_run`'s own loop keeps running, so the dock stays live and a second dock click for the other supported app opens a real second window without touching the first. `gui_multiwin_draw_all()` runs from the exact same full-repaint branch `gui_run` already used for the menu/notif/weather overlay panels, so every open window is genuinely redrawn -- from its own live state (`vfs_list`, `weather_text`), not a cached bitmap -- on top of the desktop on every real repaint.

Window 0 keeps the exact rect the pre-existing single-window model always used (x=70,y=40,w=820,h=385), so opening Files or Weather alone is pixel-identical to before and every existing test that hard-codes the close hitbox at (94,56) still passes unmodified. A second, concurrently-open window is offset (x=130,y=100) so both titlebars and both close buttons are fully visible and distinguishable -- the naive back-to-front compositing this phase settles for, no real z-order/overlap math.

**Focus, deliberately simple (point 5 of the request).** Opening a window focuses it (moves it to the end of the list = drawn last = topmost). Only the focused window is interactive at all: `gui_multiwin_focused_click_hit` tests a click against the topmost window's full rect only, matching the exact "any click in the app view closes it" contract `gui_wait_close` already used, just scoped to one list entry. Clicking the background window does nothing yet -- real click-to-focus hit-testing against every open window is explicitly phase 2, not attempted.

**Closing.** Each window's own X (really: any click inside its own rect, same as the old single-window contract) closes only that window; `gui_multiwin_close` removes it from the list and the other window, if any, stays open and drawn, proven directly by the new test below (close Weather, screenshot shows Files still fully intact) and the real screenshot evidence below.

**Real pixel evidence**, per this session's own non-negotiable bar: headless QMP absolute-pointer input opened Files (dock slot 1), then Weather (dock slot 8) *without* closing Files, and a `pmemsave` of the real framebuffer while both were open was saved to `/tmp/joshuatree_multiwindow_v073.png` and inspected directly -- both windows' real chrome (two distinct titlebars, two distinct sets of traffic lights at their real offset positions) and both windows' real content (Files' file listing, Weather's real gradient panel + "Vancouver"/"Weather unavailable" text) are visibly on screen at the same time, at their real, distinct, non-overlapping-enough-to-hide-either positions. Not a fake/cosmetic result: this is the literal thing the roadmap called out as the risk to avoid.

**New discriminating test**: `tools/checks/multiwindow-check.py` (the `appclose-check.py`/`dockhover-check.py` QMP+pmemsave shape). Opens Files, confirms its close button (single-window compatibility check), opens Weather without closing Files, asserts *both* windows' close buttons are on screen at once at their real distinct coordinates (94,56) and (154,116), and samples Weather's real gradient-panel colour at its own content rect to prove real content, not a blank/frozen window. Then closes Weather via its own X and asserts Files is untouched, closes Files and asserts a clean desktop, then re-opens/closes Mail to prove input wasn't left dead. Proven discriminating by running it against the pre-fix `kernel.c` (`git stash` of this pass's kernel.c change, same build/test/restore cycle, no `--force`/`reset`): the pre-fix kernel fails with `"Weather's own window never appeared while Files was open"` and `"closing Weather also closed/corrupted Files"` (opening Weather while Files was open just closed Files instead, since the second click landed inside Files' own blocking `gui_wait_close` loop, exactly the "any click closes" trap the architecture read above predicted) -- confirming this test would have caught the exact regression it exists to prevent, then passes cleanly with the fix restored.

**Full regression suite**, all headless, no QEMU window opened: `./check.sh` PASS; `tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean (540 references); `tools/checks/appclose-check.py` PASS (all ten dock slots, including Files and Weather, open and close exactly as before -- single-window behaviour is pixel-compatible); `tools/checks/launchpad-click-check.py` PASS; `tools/checks/multiwindow-check.py` PASS (new). `tools/checks/dockhover-check.py` and `tools/checks/app-interact-check.py` (its Contacts/`CONTACTS.TXT` disk-write assertion specifically) both fail on pristine `origin/main` *before* this change too, confirmed by stashing this pass's `kernel.c` edit and re-running each against the untouched baseline -- both are real, pre-existing, unrelated failures (dock-hover lift timing and a Contacts VFS-save gap), not regressions from this pass, and out of phase 1's scope to fix.

**Security-minded look (MINOR-bump requirement, 4f).** The new code touches input routing (which window a click reaches) but not any ring-3/privilege boundary -- every app in this kernel, including the two wired here, still runs in kernel mode with no separate address space or capability from any other GUI code path; `gui_multiwin_focused_click_hit` only decides which *window rect* a click is tested against, the same class of comparison `gui_dock_hit_test`/`gui_menu_hit_test` already do. `gui_window_count` is bounds-checked against `GUI_MULTIWIN_MAX` before every array write (`gui_multiwin_open`'s early return, `gui_multiwin_close`'s index guard), so no out-of-bounds write is reachable from input alone. No new untrusted-data parsing was added; Files/Weather's content draw functions are unchanged from their pre-existing single-window versions. No real security-relevant boundary found to newly harden.

**Deliberately deferred, not attempted here** (see the original "Multi-window, honestly scoped" entry for the full ordered list this pass advances): real z-order/overlap compositing (today's naive back-to-front draw order is the only "z-order" that exists); real click-to-focus hit-testing against a background window; converting any app beyond Files/Weather off its own blocking input loop (Mail/Calendar/Notes/Reminders/Terminal/Chat keep the old single-window `gui_launch_from_dock` path entirely untouched); per-window backing stores (both windows still draw straight into the shared framebuffer through `window_set_viewport`, same as the old single-window model, just twice); a real 4-6 window cap (capped at 2, exactly what this phase needed to prove); mixing an old-path app open concurrently with a new-path window (untested, undefined visually, not required by this pass's scope). Next session's job, per the original entry's ordering: either grow the supported-app set (batch 2: Reminders/Calendar/Mail's `sleep_ticks`+`get_key_or_click` shape) or tackle real click-to-focus, whichever the next direct request prioritizes.

MINOR bump: 0.72.3 -> 0.73.0, a real new capability (two apps genuinely open and drawn at once, not just tested/scaffolded).

## v0.73.1: satellite wallpaper wired, real code path proven, end-to-end pixels blocked by this test environment's DNS (Sep 2026)

Direct request: wire `wall_fetch`'s Satellite theme onto the JPEG decoder that shipped in v0.72.0 (`drivers/jpeg.c`) but was never actually plugged into anything. Real, scoped feature-wiring, not a decoder rewrite.

**What shipped, real and structurally verified:**
- `WALL_SAT` (4), a fifth real `wall_theme` state alongside Photo/Warm/Cool/Raw, not a parallel system: same `settings_load`/`settings_save` persistence (clamp widened to `WALL_PHOTO..WALL_SAT`), same Settings-screen cycle (`% 4` -> `% 5`), same `gui_wall_tint` dispatch (Satellite isn't Cool or Raw, so it falls through to the existing `gui_map_tint` Warm grade with zero new tint code, exactly like the direct request expected), same shell surface (`wallpaper sat`/`wallpaper satellite`).
- `wall_fetch` now branches by theme: Warm/Cool/Raw keep fetching `a.tile.opentopomap.org/{z}/{x}/{y}.png` and decoding with `png_decode`; Satellite fetches `mt0.google.com/vt/lyrs=s&x={x}&y={y}&z={z}` (real plain-HTTP endpoint, curl-confirmed both from the host and, separately, its JPEG signature/dimensions verified with `file`: `256x256, baseline, precision 8, 3 components`) and decodes with `jpeg_decode`, mirroring `png_decode`'s exact call shape and error handling (`wallerr=jpeg <code>` on a bad tile, same clean fail-and-keep-the-photo contract PNG already has, no crash path). Google was picked over Bing's `virtualearth.net`, verified by hand rather than assumed: Google's tile URL uses the identical slippy-map x/y/z convention this kernel's tile math already computes, Bing's quadkey addressing would have needed new math.
- **Real cross-source bug caught and fixed before this shipped**, not assumed away: Warm/Cool/Raw all share one `wall_map` buffer and just re-tint it (cheap theme switching, no refetch), but Satellite's pixels come from a genuinely different source, not a different grade of the same pixels. Switching from Warm/Cool/Raw straight to Satellite (or back) without dropping the stale buffer would either paint topo pixels under a "Satellite" label or vice versa. Fixed with `wall_map_is_sat` (tracks which real source is currently in `wall_map`) and `wall_switch_theme()` (the one choke point every setter -- four shell branches plus the Settings-screen cycle -- now goes through), which drops `wall_map` and forces a refetch only when crossing the sat/topo boundary, a no-op the rest of the time.
- `walltest`'s existing dispatch/round-trip self-test extended to cover the fifth state (`wall_dispatch_sat == warm_g` proves Satellite really does fall through to Warm's grade with no special case; the settings round-trip loop now iterates `WALL_PHOTO..WALL_SAT`).

**What's proven vs not, real evidence either way:**
- Topo/Warm/Cool/Raw: fully proven, unaffected. `tools/checks/wallpaper-check.py` still PASSes byte-for-byte against a fresh host decode of the same twelve OpenTopoMap tiles (fnv `9a559b9d` both sides) and on screen tinted correctly. (One real pre-existing bug fixed in passing to even get this proof: the script's own `ZOOM` constant was still `12`, stale since `WALL_ZOOM` went to 14 in v78/0.67.2 -- confirmed via `git log -S` that `WALL_ZOOM 14` predates this session entirely, not something this pass changed. One-line fix, `ZOOM=14`, script now agrees with the kernel it's testing.) `tools/checks/wallfx-check.py` still PASSes (particles composite over the fetched map). `jpeg-check.sh`/`jpeg-host-check.sh`: PASS, unchanged, the decoder itself wasn't touched.
- Satellite end-to-end: **not proven on screen.** `tools/checks/satellite-wallpaper-check.py` (written this pass, same host-vs-kernel FNV-compare shape as `wallpaper-check.py`, plus a pixel-stddev discriminator against the topo mosaic to prove real photographic texture once it does land) reproducibly FAILs at the network step: `wallpaper sat` + `wallpaper fetch`, typed into the shell via the same QMP key-injection `wallfx-check.py` already established works, reaches `wall_fetch` with `use_sat=1` correctly set (confirmed with temporary serial instrumentation, since removed) and calls `http_get("mt0.google.com", ...)`, which fails with `wallerr=http 0` -- `dns_resolve` times out on the very first tile. Isolated with a temporary in-kernel DNS probe (also since removed): `mt0.google.com`, its own CNAME target `mt.l.google.com`, `www.wikipedia.org`, and Bing's `ecn.t0.tiles.virtualearth.net` **all** time out identically via this kernel's real UDP:53 resolver, while the three domains this kernel already depended on before tonight (`a.tile.opentopomap.org`, `api.open-meteo.com`, `ip-api.com`) resolve instantly and correctly, every time, in the same boot. That's a real, reproducible, environment-level DNS restriction in this specific test sandbox (its outbound network evidently only permits UDP DNS to a small pre-existing allowlist, even though the same major domains answer fine to a plain `curl` from the host shell one layer up, a different network path than QEMU SLIRP's). Not a bug in this session's code: the same `dns_resolve`/`http_get`/`tcp_get` functions these new tiles route through are the exact ones `wallpaper-check.py` just proved correct for OpenTopoMap, unmodified by this pass.
- Per the task's own contingency for this exact situation: a structurally-correct, unit-verified code path is the honest stopping point here, not a false "it works" claim without the pixel proof. `satellite-wallpaper-check.py` stays in the suite as real, permanent infrastructure (it will PASS the moment this runs somewhere DNS to Google resolves, e.g. real hardware/unrestricted QEMU, not just once this pass) rather than being deleted or weakened to fake a pass.
- Native-QEMU-vs-browser-demo note (v86/NE2000, per this pass's own instructions): not blocked on it either way. This failure is a DNS-layer sandbox restriction in *this* environment, orthogonal to which NIC emulation is in play; it would reproduce identically under a real NE2000 driver in the browser demo, and would very likely resolve fine on real hardware or an unrestricted QEMU host where `mt0.google.com` answers like any other domain.
- Security look (the one new untrusted-input boundary this pass adds, per the MINOR-bundling rule -- downgraded to PATCH below since the feature isn't proven end-to-end, but the boundary still got the look): the satellite path hands `jpeg_decode` the exact same treatment `png_decode` already gets from a network-fetched tile -- same `w/h/ch` validation against `WALL_TILE`/3 before any pixel copy, same `kfree`-on-every-failure-path discipline, same fixed 65536-byte `http_get` cap regardless of source. `drivers/jpeg.c` itself wasn't touched this pass (already reviewed in the v0.72.1 security pass: overflow-guarded MCU/plane sizing, bounds-checked DQT/DHT/SOF, progressive/arithmetic/multi-scan rejected outright), so no new decoder-side surface, only a new caller of an already-hardened one.

PATCH bump 0.73.0 -> 0.73.1, not MINOR: real new code path shipped and unit/regression-proven, but the headline capability (satellite imagery actually on screen) isn't proven end-to-end in this environment, so it doesn't clear this project's own "MINOR needs a real working new capability" bar yet. Next real step when this is picked back up somewhere with unrestricted DNS: rerun `satellite-wallpaper-check.py`, confirm the PASS, take the Mojave-palette screenshot, then the MINOR bump is earned.

Verified: `./check.sh` PASS, `jpeg-check.sh` PASS, `jpeg-host-check.sh` PASS, `wallpaper-check.py` PASS (topo, byte-for-byte), `wallfx-check.py` PASS. `satellite-wallpaper-check.py` FAILs honestly (real DNS timeout, documented above), left in the suite. `make kernel.elf` clean (two pre-existing unused-function warnings only), `landing/v86/kernel.elf` auto-synced. Rebased onto `v0.73.0` (multi-window phase 1, landed concurrently by another session), no conflicts between the two features.

## v0.73.2, satellite wallpaper: real fix for Google's 403 bot-block, real deeper blocker found (Sep 2026)

Continued the v0.73.1 satellite-wallpaper investigation, this time from the same machine that already has working weather/topo wallpaper networking (not the previous session's sandboxed environment, which hit a DNS restriction specific to that sandbox).

**Real bug found and fixed**: `http_get` (`drivers/http.c`) sent literally no `User-Agent` header on any request, ever. OpenTopoMap, Open-Meteo and ip-api all tolerate that; Google's tile endpoint (`mt0.google.com/vt/lyrs=s`) doesn't, real evidence: a raw `nc` replay of this kernel's exact request bytes against the real server got a 403 "unusual traffic" bot page; the identical request with one added `User-Agent: JoshuaTree/1.0` header got a real 200 with a real 23KB JPEG body. Fixed by adding that header to every `http_get` request (harmless to the three existing hosts, verified: `wallpaper-check.py`'s existing topo test still passes byte-for-byte, fnv `9a559b9d` both sides).

**Real deeper blocker found, not fixed, documented honestly**: even with the 403 fixed, `tcp_get` still returns 0 bytes for the satellite fetch. Traced with real temporary serial instrumentation (added, verified, then reverted, not shipped): the TCP handshake (SYN-ACK) and the connection's final FIN both arrive and match cleanly, but *zero* of the data-carrying segments in between are ever seen by `tcp_match`, not rejected by its length-bound check (zero `tcpmatch_reject` lines), not dropped as out-of-order (zero `tcpget_oo` lines), just never delivered to it at all. Google's real response is ~24.5KB, roughly 17 back-to-back MSS-sized segments; OpenTopoMap's larger (43KB) responses already work fine in the same boot, so raw byte count isn't the variable. The real suspect, not yet proven: `drivers/rtl8139.c`'s RX ring/`BUFE`-polling loop losing frames under a fast multi-segment burst from a genuinely remote host, a distinct bug class from tonight's earlier `rtl8139_receive` length-clamp fix (v0.72.1), which was about trusting a claimed length, not about missed frames. Needs real pcap-level tracing of the RX ring under load to confirm, out of scope to chase further tonight per budget discipline.

`WALL_SAT` stays wired and selectable (Settings, shell command), still honestly non-functional until the RX-burst issue is found. `./check.sh`, `wallpaper-check.py`, `check-refs.sh` all pass, no regression. PATCH bump 0.73.1 -> 0.73.2 (a real fix, User-Agent, but the headline capability is still not proven end to end, matching the same "MINOR needs the capability proven" rule the previous pass used).

## v0.73.3: NE2000 driver (`drivers/ne2k.c`) unlocks real browser networking, closing the "fetch-mode network relay" dead end

Direct request: this session's "fetch-mode network relay" investigation (above) correctly identified the real, unconditional blocker as `rtl8139.c` being this kernel's only NIC driver while v86 never emulates RTL8139 hardware at all, only NE2000 (`ne2k`) and virtio-net, and called porting an ne2k driver "a real, scoped new-driver project" out of that session's scope. This pass did that project, end to end, with real evidence of live browser networking, not just a driver that compiles.

**The driver.** `drivers/ne2k.c`/`.h`, mirroring `rtl8139.c`'s shape (init/probe via PCI, MAC accessor, send, receive) but a structurally different chip: 8390-family register set (command register, page-select, remote-DMA start/count/address, RX/TX config), and a page-addressed ring buffer living entirely inside the card's *own* internal memory rather than DMA'd into host RAM, so there's no `KVIRT_TO_PHYS` translation to worry about here at all, unlike `rtl8139.c`. PCI identification uses a new `pci_find_device_vid` (`drivers/pci.c`), matching an exact (vendor, device) pair rather than (class, subclass): RTL8139 and the RTL8029 (NE2000-compatible) clone QEMU/v86 emulate share the identical PCI class/subclass (0x02/0x00, "network controller/ethernet"), so the existing class-based `pci_find_device` alone can't tell them apart. The vendor/device IDs (0x10EC/0x8029) were pulled straight out of `landing/v86/libv86.js`'s own `pci_space` table (`this.pci_space=[236,16,41,128,...]`, i.e. bytes 0xEC,0x10,0x29,0x80 little-endian), not assumed from general NE2000 datasheet knowledge, per the task's own instruction to verify against the real emulator source.

**Wiring.** `drivers/net.c`'s `net_init(ip)` now owns NIC discovery itself: tries `rtl8139_init()` first (real hardware, native QEMU's `-device rtl8139` path), falls back to `ne2k_init()` (v86, and QEMU's own `ne2k_pci` model) if no RTL8139 turns up, and sets a small `active_send`/`active_receive` function-pointer pair so every one of `net.c`'s own call sites (`arp_resolve`, `udp_send`, `tcp_send_segment`, every `*_receive` poll loop) is hardware-agnostic in fact, not just in spirit. Every `kernel.c`/`chat.h` call site that used to do `if (!rtl8139_init()) ... else { net_init(ip); ... }` collapsed to a single `if (!net_init(ip))`, since `net_init` now does both jobs. `nettest`/`ifconfig`'s own low-level diagnostics were also switched off hardcoded `rtl8139_get_mac`/`rtl8139_send` calls onto new generic `net_get_mac`/`net_send_raw` wrappers, so they exercise whichever NIC is actually present instead of assuming RTL8139.

**Real bug #1, the same length-trust class the earlier `rtl8139_receive` OOB fix (v0.72.1) closed, fixed the same rigorous way from the start:** `ne2k_receive`'s ring-header length is clamped through a pure, hardware-free `ne2k_clamp_len` (unit-tested, `ne2k_clamp_selftest`, mirroring `rtl8139_clamp_selftest` exactly), never trusting the NIC's own reported length past the caller's real buffer size, and guarding the same sub-4/CRC-strip underflow rtl8139's fix guarded.

**Real bug #2, found live and headless against v86, not guessable from a datasheet:** DCR (set here to 0x49, word-transfer mode) means every access to the data port, even an explicit byte-sized `inb`/`outb`, actually moves two bytes internally and advances the card's own remote-DMA pointer by two, not one, confirmed directly by reading v86's own `data_port_read8`/`data_port_write16` (`libv86.js`), which is accurately emulating real word-mode 8390 hardware behavior. The driver originally used single-byte `inb`/`outb` throughout `remote_read`/`remote_write`, silently corrupting and desyncing every PROM/packet transfer by exactly half: read evidence was the PROM MAC coming back wrong until fixed (`ne2k mac=00:22:15:...` matched v86's own `this.mac=[0,34,21,...]` array only after switching to `inw`/`outw`). Fixed by pairing two bytes per `inw`/`outw` access, the documented, standard requirement for any real word-mode NE2000-family card, not a v86-only quirk.

**Real bug #3, an actual off-by-one in the ring-buffer math, found live and headless, the class of bug this project's testing standard exists to catch before it ships:** the RX ring's oldest unread packet lives at page `BNRY + 1`, never at `BNRY` itself (BNRY is documented as "one page behind the next unread packet", standard 8390 semantics; confirmed directly against v86's own `Mc.prototype.receive`, which writes each incoming packet starting at the pre-advance `CURR` page, one page ahead of `BNRY` by the driver's own init). `ne2k_receive` originally compared the NIC's write cursor against `rx_bnry` directly and read the packet header from `rx_bnry*256`, both off by one page. Real evidence of the bug: `rx_bnry` started at `RX_START_PAGE` (0x46) and `CURR` at 0x47 (the correct one-page init gap), so the old check reported "ring non-empty" on the very first poll, before anything had arrived, and the header read hit an unused all-zero page (`status=0x00 next=0x00 len=0x0000`), silently discarding the real ARP reply that landed one page later -- `arp_resolve` timed out every single time despite the NIC successfully transmitting. Fixed by reading from (and checking emptiness against) `BNRY + 1`, wrapping to `RX_START_PAGE` at `RX_STOP_PAGE`.

**Real bug #4, not in the driver at all: v86's emulated network has no real backend by default.** Even with a fully correct driver, `arp_resolve` still timed out until `landing/v86/embed.js`'s `V86` constructor was given `net_device: { type: "ne2k", relay_url: "fetch", vm_ip: "10.0.2.15", router_ip: "10.0.2.2" }`. Two real findings here, both confirmed by reading `libv86.js` itself, not assumed: (1) `network_relay_url`/`net_device.relay_url: "fetch"` selects v86's own browser-side relay (`Xb` in the vendored bundle), which accepts any TCP connection to port 80, reads the guest's raw HTTP request text straight off the wire, and answers it with a real `fetch()` to whatever `Host:` header the guest sent -- so this kernel's existing raw TCP/HTTP stack (`net.c`/`http.c`) needed zero protocol changes, the relay meets it exactly where it already is. (2) `net_device` needs `type: "ne2k"` explicit: `libv86.js` only defaults `net_device` to `{type:"ne2k"}` when the caller passes no `net_device` object at all (`e.net_device=b.net_device||{type:"ne2k"}`); passing `net_device` with only `relay_url`/`vm_ip`/`router_ip` (an earlier attempt this pass made) silently dropped the emulated NIC from the PCI bus entirely, regressing `net_init` from "ne2k found" back to "no NIC found", caught immediately by rerunning the same test. `vm_ip`/`router_ip` matter too: `Xb`'s own defaults are `192.168.86.100`/`192.168.86.1`, but every address this kernel's stack hardcodes (`net_init`'s `0x0A00020F`, `net.c`'s `DEFAULT_GATEWAY_IP`) assumes QEMU SLIRP's `10.0.2.0/24`, the same subnet native `make run`/`geo-check.sh` already boot into; left at v86's own defaults the ARP request for the hardcoded gateway never matched and timed out identically to bug #3's symptom, a second real cause behind the same one failure mode, both had to be found and fixed before either showed through. DNS not mattering was also confirmed directly: `Xb`'s `dns_method: "static"` answers every A-record query with the same fake `192.168.87.1` regardless of hostname, by design, since the relay only reads the real target off the guest's own `Host:` header once the (fake-IP) TCP connection completes -- exactly why this needed no DNS-side workaround.

**This is the critical verification, real evidence, not "no crash":** `tools/checks/ne2k-check.mjs`, a new headless Playwright check (`landing/`, served locally, following the `tools/checks/*.mjs` pattern), boots the real `kernel.elf` in the real vendored v86/wasm build with no keyboard/mouse interaction at all, since the default `wall_theme` is `WALL_WARM` (a map theme, not `WALL_PHOTO`), so `gui_run`'s own idle loop calls `weather_fetch()` (which calls `net_init()`, then `geo_fetch()`, then `wall_fetch()`) automatically on the very first frame after the desktop draws. It polls the kernel's own serial log (exposed via `window.__jt.serial`, `embed.js`'s existing QA hook) for the exact real evidence `tools/checks/geo-check.sh` already asserts against native QEMU: `geo=<lat>,<lon>` (parsed from a real ip-api.com reply), `wxurl=<path>` (the real Open-Meteo URL built from it), `wx=<condition>` (the real forecast text). Final clean-build run (no debug tracing) landed all three real values through v86's NE2000 path:

```
geo=49.0983,-122.6498
wxurl=/v1/forecast?latitude=49.0983&longitude=-122.6498&current=temperature_2m,weather_code
wx=13ø Cloudy
PASS: real network fetch through v86 NE2000 succeeded
```

The same coordinates and forecast `tools/checks/geo-check.sh` gets natively (49.0983,-122.6498, "13ø Cloudy") landed through the browser demo for the first time ever, real end-to-end proof the browser networking gap is closed, not just that a driver compiles.

**Verified:** `./check.sh` PASS. `tools/checks/geo-check.sh` PASS (native RTL8139/QEMU path re-run after every driver/net.c change in this pass, confirmed unaffected throughout, same real ip-api.com/Open-Meteo evidence as before). `tools/shell-run.sh heaptest tasktest preempttest reaptest ring3test killtest tcpmatchtest rxclamptest`: all `ok`. `tools/checks/check-refs.sh`: clean, 540 references. `make kernel.elf`: clean (two pre-existing unused-function warnings in `kernel.c`, unrelated). `landing/v86/kernel.elf` auto-synced by the Makefile rule. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`/`README.md` updated with `ne2k.c`'s row per the docs-completeness standing rule. Security-relevant boundary check (this touches untrusted network data end to end, the MINOR-release standing requirement): `ne2k_receive`'s length clamp gets the same discipline and the same unit test shape as the already-reviewed `rtl8139_receive`/`tcp_match` fixes; no new syscall or ring-3-reachable surface added. MINOR bump: this pass started from 0.72.1, but `origin/main` had moved to 0.73.2 (real multi-window work + a satellite-wallpaper pass, unrelated files, clean rebase) by the time this landed, so the real tag is 0.73.3, not 0.73.0 -- still a MINOR bump, this is a genuinely new capability, real network access from the in-browser demo, not a fix.

## v0.73.4: real user bug, idle tour's ramfs never resets between loops ("adds like emails to the list, duplicate emails") -- partial fix, honestly not fully closed (Sep 2026)

Direct user report: "the landing page has its own persistent memory, every time it runs a demo and then restarts the demo, it adds like emails to the list, for example, so there's duplicate emails. Maybe we should make the memory reset when the demo resets."

**Root cause, confirmed by reading the code, not assumed**: this kernel's browser demo has no real disk (v0.71.0), so it falls back to an in-memory ramfs. The idle tour (`landing/v86/embed.js`, `tourLoop`, `TOUR_APPS`) composes a real email in Mail and adds a real Reminder each time it cycles through the 8 dock apps. The emulator was never rebooted between loops, only closed/reopened windows, so ramfs (and the mailbox/reminders list) accumulated across every full 8-app cycle, forever, for as long as a visitor left the tab open.

**Three attempts this pass, two reverted, documented honestly rather than glossed over:**

1. **`emulator.restart()` alone** (v86's `Q.prototype.restart` -> `S.prototype.reboot_internal`, a real CPU reset + BIOS reload). Looked like the fix and does perform a genuine reboot (confirmed: the tour visibly re-runs Files/Mail/Calendar/etc. from a fresh boot screen after each lap). But `reboot_internal` (read directly in the vendored `libv86.js`) never calls `S.prototype.reset_memory` (`this.mem8.fill(0)`), only resets CPU registers and a few specific devices.
