Proven live with a real 2-lap Playwright run (`node tools/checks/mailboxreset-check.mjs`, plus manual periodic-screenshot captures since the pixel-brightness heuristic that check relies on turned out too fragile against the newer, high-variance Mojave sunset wallpaper): lap 2's Files listing showed 6 files (README/NOTES/MAIL/EVENTS/REMINDERS/CHAT.TXT) where lap 1's showed 2 (README/NOTES.TXT), i.e. ramfs survived the "reboot" untouched.

2. **Kernel-side `.bss` zeroing in `boot.S`'s `_start`** (the standard freestanding-kernel fix: never assume RAM starts zero, zero it yourself before touching any static). This is real, generally-correct kernel hygiene -- passed `check.sh` and a real QEMU `system_reset` cleanly (`tools/shell-run.sh heaptest tasktest rxclamptest` all still `ok` after) -- but caused the v86 browser path specifically to hang after lap 1: confirmed with a live instruction-counter probe (`emulator.get_instruction_counter()` polled every 3s) that the CPU kept executing (~3.5 billion instructions counted, not frozen) while `vga.graphical_mode` never returned and the framebuffer never changed again for 90+ seconds. Some v86-specific interaction with the reboot's BIOS/option-ROM replay this session could not fully root-cause in the time available (real QEMU's `-kernel system_reset` apparently doesn't exercise the identical replay path v86's warm reset does), so this was reverted rather than shipped half-working. `boot.S`/`boot/linker.ld` are back to their pre-pass state.

3. **Shipped: `emulator.v86.cpu.reset_memory()` called immediately before `emulator.restart()`.** `emulator.v86.cpu` is the same live CPU object embed.js already reads elsewhere (`emulator.v86.cpu.devices.vga`), and it exposes `reset_memory` directly, the one call `reboot_internal` skips. This is the real root-cause fix, at the actual point of the gap (v86's own `restart()` never clearing memory), not a kernel workaround. Also added: `waitForGraphicalMode(gen, want, timeoutMs)`, reusing the same `vga.graphical_mode` signal the outer boot-detection `setInterval` already polls, so the tour waits for the kernel to actually reach its GUI again before clicking a dock icon post-reboot, instead of a fixed sleep-and-hope; the boot-logo overlay is unhidden before `restart()` so a mid-reboot black screen doesn't look broken; every wait is gated on `focused`/`tourGen` so a real visitor clicking in mid-restart still correctly interrupts (verified: no stuck-reboot state possible, the pre-existing 200ms boot-detection interval independently hides the boot logo again once `graphical_mode` returns, regardless of tour state).

**Honest result, not overstated**: real, live 2-lap verification (Playwright, `landing/v86/embed.js` deployed at `joshuatree.heyitsmejosh.com`, periodic screenshots every 5s) confirms the reboot mechanism itself now works reliably -- lap 2 completes a full, un-stuck 8-app cycle (Files -> Mail -> Calendar -> ... in order, same cadence as lap 1, no hang, no frozen CPU) -- a real improvement over the pre-pass behavior (which never rebooted at all) and over attempt 2 (which hung). **However, in this same verification run, lap 2's Files listing still showed the accumulated 6-file list from lap 1, not the fresh 2-file seed state.** `reset_memory()` is being called (confirmed via code review; `emulator.v86.cpu.reset_memory` resolves and executes without error) but the evidence does not prove ramfs content is actually clearing. Possible causes not yet investigated, for whoever picks this up next: the multiboot ELF payload/option-ROM injection may re-write into memory using cached JS-side state in a way that reintroduces stale bytes before the kernel's own init runs; or ramfs's directory structure may not actually live in the address range `reset_memory` zeroes at the point it's called (timing relative to `reboot_internal`'s own `load_bios()` call, which runs immediately after). This needs a follow-up pass with more targeted instrumentation (e.g. a serial-log breakpoint print inside `ramfs_init` comparing byte counts before/after, or reading `emulator.v86.cpu.mem8` directly from Playwright at the exact moment between `reset_memory()` and `restart()`) to actually pin down, not guessed at further here.

**Shipped anyway, per direct guidance mid-pass to stop iterating and report honestly**: the hang regression (attempt 2) is fully reverted and not present in what's deployed. The reboot-without-hanging behavior (attempt 3) is a real, verified improvement. The mailbox-duplication half of the original report is NOT proven fixed by this pass's evidence, and should be treated as still open.

Verified: `check.sh` PASS, `tools/shell-run.sh heaptest tasktest rxclamptest` all `ok` (post-revert kernel unchanged in behavior, only its embedded `JT_VERSION_STR` differs), `node --check landing/v86/embed.js` clean, `landing/v86/kernel.elf` auto-synced. `tools/checks/mailboxreset-check.mjs` added (brightness-heuristic based, following the `tourinput-check.mjs` pattern) but proved too fragile against the wallpaper's brightness variance to be a reliable automated pass/fail signal in the time available; kept in the repo as a documented starting point, not claimed as a passing CI-quality test. Manual Playwright screenshot verification (3 separate live runs against the deployed site, ~3 minutes of real wall-clock each) is the real evidence behind every claim above. This pass started from 0.72.2, but `origin/main` had moved to 0.73.3 (real multi-window, satellite wallpaper, and the NE2000 driver, unrelated files, clean rebase) by the time this landed, so the real tag is 0.73.4, not 0.72.3. PATCH bump 0.73.3 -> 0.73.4, a partial bug fix, no new capability.

## v0.73.5: real root cause of the v0.73.4 gap found and fixed, ramfs genuinely resets between idle-tour laps now (Sep 2026)

Picked up exactly where v0.73.4 left off, using its own concrete leads (serial-log byte counts around `ramfs_init`, reading `emulator.v86.cpu.mem8` directly between `reset_memory()` and `restart()`), not re-litigating what was already ruled out.

**Root cause, confirmed with direct instrumentation, not guessed at**: this kernel's browser demo has no BIOS ROM at all (`landing/v86/embed.js`'s `new V86({...})` call passes `multiboot: {url: "v86/kernel.elf"}` and no `bios`/`vga_bios` options). Reading `landing/v86/libv86.js` directly: `S.prototype.init` (the CPU constructor, called once ever, when the `V86` object is first built) loads the multiboot kernel image via a one-shot trick -- `a.multiboot && (b=this.load_multiboot_option_rom(...)) && (this.bios.main ? this.option_roms.push(b) : this.reg32[0]=this.io.port_read32(244))` -- and since `this.bios.main` is falsy here, that resolves to `this.reg32[0]=this.io.port_read32(244)`, a synchronous call that writes the kernel's ELF segments into `mem8` and sets the CPU's entry-point registers, right there in the constructor. `S.prototype.reboot_internal` (what `emulator.restart()` calls), by contrast, only ever calls `S.prototype.load_bios`, and `load_bios`'s entire body is gated on `if(a)` where `a=this.bios.main` -- completely a no-op with no BIOS. v0.73.4's fix (`reset_memory()` then `restart()`) genuinely zeroed guest RAM, exactly as advertised, but nothing ever put the kernel back afterward: v86 has no built-in path to replay a no-BIOS multiboot boot on any later reset, only on construction.

Proved this directly rather than inferring it from behavior, using the exact two leads v0.73.4 logged: a live Playwright probe wrapped `reset_memory`/`restart` and sampled `emulator.v86.cpu.mem8` at the kernel's 0x100000 load address immediately after each call. Result across 2 real reboot cycles: `sumAfter` (mem8 checksum right after `reset_memory()`) was 0 both times (RAM really is zeroed, confirming that half of v0.73.4's fix), but `sumAfterRestart` (checksum ~500ms after `restart()` returns) was ALSO 0 both times -- the kernel image never comes back. Cross-checked against the kernel's own serial log (`window.__jt.serial`, the same hook `mobiletest.mjs` already reads): `"=== kmain boot start ==="` printed exactly ONCE across the entire run, spanning two full `reset_memory()`+`restart()` cycles. `kmain`, and therefore `ramfs_init` (`drivers/ramfs.c`, confirmed by reading it: it unconditionally zeroes every `files[i].used` flag on every call, no "already initialized" guard of any kind -- the v0.73.4 hypothesis that a kernel-side skip-guard might be the culprit is refuted, ramfs_init itself was never the problem), genuinely never ran a second time. Everything downstream (the tour visibly cycling apps again, the boot logo reappearing) was the emulator continuing to render a frozen, dead CPU state, not a real second boot.

**Fix**: `landing/v86/embed.js` now fetches `v86/kernel.elf` once itself at page load (`kernelElfBuffer`/`kernelElfFetch`, cached, same file the `V86` constructor already loads independently) and, in the idle tour's reboot sequence, calls `emulator.v86.cpu.load_multiboot(kernelElfBuffer)` immediately after `reset_memory()` + `restart()`. `S.prototype.load_multiboot` (read directly in `libv86.js`) is a public method on the CPU object and is literally the same loader the constructor's one-shot trick calls internally -- reusing it redoes exactly what the very first boot did, the real fix at the actual point of the gap (v86 itself has no repeatable no-BIOS-multiboot-reboot path), not a kernel-side workaround for something the emulator never implemented.

**Verified with real evidence, both before and after**:
- Same mem8/serial-log probe re-run after the fix: `kmain boot count` went from 1 to 2 (t=79s, matching one real 56s tour lap plus the reboot wait), confirming `kmain` -- and with it, `ramfs_init`'s unconditional file-table reset -- genuinely runs a second time.
- A second, independent live run against the real unmodified idle tour (no monkey-patching, just polling `window.__jt.serial`) caught the real reboot happening mid-tour on its own schedule: the serial log's tail flipped from stale end-of-boot content to a fresh `"...r ready\r\nfat_mount: no filesystem found\r\nvfs: fat + ramfs backends registered..."` sequence at t=80s, i.e. a real second cold-boot log, not simulated.
- `tools/checks/mailboxreset-check.mjs` (the brightness-heuristic check v0.73.4 already flagged as too fragile against this wallpaper) was re-run against the fixed build and confirmed that same fragility again here (got stuck mid-lap on a stale bright/dark reading, never cleanly resolved both Mail opens); a second, Files-listing-specific pixel-band variant built for this pass hit the identical fragility. Neither is trustworthy evidence either way, consistent with what v0.73.4 already said about this technique, so this pass leans on the direct mem8/serial-log evidence above instead, which is a stronger signal anyway: it checks the real substrate (did the kernel actually reboot and did ramfs's own init code actually run again) rather than an indirect pixel proxy for it. Both throwaway pixel scripts were temporary (`tools/checks/tmp-*.mjs`) and were deleted before committing, not shipped as unreliable tests.
- `check.sh`: PASS. `tools/shell-run.sh heaptest tasktest rxclamptest`: all `ok` (heaptest's alloc/coalesce/split checks, tasktest's ABABAB... preemption pattern, rxclamptest's oversized/runt NIC-length clamp). `tools/checks/check-refs.sh`: clean, 627 references. `node --check landing/v86/embed.js`: clean. `make kernel.elf`: clean rebuild (only `drivers/version.h`'s `JT_VERSION_STR` changed, no kernel-source changes this pass), `landing/v86/kernel.elf` auto-synced by the Makefile rule.

No kernel-side change was needed at all this pass: `drivers/ramfs.c` is untouched, confirming v0.73.4's own final hypothesis was on the right track (something about the JS-side reboot mechanism, not a kernel init guard) even though it hadn't yet pinned down which half. PATCH bump 0.73.4 -> 0.73.5: a real bug fix, closes the loop v0.73.4 left open, no new capability.

## v0.74.0: real multi-window, phase 2 -- click-to-focus and real z-order compositing (Sep 2026)

Direct request, phase 2 of the "Multi-window, honestly scoped" plan v0.73.0 started. Architecture read first, as required: re-read v0.73.0's own entry above and grepped `kernel/kernel.c` for `gui_window_t`/`gui_windows`/`GUI_MULTIWIN_MAX`/`gui_multiwin_open`/`gui_multiwin_focused_click_hit` before touching anything. Confirmed phase 1's exact honest gap: `gui_multiwin_focused_click_hit` tested only `gui_windows[gui_window_count-1]` (the single most-recently-opened window), so a click on a visible BACKGROUND window did nothing at all; and `gui_multiwin_draw_all` already drew strictly back-to-front over the same list `gui_multiwin_open` reordered on open, so the "z-order bug" was really the same root cause as the focus bug -- there was never a way to change the list's order except by opening a window for the first time, so an already-open background window could never be brought forward either for drawing OR for input.

Scoped to exactly the two things asked for, nothing else touched:

1. **Click-to-focus.** Replaced `gui_multiwin_focused_click_hit` (tested only the topmost window) with `gui_multiwin_hit_test(mx, my)`, a real hit test over every open window, topmost-drawn first (`gui_windows[count-1]` down to `[0]`, the reverse of the draw order), returning the first (topmost) window whose rect contains the click -- the same "topmost visible thing wins" rule every real windowing system's hit test uses (X11 stacking order, Win32 Z-order). At the call site (`gui_run`'s input loop), a click is now split two ways: if the hit window is already the topmost/focused one, the existing v0.73.0 "any click inside the focused window's rect closes it" contract still fires unchanged on release (`win_close_here`, `press_window`); if the hit window is a BACKGROUND window, the click is instead swallowed as a pure focus-raise on press (no close, no fall-through to the dock/app-launch paths) via a new `gui_multiwin_focus(idx)` -- the exact shift-to-end-of-list logic `gui_multiwin_open`'s "already open, refocus" branch already had, factored out into one real choke point both call sites now share.

2. **Real z-order compositing.** With `gui_multiwin_focus` as the single place window order changes, `gui_multiwin_draw_all`'s existing back-to-front loop (untouched -- it was already correct once given a correct list) now genuinely reflects real focus: raising a background window via click-to-focus moves it to the end of the same list the draw loop iterates, so the very next repaint draws it last/on top over whatever it overlaps, not just wherever it happened to be drawn most recently. Draw order and input hit-testing were made to share the exact same z-order data on purpose -- `gui_multiwin_hit_test` and `gui_multiwin_draw_all` both walk `gui_windows[0..count-1]`, one forward (back-to-front draw) and one backward (topmost-first hit test) over the identical array, so they cannot disagree about which window is "on top." A `win_focus_changed` flag (folded into the existing `launched` full-repaint trigger, declared just after it so the fold-in compiles) forces the real full desktop repaint the same frame a click changes z-order, so the flip is never a stale frame behind the click that caused it.

**Real pixel evidence.** Headless QMP absolute-pointer input (the `appclose-check.py`/`multiwindow-check.py` pattern) opened Files (dock slot 1) then Weather (dock slot 8) without closing Files, so Weather -- opened second -- is topmost and visibly covers Files where their rects overlap (window 0 at x=70,y=40; window 1 at x=130,y=100, w=820,h=385 each, the same v0.73.0 offsets, genuinely overlapping across most of both windows' content area). A `pmemsave` dump before any focus click (`/tmp/joshuatree_mw_phase2_before_focus.png`) shows Weather's real gradient panel and "Vancouver"/"Weather unavailable" text fully on top of Files in the overlap region, Files' own titlebar only visible where Weather doesn't reach. Clicking Files' own titlebar at (94,56) -- a point only Files' rect covers, since Weather's rect starts at y=100 -- and dumping again (`/tmp/joshuatree_mw_phase2_after_focus.png`) shows Files now genuinely redrawn on top: its real `README.TXT`/`NOTES.TXT` listing fully covers the region Weather's panel occupied a moment before, with only Weather's rounded bottom-right corner still visible where Files' smaller effective reach doesn't cover it. Not a flag flip: the actual drawn pixels changed which window's real content wins the overlapped region, matching which window was actually clicked.

**New discriminating test**, extending `tools/checks/multiwindow-check.py` in place (reused its existing QMP/pmemsave/dock-click helpers rather than writing a parallel script) with a new step 5: opens Files then Weather fresh, samples the overlap pixel at (330,220) to confirm Weather is on top before any focus click, clicks Files' background titlebar at (94,56) and asserts (a) Files did NOT close (the old any-click-closes contract must not fire for a background window), (b) the SAME overlap pixel now reads as Files' content instead of Weather's gradient (real z-order flip, not just an internal flag), (c) a SECOND click at the same spot, now that Files is genuinely topmost, DOES close it (proving click-to-focus only swallows the first click on a background window, closing still works once it's really focused), and (d) Weather was untouched by either click. Proven discriminating the same way v0.73.0's own test was: ran against a `git stash` of this pass's `kernel.c` change only (stashed with a unique tag, applied back with `git stash apply`, dropped after -- never a bare `stash`/`pop`, per this worktree's shared-stash-stack rule), rebuilt, reran. The pre-fix (phase-1) kernel failed exactly as expected: the overlap pixel never changed after the "focus" click (`z-order: focusing Files did not bring it in front of Weather`), the second click didn't close Files (`Files did not close on a second click`), and -- a real, useful side effect of the bug -- input was left dead afterward (`Mail could not be opened and closed again`), confirming this test would catch a real regression, not just an invented assertion. Restored the fix immediately after (`git stash apply` + `git stash drop`), reran clean.

**Full regression suite**, all headless: `./check.sh` PASS; `tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean (636 references); `tools/checks/multiwindow-check.py` PASS (phase 1's original four steps plus the new phase-2 step 5); `tools/checks/appclose-check.py` PASS (every dock app, including Files/Weather's single-window path, still opens/closes exactly as before); `tools/checks/launchpad-click-check.py` PASS. `tools/checks/dockhover-check.py`, flagged in v0.73.0's own entry as failing on pristine `origin/main` before that pass (pre-existing, unrelated dock-hover-lift timing issue) -- re-ran it three times against this pass's build and it PASSed cleanly every time, this pass's diff never touches dock-hover code at all (`gui_dock_hit_test`/`dock_hover_extra` untouched), so either that pre-existing issue is genuinely timing-flaky (consistent with v0.73.0's own "lift timing" description) or was already fixed incidentally somewhere between v0.73.0 and here; either way it's not a regression from this pass and not something this pass's scope covers investigating further.

**Security-minded look (MINOR-bump requirement, 4f).** Same class of change v0.73.0's own security look already covered: `gui_multiwin_hit_test`/`gui_multiwin_focus` only decide which window RECT a click is tested against and which order the same fixed-size `gui_windows[GUI_MULTIWIN_MAX]` array is walked in, no new privilege boundary (every app still runs in kernel mode, no separate address space). `gui_multiwin_focus`'s array shift loop is bounds-checked by its own `idx < 0 || idx >= gui_window_count` guard before touching the array, mirroring `gui_multiwin_close`'s existing guard; `GUI_MULTIWIN_MAX` itself (2) is unchanged, so no new out-of-bounds surface. No new untrusted input parsing, no new network/disk data path touched. No real security-relevant boundary found to newly harden.

**Deliberately deferred, not attempted here**, per the task's own scope: raising the 2-window cap; converting any app beyond Files/Weather off its own blocking `gui_wait_close` loop (Mail/Calendar/Notes/Reminders/Terminal/Chat keep the untouched v0.73.0 single-window path); per-window backing stores/double-buffering (both windows still draw straight into the shared framebuffer through `window_set_viewport`, same as v0.73.0, no new tearing risk introduced by this pass beyond what v0.73.0 already had). Next session's job, per the original roadmap entry's ordering: batch 2 of the app conversion (Reminders/Calendar/Mail's `sleep_ticks`+`get_key_or_click` shape) is the next real multi-window increment; the backing-store/z-order-cost tradeoff the original sketch called out stays unstarted, real future work.

**Screenshot check (4f's visual requirement).** `/tmp/joshuatree_mw_phase2_after_focus.png`: Files' rounded panel, three-dot traffic-light chrome (`0xFF5F57`/`0xFFD64A`/`0xD8D4CE`), and titlebar text all match the same Mojave-palette panel chrome v0.73.0's screenshot already established, genuinely drawn in front of Weather's own matching chrome where they overlap -- no new visual language introduced, real overlap read as real overlap.

MINOR bump: 0.73.5 -> 0.74.0. Both real capabilities this phase was scoped around -- click-to-focus and real z-order compositing driven by the same shared list -- shipped working and are proven with real pixel evidence plus a discriminating regression test, not a partial/rough-edge result.

## v0.74.1: CI-improvement pass (standing requirement 4d) -- 15 orphaned regression tests wired up, a real cross-script bug found and fixed on the way (Sep 2026)

Fell back to the loop's own standing CI-improvement requirement: no direct request pending, and `.github/workflows/check.yml` was overdue for a real gap check the same way `deploy.yml` was before v0's own CI existed. Audited `tools/checks/` against `.github/workflows/*.yml` directly (`grep -oE` for every script path actually referenced) rather than trusting memory: 24 of the 30 scripts in that directory never ran anywhere, in CI or otherwise, only `check.sh`'s own boot marker plus six checks (`png-*`, `textspacing`, `geo`, `wallpaper`, `wallfx`) were wired up.

**A real bug found wiring the rest up, not just a gap.** Every QMP-driven check (12 scripts) ends with `cmd({"execute": "quit"})`, which blocks reading a JSON reply. Running each one standalone (`python3.12`, this sandbox's own PIL/cpython mismatch on plain `python3` forced that -- see below) showed every single one throwing an uncaught `ConnectionResetError` at that exact line: QEMU tears the QMP socket down the instant it processes `quit`, before the reply is ever written back. Every real assertion in the script had already run and passed by that point, but the uncaught exception meant the process still exited non-zero -- these checks would have LOOKED broken the moment anyone actually ran them unattended, a real, previously-latent bug hiding behind the fact that none of them had ever been run outside a human's own terminal (where a stray traceback after real PASS output is easy to eyeball past). Confirmed reproducible on `iconedge-check.py` in isolation, then found the identical `cmd({"execute": "quit"})` shape in all 12: `app-interact-check.py`, `appclose-check.py`, `dockhover-check.py`, `iconedge-check.py`, `iconhalo-check.py`, `launchpad-click-check.py`, `multiwindow-check.py`, `satellite-wallpaper-check.py`, `titlebar-aa-check.py`, `traycorner-check.py`, `wallfx-check.py`, `wallpaper-check.py` (the last two already run in CI's `network` job today, `continue-on-error: true` was silently absorbing the same crash every run). Fixed at all 12 call sites identically: `try: cmd({"execute": "quit"}) / except (ConnectionResetError, BrokenPipeError, OSError): pass`, a reset on cleanup is expected now, not a masked test failure. Re-ran every one of the 9 non-network scripts after the fix: all 9 real PASS, no more tracebacks.

**A second, unrelated real bug found for free**: wiring up `check-refs.sh` (never run in CI, confirmed by the same grep) turned up a genuine stale reference already sitting in `roadmap.md` -- the v81 icon-sharpness entry cited a scratch path under /tmp as if it were a real committed file, when it was always a one-off script never meant to be tracked (this repo's own /tmp-scratch convention). Fixed the wording to describe it as what it was instead of a phantom path (deliberately not backtick-quoting that old path here either, or this very sentence would trip the same check); `check-refs.sh` went from FAIL (1 stale ref) to a clean 646-reference pass.

**What got wired into `.github/workflows/check.yml`'s main `check` job**, each verified passing locally first (`python3.12` for the PIL-based ones, see below): `chat-check.sh`, `ramfs-demo-check.sh`, `vmmouse-check.sh`, `check-calendar.sh`, `jpeg-host-check.sh`, `jpeg-check.sh`, `appclose-check.py`, `dockhover-check.py`, `launchpad-click-check.py`, `multiwindow-check.py`, `iconedge-check.py`, `iconhalo-check.py`, `titlebar-aa-check.py`, `traycorner-check.py`, `tourappcount-check.mjs` (static, no QEMU, just parses `embed.js`'s own `TOUR_APPS`). `satellite-wallpaper-check.py` went into the `network` job instead, alongside `wallpaper-check.py`/`wallfx-check.py`, same real-network shape and same retry-once-then-report pattern.

**A new standalone `check-refs` job**, per this file's own 4d candidate list ("a second job for check-refs.sh"): pure bash, no clang/qemu/PIL install at all, so it gives fast feedback independent of the full build-and-boot job succeeding.

**Deliberately left out, a real gap but a different, bigger task**: `app-interact-check.py` and `fatcyclehang-check.sh` both shell out to `tools/mkdisk.sh`, which uses `hdiutil`/`newfs_msdos` (macOS-only); `editor_qa.py` hardcodes `/opt/homebrew/opt/llvm/bin/llvm-nm`. All three stay local-Mac-only checks until a real Linux/`mtools` rewrite of `mkdisk.sh` exists -- not attempted here, a separate task, not rushed into this pass just to hit a bigger number. `ne2k-check.mjs`, `tourinput-check.mjs`, and `mailboxreset-check.mjs` need a real Playwright browser install plus (for two of them) a real deployed/served page and real internet; also a separate, bigger scope than a mechanical CI-wiring pass.

**Environment note, not a repo bug**: this pass's own sandbox has a broken plain `python3` (`python3-pil` built for cpython-3.12's ABI, `python3` itself resolving to 3.11), confirmed with `dpkg -L python3-pil` showing `_imaging.cpython-312-*.so` next to a `python3 --version` of 3.11.15 -- a mismatched-package-versions quirk specific to this container image, not present on a clean `ubuntu-latest` GitHub runner (checked: `check.yml` apt-installs both from the same repo snapshot, so they always match there). Worked around locally with `python3.12` (present and correct) to verify every PIL-based check before trusting it enough to wire into CI; the workflow file itself still invokes plain `python3`, correct for the real CI environment.

**Full regression suite**, all headless, re-run after every edit: `./check.sh` PASS; `tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean (646 references, was 1 stale before the fix above); all 9 previously-orphaned QMP/pixel checks PASS after the quit-crash fix; `check-calendar.sh`, `chat-check.sh`, `ramfs-demo-check.sh`, `vmmouse-check.sh`, `tourappcount-check.mjs` all PASS unchanged (first real run for the first four, confirming they were never actually broken, just never run).

PATCH bump: 0.74.0 -> 0.74.1. Real bug fixes (the QMP-quit crash, the stale doc reference) and real CI coverage added, no new kernel capability.

## v0.74.2 attempted and reverted: rate-of-change line in progress.svg hits the same wall the doc-coverage line did (Sep 2026)

Session-task-queue item 7 ("progress.svg rate-of-change series ... note the doc-coverage line was deliberately cut, don't reintroduce it as a trend line") picked up and shipped structurally correct: `progress.sh` gained a second polyline (lines-added-per-day, centered finite difference, 95th-percentile clamp against outliers, right-axis 0-100% scale so it doesn't fight the left axis' cumulative total), both lines present with distinct colors/legend/axis labels, `check.sh` and `check-refs.sh` both stayed clean.

Reverted anyway, on an actual rendered look at it, not just the structural check: rendered `progress.svg` to PNG (`rsvg-convert`, with the sheet's `var(--...)` custom properties substituted for their real hex values first, since `rsvg-convert` doesn't resolve CSS variables and silently renders an all-black image otherwise -- a real rendering-tool gap worth remembering for the *next* person who tries to eyeball one of these charts this way). The result reproduces the exact failure this file's own v72 postmortem already named for the doc-coverage line: "the metric itself is fundamentally lumpy (jumps in one pass, not a smooth trend) and just reads as a noisy, ugly zigzag." Lines-added-per-day is exactly that shape too -- most commits are small, a few are huge, so the line sits flat near zero and then spikes to the full 100% ceiling (clipped there by the percentile clamp, on two separate days in this repo's own real history) rather than reading as a trend. Different metric, same visual failure mode as the thing already cut three times before landing on the current single-line-plus-caption-stat design; the "don't reintroduce doc-coverage as a trend line" warning in the queue item was correctly followed to the letter and still weren't enough, because the real problem was never which metric, it's that ANY second, more-volatile-than-cumulative series fights this specific chart's one job (show smooth, real growth over time).

Reverted with `git revert` (clean single-commit revert, `59e7700`, nothing else had built on top of it) rather than a bare rollback, so the attempt and its real reasoning stay in history instead of disappearing. VERSION stays at 0.74.1, nothing shipped. Recorded here so a future pass doesn't reopen this exact idea without reading why it didn't survive a real look at the output; if this chart ever gets a second series, it needs a metric that's actually smooth by construction (a rolling average over a real multi-day window, not a raw day-over-day delta), not another raw derivative of the same lumpy commit-by-commit reality.

## v0.75.0: real multi-window, phase 3 -- batch 2 of the app conversion (Reminders/Calendar/Mail) (Sep 2026)

Direct request, the exact next step both v0.73.0 and v0.74.0's own entries named: "batch 2 of the app conversion (Reminders/Calendar/Mail's `sleep_ticks`+`get_key_or_click` list shape) is the next real multi-window increment." Architecture read first, as required: re-read `kernel/kernel.c`'s `gui_multiwin_*` system (`gui_windows`/`gui_multiwin_open`/`gui_multiwin_draw_one`/`gui_multiwin_hit_test`/`gui_multiwin_focus`) and the Files/Weather content-only-draw split (`gui_draw_files_content`/`gui_draw_weather_content`, called fresh every repaint with no blocking call inside) before touching anything. Then read `kernel/reminders.h`, `kernel/calendar.h`, `kernel/mail.h` in full: all three share the `sleep_ticks`+`mouse_click_edge_sync`+`get_key_or_click` polling-loop shape, but unlike Files/Weather's static viewers, each has real interaction state a repaint must not lose -- Reminders' selection + add-mode + typed buffer, Calendar's month/year + day cursor + day-view edit buffer, Mail's selection + read/compose mode + three-stage compose buffer.

**What shipped.** All three converted to the `gui_multiwin_open` model, real content-only draw functions (`gui_draw_reminders_content`/`gui_draw_calendar_content`/`gui_draw_mail_content`) plus real non-blocking `on_key` handlers (`gui_reminders_on_key`/`gui_calendar_on_key`/`gui_mail_on_key`) that mutate a **second, separate set of state statics** per app (`reminders_mw_*`/`cal_mw_*`/`mail_mw_*`) rather than touching the existing single-window functions at all. This was a deliberate scope call, not laziness: `gui_launch_reminders`/`gui_launch_calendar`/`gui_launch_mail` (the Apps-folder/test-harness single-window path, still used by every existing test that opens these apps outside the dock's multi-window path) are completely untouched, byte-for-byte, so there is zero regression risk to single-window behavior, including small contract details the naive shared-state approach would have broken (e.g. `reminders_add_new`'s own click-cancels-add-mode behavior, which only makes sense in a model where a click is otherwise unclaimed -- in multi-window, a click on the focused window always closes it, the same contract Files/Weather already established, so add/compose/day-view modes are cancelled with esc only there, not a click; documented inline at each `on_key`). The three new state machines reuse every existing data function (`reminders_load/save`, `cal_events_load/set/find`, `mail_load/save/delete_at/mail_str_copy`) rather than re-deriving persistence, so REMINDERS.TXT/EVENTS.TXT/MAIL.TXT's on-disk format is completely unchanged.

**Real, distinct interaction per app**, not a generic list-only conversion: Reminders' `a` enters add mode inline (typed buffer drawn live, enter commits + saves, esc cancels back to the list); Calendar's left/right/`a`/`d` step months, `[`/`]` move the day cursor, `t` jumps to today, enter opens a real inline day-view editor (esc cancels, enter saves the event); Mail's `c` starts a real three-stage compose (from -> subject -> body, enter advancing each stage, esc cancelling back to the list at any stage) and enter on a selected message opens it full-screen with `render_wrapped_text`, marking it read and saving through immediately, same as the single-window path always did.

**Input routing.** `gui_multiwin_interactive(icon)` (icon 1/2/4) gates a new per-frame non-blocking key path in `gui_run`'s own loop: `gui_multiwin_key_nonblock()` mirrors `get_key_or_click`'s exact `SC[]`/extended-0xE0 decode but never `hlt`-waits, popping at most one scancode (or an extended pair) per frame. The one scancode-per-frame budget matches the existing `kbd_pop()` call this loop already made for the global esc-quits-the-GUI check, so the two branches (route to the focused interactive app vs. the old global check) can never double-consume the same scancode -- when no interactive window is focused, behavior is completely unchanged (the pre-existing esc-quits-GUI path fires exactly as before). Only the topmost/focused window's app gets the keystroke, the same "topmost owns input" rule click-to-focus (v0.74.0) already established for clicks; a background Reminders/Calendar/Mail window sitting unfocused behind another open window receives no keystrokes at all, matching the "only the focused window is interactive" contract v0.73.0 set for clicks.

**Real bug found and fixed before this could ship, not assumed away**: the first working version routed every keystroke into the existing `launched=1` full-repaint branch (the same one window-open/close/focus already forces), which redraws the ENTIRE desktop -- a full wallpaper photo blit, menubar, and all ten supersampled dock icons, the exact expensive path `cursor_only`/`dock_only`'s own comments say this codebase deliberately avoids on anything less than a real state change. Typing a real reminder/event/compose field fires this on every single keystroke. Verified live and headless (`tools/checks/app-interact-check.py`, run repeatedly): this measurably fell behind the keyboard IRQ ring's fill rate during a fast multi-character type burst, intermittently dropping/misordering input and producing real, reproducible flakiness (window failed to close, dock click silently swallowed, one run even caught a visibly torn mid-repaint frame via `pmemsave`, a real symptom of the CPU still being mid-redraw when the snapshot landed). Root-caused by disabling the fix and reproducing the exact failure class three separate times (`app-interact-check.py` failing a different app each run, never cleanly), then fixing it the right way: a new, cheap, scoped repaint tier (the same "cheapest repaint that's correct" discipline `cursor_only`/`dock_only` already use below it) that redraws only the focused window's own rect (`gui_multiwin_draw_one`, cursor patched around it exactly like the existing tiers) when a keystroke changes only that window's content; the full desktop repaint is still paid, correctly, the moment a window actually opens/closes/changes focus, unchanged from v0.73.0/v0.74.0. Re-ran `app-interact-check.py` clean five times in a row after the fix, zero flakes, confirming the root cause (not a coincidence/timing fluke in one run).

**Real pixel + disk evidence.** `/tmp/joshuatree_batch2_v075.png`: Files (window 0, opened first) and Reminders (window 1, opened second, genuinely on top by real z-order) both open simultaneously, Reminders' own real add-mode UI mid-type ("ship v0.75.0" in the live text field), both windows' chrome matching the established Mojave-palette panel/traffic-light look, no new visual language introduced.

**New discriminating test**, extending `tools/checks/multiwindow-check.py` in place (reused its existing QMP/pmemsave/dock-click/open_slot helpers, added the same `send-key` helpers `app-interact-check.py` already established, rather than writing a parallel script) with a new step 6: opens Files then Reminders (a real newly-converted app) without closing Files, types a real reminder into Reminders' own add-mode, samples Files' title-strip pixel before/during/after to prove no cross-window bleed, closes Reminders via its own X and asserts Files survives untouched, then (when `/tmp/jt-qa-test.img` is present, the same real FAT16 test image `app-interact-check.py` uses) mounts the disk on the host afterward and greps REMINDER.TXT for the real marker text, proving the save reached the real disk while a second window was genuinely open alongside it, not just that the UI didn't crash. Proven discriminating the standard way: stashed this pass's `kernel/*.h`+`kernel/kernel.c` changes only (unique-tagged `git stash push -u`, applied back with `git stash apply`, dropped after -- never a bare `stash`/`pop`, per this worktree's shared-stash-stack rule), rebuilt against the pristine v0.74.0 kernel, reran. It failed exactly as expected -- `"batch2: opening Reminders alongside Files did not leave both windows open"` (the pre-batch-2 kernel doesn't recognize icon 4 as multiwin-supported, so the second dock click falls through to the old blocking single-window path and takes over the whole screen) -- confirming this step would catch the exact regression it exists to prevent. Restored the fix immediately after, reran clean.

**Full regression suite**, all headless: `./check.sh` PASS; `tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean (718 references, after the rebase onto v0.74.1's own CI-wiring pass); `tools/checks/multiwindow-check.py` PASS (phase 1/2's original steps plus the new batch-2 step 6, disk-verified); `tools/checks/appclose-check.py` PASS (all ten dock slots, including the three newly-converted apps, still open/close exactly as before via the pointer alone); `tools/checks/launchpad-click-check.py` PASS; `tools/checks/dockhover-check.py` PASS. `tools/checks/app-interact-check.py` -- the test that already proves Reminders/Calendar/Mail's real save-to-disk behavior -- re-run clean five times in a row post-fix (Notes/Reminders/Mail/Calendar/Contacts/Calculator/Stocks all real-interact and persist correctly, desktop stays responsive after the full sweep); its one remaining failure (`CONTACTS.TXT` missing the marker after a delete-then-check sequence) reproduces identically on the untouched v0.74.0 baseline (confirmed by stashing this pass's kernel changes and rerunning against pristine v0.74.0), a real, pre-existing, unrelated Contacts VFS-save gap already logged in v0.73.0's own entry, not a regression from this pass and not in this pass's scope to fix.

**Security-minded look (MINOR-bump requirement, 4f).** Same class of change v0.73.0/v0.74.0's own security looks already covered, extended to real per-keystroke input: `gui_multiwin_key_nonblock`/`gui_multiwin_interactive` only decide whether a scancode routes to the focused window's own `on_key` handler or the pre-existing global esc-check, no new privilege boundary (every app, old and new path alike, still runs in kernel mode with no separate address space). Every `on_key` handler writes into fixed-size static buffers (`reminders_mw_buf[REMINDERS_TEXT_MAX]`, `cal_mw_buf[CAL_EVENT_TEXT_MAX]`, `mail_mw_from/subject/body[...]`) with the same length-bounded write pattern (`n < MAX - 1` before every store) the existing single-window text-entry loops already use, reviewed clean; no new unbounded copy introduced. `GUI_MULTIWIN_MAX` (2) and the array-bounds guards on `gui_multiwin_open`/`gui_multiwin_close`/`gui_multiwin_focus` are unchanged. No new untrusted network/disk input parsing (REMINDERS.TXT/EVENTS.TXT/MAIL.TXT are read through the exact same, already-reviewed `vfs_read_file`/line-parsing functions the single-window path has used since v52/v54/v58). No real new security-relevant boundary found to harden.

**Deliberately deferred, not attempted here**, matching the original roadmap entry's own remaining order: raising the 2-window cap; per-window backing stores/double-buffering (every window, old and new, still draws straight into the shared framebuffer through `window_set_viewport`); Notes/Terminal/Keyrate/Chat, the four apps the original entry calls out as having "a real bespoke loop" and converting last, one at a time (a different, harder shape than the `sleep_ticks`+`get_key_or_click` list pattern this batch covers). Mixing an old-path app open concurrently with a new-path window stays untested/undefined, same as v0.73.0 left it.

MINOR bump: 0.74.1 -> 0.75.0 (this pass started against origin/main's own v0.74.0, but origin/main had moved to v0.74.1's CI-wiring pass plus a reverted v0.74.2 attempt, unrelated files, by the time this landed; rebased clean). Three real, working multi-window-capable apps shipped (5 of 18 dock apps now multi-window capable: Files, Weather, Mail, Calendar, Reminders), each independently proven to open/interact/persist/close correctly alone AND alongside another open window, with real pixel and real disk evidence, not a partial/rough-edge result.

## v0.76.0: Settings clicks act on the row actually clicked; the idle tour finally shows the Satellite wallpaper (Sep 2026)

Two sessions picked "0.75.0" independently the same day (this entry started against origin/main's own v0.74.1, unaware the multi-window batch-2 pass above had already claimed 0.75.0 by the time this landed); renumbered to 0.76.0 on rebase, no content changed.

Direct user report: the landing page keeps showing the same old wallpaper, never the Satellite imagery this kernel has supported since v0.73.1. Traced, not guessed. `wall_theme` defaults to `WALL_WARM` (a tinted OpenTopoMap tile, not the old baked photo -- that only shows if the fetch never lands), and Satellite is real but strictly opt-in, reachable only through Settings' own Wallpaper row. `landing/v86/embed.js`'s idle tour (`TOUR_APPS`, `tourLoop`) never once referenced wallpaper, theme, or Settings (confirmed by grep, zero hits) -- a passive visitor watching the auto-playing demo was never going to see it, full stop, regardless of whether the underlying fetch works.

The real blocker to just having the tour click its way there: `gui_launch_settings`'s click handler (`kernel/kernel.c`) acted on whichever row a PRIOR arrow-key press had left `sel` on, which starts at row 0 (Wind) and a bare click never moved. A mouse/touch-only visitor -- this tour included, it only ever sends clicks and a handful of scripted key sequences, never arrow keys -- could therefore only ever toggle Wind, never reach Wallpaper, Dock size, or either LLM setting by clicking alone. Real, pre-existing usability gap, not something specific to the browser demo (any touchscreen user hits the exact same wall).

Fixed at the root: extracted the row hit-test into `settings_row_at(cx, cy, ww)`, a pure function with no GUI/mouse/boot dependency (the exact same shape `rtl8139_clamp_len`'s own extraction used in v0.72.1, "so it's unit-testable without a NIC" -- here, without a mouse). `gui_launch_settings`'s click branch now calls it and sets `sel` to whatever row the click actually landed in before acting, scoped to `k == KEY_CLICK` only so a real 'a'/'d' keypress still acts on whatever `sel` already is, untouched.

New `settingsclicktest` shell command (mirrors `texttest`/`chattest`/`jpegtest`'s own serial-mirroring convention, since a shell command's plain `puts()` isn't mirrored to serial) exercises `settings_row_at` against real row centers, real gaps between rows, and both x-out-of-bounds edges. Proven discriminating, not just asserted: temporarily stubbed `settings_row_at` to always return -1 (simulating the exact pre-fix behavior), rebuilt, reran -- `settingsclick FAIL`, exactly as expected; restored immediately after, reran clean (`settingsclick PASS`). New `tools/checks/settingsclick-check.sh` wraps this the same way `tools/checks/chat-check.sh` wraps `chattest`, wired into `.github/workflows/check.yml`.

`embed.js`'s tour gained `demoSatelliteWallpaper`, called once per lap right before the loop-boundary reboot (has to run every lap, not just once: `reset_memory()` wipes ramfs, `SETTINGS.TXT` included, back to the `WALL_WARM` default): clicks the Apple-menu logo, clicks "Settings", clicks the Wallpaper row three times (Warm -> Cool -> Raw -> Satellite, a tap always steps forward per the existing dock-size convention), waits for a real fetch window, then closes. Real gotcha caught before shipping, not after: Settings has no close-X chrome, only Escape closes it, and Escape's ASCII code (27) isn't in `libv86.js`'s own `simulate_char` lookup tables (read directly: neither table `B` nor `C` has key `27`), so this file's usual `keys`-type script step would have silently no-op'd, exactly the class of "looks scripted, does nothing" bug this file's own v71 header comment already describes for a different key path. Used `keyboard_send_keys([27], ...)` instead, which drives `simulate_press`'s own keyCode-indexed scancode table directly (`F[27] === 1`, confirmed by reading that table too -- the real PS/2 Escape scancode), the same reliable path this file's existing Enter/text sends already use one layer down.

Also added `serial_puts("walltheme=N")` at `wall_switch_theme`'s one real choke point (every theme setter already goes through it), matching `weather_fetch`'s existing `geo=`/`wx=` convention, so a real browser run's `window.__jt.serial` (the same hook `tools/checks/ne2k-check.mjs` already reads) can confirm which theme actually got switched to without a physical screen.

**Verified**: `./check.sh` PASS; `tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean (700 refs); `tools/checks/settingsclick-check.sh` PASS, proven discriminating as described above; `tools/checks/appclose-check.py` PASS (every dock app still opens/closes normally, Settings is reached through the Apple menu, not the dock, so this path was never at risk but re-checked anyway); `node --check landing/v86/embed.js` clean; a real headless Playwright run against the local build (Chromium at `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium`, matching this environment's own pinned-browser setup) confirmed the page loads, boots, and reaches `window.__jt.ready` with the tour running end to end with no script errors.

**Honest, not fully closed**: the Playwright run confirmed the tour *runs* the new click sequence without erroring, but did not, in this pass, capture a `walltheme=4` line on `window.__jt.serial` within the run's own time budget (a full tour lap plus the demo step is 90-100s of real wall-clock time before the Wallpaper clicks even begin) -- inconclusive rather than a failed proof, not claimed as a full end-to-end confirmation that Satellite tiles genuinely paint on screen in the live deployed page. `settings_row_at`'s own correctness is proven independently (the discriminating unit test above, which needs no browser and no network at all), which is the part this pass actually set out to fix; whether Google's tile bytes reliably arrive over v86's NE2000 path in a real deployed session is the same open question v0.73.1/v0.73.2 already logged honestly (a suspected RX-burst frame-drop under native RTL8139; untested for NE2000 specifically), not resolved or claimed resolved here.

**Security-minded look (MINOR-bump requirement, 4f)**: `settings_row_at` reads `app_cursor_x`/`app_cursor_y`, both already-existing, already-bounds-clamped globals (see `gui_app_mouse_tick`) that every other click-hit-testing app in this file already trusts the same way; no new untrusted input parsing, no new privilege boundary. `wall_switch_theme`'s new `serial_puts` writes a single ASCII digit derived from `wall_theme`, which is already range-checked to 0..4 at every call site (`settings_load`'s own clamp, the Settings cycle's `% 5`) before it ever reaches this function; no new overflow surface.

**Screenshot check (4f)**: not possible in this pass's own environment (a remote cloud container, no attached physical display -- see CLAUDE.md's own note on when real `screencapture` verification is and isn't available). Substituted the framebuffer/serial evidence described above; a real screenshot of Settings' Wallpaper row and the resulting desktop is still owed next time this runs somewhere with a real display attached.

MINOR bump: 0.75.0 -> 0.76.0. Real capability shipped (Settings is now click-usable by row for the first time, not just row 0; the idle tour genuinely demonstrates Satellite) and unit-proven at the root cause, with the deeper live-network question left exactly as honestly open as it already was.

## v0.76.3: real fix, heap/task growth breaks after the GUI runs and exits (Sep 2026)

Session-task-queue item 10, root-caused for real. Reproduced first via `tools/shell-run.sh heaptest tasktest preempttest ps` (esc out of `gui_run()`, then run the sequence from the shell): before this fix, `preempttest`/`killtest` reported "no free task slots" while `ps` showed 0 tasks used, and heap allocations past `0x400000` failed -- exactly the symptom roadmap.md's own item 10 already described, confirmed live rather than assumed.

**Real root cause**, confirmed with direct instrumentation before touching any code: `paging_map_region()` hands out one of `MAX_EXTRA_TABLES` (16) page-table slots every time something maps a region larger than the base identity map -- the GUI's own framebuffer (`window_open`/`window_open_scaled`) is exactly this kind of caller. `window_close()` already unmapped the framebuffer's page-directory entries, but nothing ever freed the underlying table SLOT back to the pool; `extra_tables_used` only ever counted up. Booting, opening the GUI, closing it, and reopening it a few times (each lap through `gui_run()`/`window_close()`) burns one slot per open and never gives any back, so after enough laps `paging_map_region()` runs out of slots for anything, heap growth included, and `kmalloc` starts failing past the base-mapped region -- matching the exact "no free task slots" / heap-growth-fails signature, since `task_create`'s own stack allocation is just another `kmalloc` call downstream of the same starved pool.

**Fix, three coordinated pieces**, each verified to compose correctly with the others: (1) `paging_unmap_region()` (new, `paging.c`/`.h`), the real missing reverse operation for `paging_map_region()` -- clears the page-directory entries for a region and marks its extra-table slot free again, guarded to only ever touch PDEs that are tracked as using an extra table (`pde_to_extra_table[]`), never the permanent base map. (2) `paging_map_region()` itself now scans for the first FREE slot instead of always appending past `extra_tables_used`, so a freed slot in the middle of the pool is actually reusable, not just the most-recently-appended one. (3) `window_close()` now calls `paging_unmap_region()` on the framebuffer it just unmapped from the directory, and `gui_run()`'s own exit path frees the GUI's large heap scratch buffers (`wall_caches_drop()`'s existing cache-invalidation call, plus `dock_band_cache`/`dock_band_frame`) so they don't sit on the heap indefinitely between GUI sessions. Re-entrancy checked, not assumed: `dock_band_cache`/`dock_band_frame` are already allocated lazily (`if (!dock_band_cache || ...)` at their one real call site), so freeing and nulling them at exit is safe for a second `gui_run()` call (there is a second real call site, a shell `gui` command) to reallocate fresh; `wall_caches_drop()` is already called repeatedly elsewhere in normal operation (every theme switch), so calling it again at exit is within its existing, already-proven contract, not a new usage pattern.

**A real quality problem caught and fixed on the way, not shipped as-is**: the pass that found this root cause left permanent debug instrumentation directly in `kmain()` -- inline `HEAPTEST1`/`TASKTEST1`/`HEAPTEST2`/`TASKTEST2` blocks that unconditionally created and killed a real task, twice, on every single boot, production and browser-demo alike, never removed after serving its diagnostic purpose. Stripped entirely before this shipped; the permanent regression coverage lives in the existing `heaptest`/`tasktest`/`preempttest`/`ps` shell commands instead, run through `tools/shell-run.sh`, not baked into the boot path. Also found and fixed a second real, separate bug while re-verifying: `tools/shell-run.sh`'s own VGA-text-capture regex was the exact same `{8}`-hex-digit (macOS-only) pattern already found and fixed in `tools/checks/fatcyclehang-check.sh` this same session (v0.76.2) -- silently returned empty VGA text on this Linux container's longer QEMU monitor address format, which would have made this fix's own verification look like a no-op. Fixed the same way (`{8}` -> `+`).

**Verified for real**, not just "check.sh passes": `tools/shell-run.sh heaptest tasktest preempttest ps` after a real `gui_run()` session now shows `coalesced adjacent free blocks: ok`, `split leftover reused: ok` (both real `heaptest` discriminators, not just "didn't crash"), `tasktest`'s real `ABABABABAB...` interleaving (not the broken "done" with no interleaving), `preempttest`'s `preempted without yield: ok` (not "no free task slots"), and `ps` correctly reporting free task slots -- the exact working signature, matching the pre-GUI baseline this bug previously only held before the GUI ever ran. `./check.sh` PASS, `./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean.

**Security-minded look**: `paging_unmap_region`'s guard (`pde >= BASE_MAP_TABLES` and `pde_to_extra_table[...] >= 0`) prevents it from ever touching the permanently-mapped base identity map or the kernel's own higher-half PDEs regardless of what `phys_addr`/`length` a future caller passes -- reviewed as the one real new way to zero out a page-directory entry outside boot-time setup, confirmed it can't be pointed at kernel memory. No new untrusted input reaches either new function; both are only ever called with this kernel's own internally-computed addresses (the framebuffer's own mapped region).

PATCH bump: 0.76.2 -> 0.76.3. A real fix restoring already-intended behavior (heap/task growth was always supposed to survive a GUI session), no new capability.

## v0.76.4: real fix, GitHub Actions had been red on every push since v0.74.1's own CI-wiring pass (Sep 2026)

Direct report: CI kept failing, repeatedly "fixed" without actually going green. Checked the real GitHub Actions history for the first time this session instead of trusting local `./check.sh` runs alone, and found the actual pattern: the `check` job (the real kernel build/boot/regression suite) passed on every single run. The separate, standalone `check-refs` job -- added this same session, deliberately with no build step, per CLAUDE.md 4d's own candidate list ("a second job for check-refs.sh") -- had failed on every push since the exact commit that introduced it.

**Real root cause**: `roadmap.md` legitimately references `` `drivers/version.h` ``, a real file, but one `.gitignore` already lists (Makefile-generated at build time, never committed). `tools/checks/check-refs.sh` only ever checked `os.path.exists()`, so on a truly fresh GitHub Actions checkout -- no build has run yet, by design, that's the whole point of a build-free standalone job -- the file genuinely isn't there and got flagged `STALE`. This session's own earlier local runs of `check-refs.sh` never caught it because this container's working directory already had a stale `drivers/version.h` sitting around from an earlier `make` in the same session, masking the exact condition CI hits on every fresh checkout. Confirmed directly, not guessed: `rm drivers/version.h && ./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` reproduced the exact `STALE ... drivers/version.h` failure locally for the first time; the real GitHub Actions job log (fetched via the API, not assumed) shows the identical line.

**Fix**: `check-refs.sh` now also accepts a referenced path if `git check-ignore -q <path>` succeeds -- a path `.gitignore` already owns is, by definition, a real local/generated file that may not exist in a fresh checkout, not documentation drift. Proven still discriminating, the standard bar: a genuinely fake, non-gitignored made-up filename appended to this doc as a real backtick-quoted reference still fails the check (deliberately not spelled out literally here in backticks, or this very sentence would trip the same check); removing it passes clean again; removing `drivers/version.h` and rerunning now also passes clean, matching what CI actually sees.

**Verified**: local repro of the exact CI failure condition, then the fix resolving it; a genuinely-stale reference still caught (not weakened into always passing); `./check.sh` and a full `check-refs.sh` pass with `drivers/version.h` both present and absent.

PATCH bump: 0.76.3 -> 0.76.4. A real CI-tooling fix, no kernel capability change. Standing lesson recorded here, not just in this entry: verify against the actual GitHub Actions run result after a push that touches CI, not only a local `./check.sh`/`check-refs.sh` pass -- this session pushed five commits in a row believing CI was green because local checks were, while the real, separate `check-refs` job silently failed every single time.

## v0.76.6: shell regression test suite wired into CI (Sep 2026)

**Standing CI-improvement requirement (CLAUDE.md 4d)** candidate, directly addressed: `heaptest`, `tasktest`, `preempttest`, `killtest`, `ring3test`, and `ps` are six built-in shell commands with real, discriminating regression checks embedded in their own implementations (`kernel.c`), proved in place when this session's earlier major heap-stability pass (v0.76.3) broke and fixed them one by one. All six already live in the kernel; all six print real PASS/FAIL signatures to VGA text; but until today, every one only ever ran when a human typed it interactively, never in CI, never automatically gating a push.

**Real gap closed**: new script `tools/checks/shellregress-check.sh`, modeled on the existing `tools/checks/chat-check.sh` and `settingsclick-check.sh` pattern, boots the kernel once and runs all six commands in a sequence proven safe (no collisions, no stale state): `heaptest tasktest preempttest killtest ring3test ps`. Parses the real VGA text output, asserts each command's known-good output signature is present (coalesced, split, ABABAB interleaving, preemption, killed-task stop, ring-3 syscall round-trip, task enumeration), and reports real PASS if all six are clean. Wired into `.github/workflows/check.yml` as a new step in the main `check` job, so every push now runs it live.

**Proved discriminating, the standing bar** (CLAUDE.md 4b): ran the test against the real kernel, got PASS; then changed line 4948 in `kernel.c` from `big == x` to `big != x` (inverting the heap coalescing check's condition), rebuilt, reran, and the test correctly reported `FAIL: heaptest did not pass all three checks`, exactly the failure mode expected when a real allocator invariant breaks; restored the original condition, reran, and got PASS clean again. Not just "test runs," a real, discriminating regression detector.

**Verified**: `./check.sh` still PASS; `./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean; `shellregress-check.sh` PASS headless in CI harness; `heaptest`/`tasktest`/`preempttest`/`killtest`/`ring3test` each run individually via `tools/shell-run.sh` and all report their expected pass signatures (not just "didn't crash").

**Manager fix (Sonnet review pass)**: the agent's version bump collided with another concurrent push that had already claimed 0.76.5 (`editor_qa.py` portability fix), so this entry was renumbered 0.76.6 on rebase. Also found and fixed a real bug in the agent's own script: the `ps` assertion was written as `if grep -q "free\|used" "$VGA" | head -1; then`, which pipes `grep -q`'s (suppressed) stdout into `head -1` and then checks *`head`'s* exit status, not `grep`'s -- `head` succeeds on empty input, so that branch could never actually fail. Fixed to a plain `if grep -q "free\|used" "$VGA"; then`, verified it now genuinely gates on the VGA content.

PATCH bump: 0.76.5 -> 0.76.6. A real CI-tooling addition (a permanent regression suite now wired in), no kernel capability change or modification to existing code beyond this new script and the CI step that runs it.

## v0.76.7: Satellite wallpaper is finally the real default, not just reachable (Sep 2026)

Direct, repeated user report: "Landing page still showing stale/old wallpaper rather than the satellite update." Investigated for real rather than re-patching blind, since this exact complaint had already prompted two earlier passes (v0.73.1's satellite wiring, v0.76.0's Settings-click fix + idle-tour demo) that both landed correctly but evidently didn't resolve what the user is actually seeing.

**Deploy pipeline checked first, and it's genuinely fine.** Pulled the real GitHub Actions job log for the latest "Deploy landing page" run (`npx wrangler deploy`, run 35032715123): `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` is set and working, wrangler genuinely uploaded the changed `/v86/kernel.elf` asset and reported `Deployed joshuatree triggers ... joshuatree.heyitsmejosh.com (custom domain)`. CLAUDE.md's own note that this token "still needs adding" is stale -- it works and has been deploying successfully on every push that touches `landing/**`. Not the bug.

**Real root cause, found by re-reading the wallpaper default and both prior fix entries together**: `wall_theme` (`kernel/kernel.c`) has been statically initialized to `WALL_WARM` since the theme enum existed, and neither v0.73.1 (which made Satellite real) nor v0.76.0 (which made the idle tour click through to it) ever flipped that default. Joshua's own direction, recorded verbatim back in the "Later idea: location-dynamic satellite wallpaper" entry: "the real satellite-town wallpaper should become the default once buildable." It became buildable in v0.73.1 and stayed non-default for four more versions. Compounding it: `settings_load()` returns immediately ("no file yet: compiled-in defaults stand") whenever `SETTINGS.TXT` is missing, and the browser demo's idle tour wipes ramfs (and with it `SETTINGS.TXT`) via `reset_memory()` every single lap -- so even the one browser session that *did* click through to Satellite via v0.76.0's demo script would boot right back to Warm map on the very next lap. A passive visitor watching the demo, or anyone hitting the site fresh, was always going to see the Warm map first, for as long as they watched, regardless of whether the Satellite fetch itself works.

**Fix**: `wall_theme`'s static initializer changed from `WALL_WARM` to `WALL_SAT`. Checked for regression risk before flipping it, not assumed safe: `wall_apply()`'s fallback (`next = (want_map && wall_map) ? wall_map : wallpaper_rgb`) already falls back to the baked-in photo whenever `wall_map` is still null, which is exactly what happens today if a fetch hasn't landed yet or fails -- this is the identical fallback path `WALL_WARM` already relied on, so flipping the default changes which real image a *successful* fetch shows, nothing about failure-mode safety.

**Honest, not newly resolved by this pass**: whether Google's satellite tile bytes reliably arrive over the browser demo's real v86/NE2000 network path is the same open question v0.73.1/v0.73.2/v0.76.0 already logged (a suspected RX-burst frame-drop, never confirmed either way for NE2000 specifically) -- tried to re-check it directly from this session's own dev container and hit a different, unrelated wall: this container's own outbound network is itself behind a host-level allowlist proxy (confirmed via `curl -v http://ip-api.com/json/` returning a real `403`/`host_not_allowed` from the gateway, not from the kernel or the target host), so a local repro of the live fetch path isn't possible from here either. Whatever the real answer, this fix is unconditionally an improvement: the *fallback* image on any failure is unchanged (the baked photo, same as before), and the intended default on any success is now what Joshua actually asked for, instead of a config value nobody without a live comms channel back to this session was ever going to click into on a wiped-every-lap browser demo.

**Discriminating regression test, per 4b**: new `tools/checks/walldefault-check.sh`, network-independent by design so it doesn't inherit the live-fetch question above -- reads the real `wall_theme` global directly out of a freshly-booted kernel's memory via the QEMU monitor (`llvm-nm` for the symbol address, `xp` for the value), before any shell command or Settings click could have touched it, the same "read the symbol, don't trust the source" standard `editor_qa.py`'s `Machine.integer()` established. Proven both directions: temporarily reverted the initializer back to `WALL_WARM`, rebuilt, reran -- `FAIL: wall_theme defaulted to 0x01, expected 0x04 (WALL_SAT)`; restored, reran -- `PASS: wall_theme defaults to WALL_SAT (4) on a fresh boot`. Wired into `.github/workflows/check.yml` as a new step (4d).

**Verified**: `./check.sh` PASS; `./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean (791 refs); `./tools/checks/shellregress-check.sh` PASS (all six shell regression commands, confirming this change didn't disturb task/heap state); `walltest` (the existing wallpaper-theme unit test, which saves/restores `wall_theme` around its own dispatch checks) still reports its full "ok" line unchanged, confirming the default flip doesn't interact with its independent save/restore logic. `landing/v86/kernel.elf` resynced, since this changes what every visitor's first boot shows.

PATCH bump: 0.76.6 -> 0.76.7. A real default-behavior fix (the config surface, fetch logic, and fallback behavior are all unchanged; only which theme a fresh boot starts on), not a new capability.

## v0.76.8: real CI flake root-caused, `tasktest` was racing the kernel's own preemption (Sep 2026)

Direct request: strengthen CI so GitHub status/commits/tests stop failing. Immediately hit a real one: the v0.76.7 push's own "Boot check" run went red on `shellregress-check.sh`'s `tasktest` step, printing `ABABAABABABABABABABB` instead of the expected clean `ABABAB...` -- a genuine flake this exact push's kernel change (the wallpaper default) had nothing to do with, so root-caused it properly rather than just re-running until green.

**Root cause, confirmed by direct code reading then reproduced on demand, not guessed**: `yield()` (`kernel/task.c`) issues a software `int $32`, and the hardware PIT timer's IRQ0 is remapped onto that exact same IDT vector, so both land in the identical `schedule()` call (task.c's own comment already said as much). Critically, `kernel/irq.c`'s `irq_handler` for IRQ0 was already just bumping `tick_count` -- the actual scheduling happens in the shared asm stub for vector 32 regardless of which source (hardware or software) raised it. That means this kernel has been truly, continuously preemptive in the background for every boot with 2+ live tasks, not just during `preempttest`'s deliberate no-yield demo. `tasktest`'s strict "ABABAB..." expectation is only true if zero hardware ticks land during its own tiny execution window -- true by luck on a fast/idle machine, not guaranteed on a slower or shared CI runner, where more real wall-clock time (and therefore more real 100Hz PIT ticks) elapses per instruction executed.

**Reproduced on demand, not left as a theory**: temporarily reconfigured `irq_install`'s `pit_init(100)` call to `pit_init(5000)` (a 50x higher tick rate, cheaply simulating what a slow runner does to the *relative* tick rate without needing to actually throttle a CPU) and reran `tasktest` -- corrupted on 3/3 runs (`ABABAABABABABABABABB`, `BABABABBABABABABAB`, `ABABABABABABABABBABA`), confirming the exact failure shape CI hit. Real PIT rate restored byte-for-byte afterward (`diff` against a saved copy of `kernel/irq.c` confirmed clean).

**Fix**: new `pic_set_mask(irq, masked)` in `kernel/pic.c`/`pic.h` masks a single IRQ line at the 8259 itself. `tasktest` now masks IRQ0 before its `task_create`/`yield` loop and unmasks it right after, so only the tasks' own explicit software `yield()` calls drive scheduling during this specific cooperative-round-robin demo. Masking at the PIC (not `cli`/`sti`) was deliberate: `cli` only clears the *currently running* task's `EFLAGS.IF`, which a task switch immediately overwrites with whatever IF the next task's own suspended frame carries (task A/B's initial fabricated stack frames carry IF=1, so a bare `cli` in the shell's own context would get silently undone the instant control switched to A or B) -- masking at the PIC prevents the hardware line from ever reaching the CPU at all, regardless of which task is current. `preempttest`/`killtest` and every other real caller of the scheduler are untouched; the always-on preemption they depend on is a real, intentional feature, not something this fix disables generally.

**Proven discriminating, both directions, at the same artificially-high tick rate used to reproduce it**: with the mask/unmask fix built at `pit_init(5000)`, `tasktest` came back clean on 5/5 runs (`ABABABABABABABABABAB` every time) -- the same stress that broke it 3/3 times without the fix. Real rate restored, `tasktest` still clean 5/5 at 100Hz, `preempttest`/`killtest`/`ps` all unaffected (`preempted without yield: ok`, `kill: task really stopped: ok`, real free/used slots).

**Also strengthened this pass, matching the same direct request**: `.github/workflows/check.yml`/`deploy.yml` got a real hardening pass beyond this one bug fix. `check.yml`: `concurrency: cancel-in-progress: true` (this session pushes to `main` frequently enough that overlapping runs on the same ref were possible, and only the latest push's status actually matters for a no-side-effect test job); `timeout-minutes` added to all three jobs (`check` 20, `check-refs` 5, `network` 15) so a wedged QEMU process reads as a real timeout failure instead of sitting `in_progress` forever; apt package caching via `actions/cache@v4` (CLAUDE.md 4d's own long-standing candidate, "caching the apt/qemu install step for faster runs", finally done). `deploy.yml`: `concurrency` too, but serialized rather than cancelled (`cancel-in-progress: false`) since a live `wrangler deploy` has a real external side effect mid-flight that a cancel could leave half-applied; `timeout-minutes: 10`.

**Verified**: `./check.sh` PASS; `./tools/checks/check-refs.sh` clean (798 refs); `./tools/checks/shellregress-check.sh` PASS x3 in a row at the real 100Hz rate; `python3 -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(...)"` confirms both workflow files still parse. `landing/v86/kernel.elf` resynced (`pic_set_mask` and the `tasktest` change are real kernel-visible behavior).

PATCH bump: 0.76.7 -> 0.76.8. A real bug fix (a genuine, previously-invisible-locally race between cooperative and preemptive scheduling paths) plus CI-infrastructure hardening, no new user-facing capability.
